# Storyboard: editing it (for the agent)

The storyboard (`/storyboard`, `components/Storyboard.jsx`) is the user's board of the videos'
frames. What the user changes on it lives apart from the captured frames, in
`content/storyboard/<video>.json` (and `trash.json` for the Trash). This page is how to change it
when the user asks.

## The rule: change only what was asked — DO NOT CHANGE HOW IT LOOKS

When the user asks for an edit on the storyboard ("retype frame 3 of Date & time", "make that
text red", "move the bubble up", "add a line called …"):

- Change **exactly** that, with `scripts/storyboard.mjs` (below). Never rewrite the edits file by
  hand, never recapture (`gen:storyboard`), never touch the demos' CSS or components for it, never
  "tidy" other frames, positions, sizes, colors or fonts.
- Look first (`frame`, `texts`), then change, then look again to confirm. Say what changed in the
  user's words (line name + frame number, as the board shows them).
- An open storyboard page shows the change within ~3 seconds (it polls the file's revision) and
  keeps the user's own unsaved edits (it merges); the user can Ctrl+Z it on the page.
- If the user's request is about the look of the whole board or the video itself, that is not a
  storyboard edit: ask.

## The command line

```
node scripts/storyboard.mjs help                  # every command
node scripts/storyboard.mjs lines                 # lines: id, name, number of frames
node scripts/storyboard.mjs frames "date"         # frames of a line, named <line>:<n> as numbered on the board
node scripts/storyboard.mjs frame "date:3"        # its note, its assets (key, name, box), what was changed
node scripts/storyboard.mjs texts "date:3" bubble # the texts of an asset, with their keys
node scripts/storyboard.mjs find "Knowledge Base" # frames whose label, note or texts say it
```

`--video increased-efficiency-v2` for the other video (`improved-quality-v2` is the default,
`trash` the Trash). A frame is `<line>:<n>` (the line by id, name, or the start of its name; `n` as
the board numbers it, from 1 in each line) or its id. An asset is its key (or its name); a part of
one is the key `texts` prints (`report/0.0.1.2`, `report/0.0.1#1` = a piece of text beside others).

Changes (each one saved like the page saves: with the file's revision, a backup kept):

| Command | What it does |
| --- | --- |
| `note <frame> <words>` | the note under a frame (`""` takes it off) |
| `name <frame> <words>` | the name a frame shows in its header instead of its label (`""` takes it off; the sidebar's **Name** does the same). The label stays what the video's director reads, so a name never changes how a video plays |
| `text <frame> <key> <words>` | retype a text, in that frame alone (nothing is linked, since 2026-11-09); `\n` = new line in a chat bubble |
| `move <frame> <asset> <dx> <dy>` | move by stage px (a frame is 1600 × 900); `--to x y` puts its top-left corner there |
| `scale <frame> <asset> <s>` | scale a UI element (it keeps its proportions) |
| `size <frame> <asset> <w> <h>` | the size of a box: picture, spotlight, chat bubble (its words keep their size) |
| `tip <frame> <bubble> <dx> <dy>` | move where a bubble's tail points |
| `style <frame> <asset> k=v …` | what the sidebar sets: `opacity rotate radius fill stroke strokeWidth shadow blur bgBlur color font size weight align lineHeight letterSpacing` (`blur`: layer blur px, `bgBlur`: background blur px; `k=` takes one off; fonts `inter montserrat poppins`) |
| `lock <frame> <asset> w\|h\|wh\|none` | lock its width, height or both: the handles and W / H leave them as they are |
| `bubbles <width> [--lock]` | every chat box of the board that wide, **not locked** (the user, 2026-10-07; `--lock` locks the width; **300**, run again after a recapture) |
| `tail <frame> <chat box> on\|off` · `tails on\|off` | one chat box's tail, or every one of the board (only those that have one) |
| `words <frame> <chat box> show\|hide` | show the words of an empty chat box (a typing frame), or hide them |
| `highlight <frame> <x> <y> <w> <h> [--z n]` | a yellow rectangle there (the demos' `.iq2-mark`); `--z` its place in the stack (under a hand: lower than the hand's) |
| `add-html <frame> <name> <file> <x> <y> <w> <h>` | an asset made of that HTML (a player bar, a badge…), at that place |
| `typing <file.json>` | chat boxes mid-typing in one save: `[{"frame": id, "asset": key, "key": text key, "text": "As Teams\n\n"}]` (shows the words, set to the first words plus empty lines) |
| `moves <file.json>` | many moves in one save: `[{"frame": id, "asset": key, "dx": 0, "dy": 12.5}]` (stage px, added to where the asset is now) |
| `layer <frame> <asset> <back|front|backward|forward>` | the stacking: send an asset to the back / front, or one step down / up |
| `paint <frame> <background|asset> <color|preset|json>` | a frame's background, or an asset's fill: a color, a ready paint id (`PAINT_PRESETS`: night, gold, carbon, rose-gold…) or a gradient as JSON; `none` takes it off |
| `animate <frame> <chat box> <handwrite|off> [letters/s]` | the chat box's words are written by a pen when the slide is played (the blue play button at the slide's top right); `off` takes it away |
| `put-back <frame> <asset>` | the asset as captured (place, size, scale, tail, styles; words stay) |
| `hide <frame> <asset>` · `reset <frame>` | take an asset out · the frame as captured (note stays) |
| `duplicate <frame>` · `blank <frame or line>` | a copy after it · a blank frame after it / at the end of a line |
| `delete <frame> …` | to the Trash, with its line's name |
| `to-line <frame> <line> [n]` | move a frame to another line |
| `line-add <name> [--after <line> | --first]` · `line-rename <line> <name>` · `line-duplicate <line>` · `line-delete <line>` | lines (a deleted line's frames go to the Trash; a line of videos is duplicated as one) |
| `line-view <line> videos|slides` | a line of videos (Animations) as videos, one player each, or as the slides they are made of (the sidebar's **Show**) |
| `export [file]` · `import <file>` · `backups` · `backup` · `restore <backup>` | files (below) |

## Files, revisions, backups

- `content/storyboard/<video>.json` holds the edits (`lib/storyboardEdits.mjs` documents every
  field) and `rev`, bumped on every save. A save made from an older revision is refused (409) and
  the page merges and saves again, so the page and the command line never overwrite each other
  (`lib/storyboardStore.mjs`, `/api/storyboard`).
- Backups: `content/storyboard/backups/<video>/<time>--<reason>.json`. Always before an import or
  a restore, at most one every 10 minutes while saving, the newest 80 kept. The sidebar lists them
  (nothing picked → Backups → Restore) and `/api/storyboard/backups` serves them.
- Export (sidebar or `export`): every board and the Trash in one file
  (`{mark: 'taskmaverick-storyboard-export', version, exportedAt, boards}`, `makeExport` /
  `readExport` in `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`), editable: Import puts it back. Without the file
  (production), the page downloads the storyboard as it was before it imports.
- **A reload keeps your place (the user, 2026-10-13: "when I refresh the website using f5 or ctrl+shift+r or any way, I wanna be where I was before, when I refresh it takes me up"):** the board
  is a canvas, not a scrolled page, so the browser has no position to give back and it used to open on the first row every time. The view is kept in `localStorage`
  (`storyboard:view:<video>`, one per board and the Trash) as the board point in the middle of the window plus the zoom (`keepView` / `restoreView` in `lib/storyboardView.mjs`: the same
  place is in the middle of a window of any size), each time a move settles and on `pagehide` / `visibilitychange` (a reload in the 130 ms before a move has settled keeps where it was
  heading). The board opens on it (`opened` effect in `components/Storyboard.jsx`); it is only ignored when it is not usable or its middle is nowhere near the board (a line deleted:
  the bounds are every frame and label, NOT the rows, which are only the labels' width), then the first row as before. The other long pages scroll the window, and the browser already
  returns to the same spot after F5 and Ctrl+Shift+R (checked: the landing page, both libraries, `/improved-quality-v2`).

## Chat boxes: 300 wide, not locked (the user, 2026-10-06; unlocked 2026-10-07)

Every chat box on the boards is **300 wide (stage px), not locked** (2026-10-07: the lock was removed by default, on both boards; the sidebar's lock still locks one); a new one (Shift+A)
starts that way. A chat box given a width wraps its words to it: as captured they flow as one text
(the demo's own lines were made for its width), words retyped keep their own lines, and a box made
narrower sends its words to the next line (the font never changes). Its height follows its words
(`sizes` [w, 0]) unless one was given. After `npm run gen:storyboard` the new frames' boxes are not
sized yet: `node scripts/storyboard.mjs bubbles 300` (and `--video increased-efficiency-v2`).

## Chat boxes centered vertically (the user, 2026-10-06)

Every chat box on the storyboard sits with its vertical middle on the frame's (y = 450 of 900); only
its height position changed (its x is where the user put it). A box's middle is the middle of its words
(`bubbleBoxOf`), so it was measured in the browser (every frame drawn live, the words' middle in stage
px) and moved by the difference with `moves`: 85 boxes on Improved Quality (all lines but "The tablet and
its Knowledge Base", which the user had just reviewed and keeps as placed) and 36 on Increased
Efficiency. A new line of frames, a recapture or a retyped box (it grows) needs it again.

## Background designs (the user, 2026-10-07: "not changing just colors, I need different styles")

A background can be a DESIGN, not a color: an `add-html` asset 1600 x 900 at 0,0 (inline SVG / CSS gradients only), sent `layer ... back`, over the frame's own background paint. Ten were made for the Scratch line (styles 15-24): dot grid (a dot field masked to thicken round the words), blueprint (40 / 200 px grid + cross marks), aurora (big blurred orbs), diagonal split (SVG polygons), topography (seeded noisy contour rings), signal rings (concentric circles with nodes), hex mesh (a honeycomb fading away from the words), waves (layered paths), pinstripe (a repeating 135-degree gradient + a beam), light rays (blurred polygons fanning from the top). Each has the inset panel (`r2-rectangle` at 28,28, 1170 x 844, radius 40: frosted glass on dark, white card with a shadow on light), a typeface, the text 360 wide and an accent bar 96 x 4 placed 30 px under the measured text. Replace one design: `hide <frame> <asset>`, `add-html` again, `layer ... back`.

## Handwriting: words written by a pen (the user, 2026-10-07: "literally handwritten, not word by word, not line by line")

A chat box can have an animation, `anims.<bubble key> = {type: 'handwrite', cps}` (letters a second: the sidebar's **Speed** slider, 4 to 60, default 22; older ones hold `wps` or `seconds`) on its frame (sidebar: **Handwriting**, `animate` on the command line; copied by a duplicate and by Ctrl+C / V). It lives **in the slide itself**: a frame with one shows a small **blue round play button with a white triangle at its top right** while it is selected (a white square while playing; Esc or the button stops it). Playing writes the words with a pen along the strokes of the letters. `lib/storyboardHandwriting.mjs` (pure, tested) thins the text's pixels to a center line (Zhang-Suen), splits it into lines of text, then into pieces that touch (a letter, or a whole word in a joined hand), and walks them like a hand: line after line, piece after piece left to right, a stem from its top down, the dot of an i and the bar of a t after their letter; `components/storyboardHandwriting.js` draws the words on a canvas in their own font, shows them through a pen-thick mask the pen paints, with a small nib at its tip, then puts the page's own words back (the frame never changes). The words are hidden only while the pen writes. Not in the storyboard video player (yet).

The **Fonts** line (above Scratch in `improved-quality-v2`, `line-add Fonts --first`): the same slide in 14 handwriting faces (Caveat, Kalam, Patrick Hand, Indie Flower, Shadows Into Light, Reenie Beanie, Dancing Script, Permanent Marker, Homemade Apple, Nanum Pen Script, Gloria Hallelujah, Covered By Your Grace, Zeyada, Sacramento), each with its handwriting animation and a note naming the font. Fonts are `next/font/google` instances in `app/layout.jsx` (`preload: false`) and ids in `FONTS` (`lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`) / `FONT_STACK`, `FONT_MATCH` (`components/storyboardDom.js`).

## Font size and line height (the user, 2026-10-07: "I change the font size and it just spreads the lines apart")

A text's line height is automatic (1.3 x its size) until someone sets it; a set one is a fixed px number. Changing the font size in the sidebar now moves a set line height with it (`scaledLineHeight`), and a number field only saves when its value was really changed (clicking into Line height and out again used to save the number it showed, which then stayed put while the size changed). The one frame that had such a frozen value (`links/bubble`, 45.49 at 34.99, identical to automatic) was set back to automatic.

## No empty chat boxes: they show the start of their words (the user, 2026-10-06)

A frame of "Bubble, no words" (the box popped, the words not yet typed) never shows an empty yellow
box: it shows the first half of the first line of the words that will come (3 words: the first 2; 2
words: the first 1), followed by empty lines, so the box keeps the size of all its words and looks like
mid-typing. It is `bubbles.words = true` plus a retyped text (`typing`); the numbers of lines were
measured from the box itself (`[data-sb-mode="empty"]`, its wrapped words at 300 wide). A new frame of
that kind, or a recapture's, needs it again.

## Captured frames never show a click half done (the user, 2026-10-06)

The frame "After the click" is taken when what the click did is finished: `planChapter`
(`lib/storyboardFrames.mjs`) waits for the stop's `settle` (seconds) when it has one, else 0.55 s after
it. Micro-training's Submit folds the quiz and the video into a Done tile, which takes longer than that
(the frame once showed it 13% open, the hand cut off under the card): its stop has
`settle: MICRO.done + .1`. Give any new click whose effect is slow a `settle`.

## No tails on the storyboard (the user, 2026-10-06)

Every chat box on the storyboard has its tail taken off (`tails off`; per box: `frames[id].bubbles`,
`{tail: false}`; the sidebar's Chat box section turns it back on). The frames that only showed a tail
("Tail", "Hand coming out, tail halfway") and the "Tail fading" frames that then looked like the one
before were moved to the Trash (24 on Improved Quality); frames with a note, or made / edited by the
user, were kept. The videos themselves still have their tails (that is the demos' rule in CLAUDE.md).
To dim a tablet's board under an open panel, use its own shade: `style <frame> tablet/0.0.0.0.1
opacity=0.5` (`.mi-demo-shade`, the layer the demos dim with).

## The split design: every frame looks like the model (the user, 2026-10-07)

**2026-10-08 (the user, after the second slide of the Fonts line): the backdrop is TWO layers.** A light **Plate** over the whole slide
(-1.69, -2.25, 1601.69 x 904.35, gradient #656a73 -> #9ea4b0) and over it a darker **Panel** over the right side behind the words
(x 1042.6, 559 x 904, gradient #404245 / #333537 / #444547), so the dark gray can be moved from left to right between slides. Every slide
outside Scratch and Fonts has them (a slide of only words: the panel covers the whole slide). `scripts/storyboard-split.mjs` takes both from
the frame given by `--backdrop` (default `knowledge/bubble#9`, Fonts slide 2) and skips the lines Scratch, Fonts and Animations by default; the old
description below (one plate over the left part) is what it was before.

**The Panel moves; the video plays it (the user, 2026-10-08, first video "The tablet and its Knowledge Base"): "no transition between slides — real animation: cursor moving, the rectangle coming from the right to the left when we want to say something, tablet not changing, handwriting".**
The Panel has three places (`plan.panel` in `lib/storyboardSplit.mjs`): `right` (behind the words, the default of a frame with a Plate),
`full` (a words-only slide: covers everything) and `away` (parked off the right edge: a slide where nothing is said yet; `--panel-away <line>`,
default `knowledge`). It is the same size in every slide, so the video slides it. The player (`lib/storyboardPlayer.mjs`) pairs layers between
two slides by **role** (`roleOf`: every chat box = `words`, the layer named Tablet = `tablet`, Hand = `hand`, others by key): the tablet and the
hand glide instead of fading. Menus (Knowledge Base, Onboarding...) dim the rest of the tablet in the slides: `tablet/0.0.0.0.1` at opacity 0.6,
so the video dims it too.

**One video for each run of lines (the user, 2026-10-09: "after finishing a scene I see the next one: the mission, then the alerts, then the link, ... the video proof last, then a video on the right side of that slide with all of the above"; earlier the same day: "make the other videos for the other storyboards, same pacing, same everything").**
The runs are a table, `VIDEO_RUNS` in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs` (a line that is in no run, like Scratch, Fonts or a line of your own, has no video): Improved Quality has 6 (the Animations reel, a line of videos: see The Animations line below; the Knowledge Base; the mission: instructions, alerts, links, translation,
steps, micro-training, photos and videos, photo proof, video proof; the tablet, the mission and the ratings: emphasis, self-rating, peer ratings; what managers see: Business Proofs, mobile, documented evidence; the web report: web,
remote, performer, timestamp, closing words), Increased Efficiency has 4. A player sits at the right of the last frame of the last line of each run (`videoSpots` in `components/Storyboard.jsx`), plays the frames of all its lines in order (the
bar has a mark where each line starts, the label says which line and frame) and is made ready only when it comes near the screen (`armed`, an IntersectionObserver in `components/StoryboardPlayer.jsx`). All of them are made by the same director, so what
is said below holds for every run; between two scenes the words of the first go out, the next ones are written, outlines go out first and come in after the art. What makes it work for runs that are not the first: the cursor goes straight from a click to the next
one when no new words come between them (one piece of work), else it clicks, fades out and comes in again from the words for the next click (`together`, `clickOut`). **The art** of a slide is any of Tablet, Mission, Team board, Lifted mission, Report, Board, Names, Dates,
Mission cards, Isolated missions, Layer (`isArt`; the main one has the role `tablet`); **a click** is where the demo made its click ripple when the slides have ripples (the ripple
itself is not played: the video has its own ring), else where the cursor rests when the art changes; the demo's chat box with no words yet is not played; a cursor with no click is
not shown; **the last click of a part** has no slide after it, so it is made at the end of its last frame (press, ring, the cursor fades: `endClick`); a title with nothing typed yet
is not played. A part ends on its last frame (a title one stays 2.4 s).

**The video is not the slides one after the other (the user, 2026-10-08): "don't worry about the slides, just make me a good video".** The player's
director (`directorOf` in `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`) turns the slides of a line into the script of the video; the slides are never changed:
- **Slides that are not played:** one that only holds the start of the next one's words (`isTypingStep`: "As Teams", "Teams Are"), one that shows the
  tablet, a title or the words half faded (a layer's `opacity` under 1: a step of a fade), one that looks like the one before. The video never shows
  incomplete or faded text.
- **Words:** a chat box's words are always written by the pen (the chalkboard hand), at the chat box's Speed (sidebar Handwriting, words a second),
  else 22 letters a second (a little faster than 3 words a second; the user, 2026-10-09). Words that stay from one slide to the next are not written again. A **title and the conclusion** fade in small and grow (see below); one that comes after the art waits until the art has gone out (tablet out,
  the Panel wiping across), then it comes in.
- **Highlights and what is drawn over the art** (a Highlight, the timeline of a video, a ripple: `isOverlay`) come in only after the art they point at
  is in place (`LATE`: a moment after the move ends, then a short fade), and go out first; they never glide. The frame stays on long enough to see them.
- **The cursor** (the Hand layer; the user, 2026-10-09) is ONE cursor, on screen from its first click to the end of the scene (it fades out slowly with the
  scene change). It comes in once from the top left of the words, goes to the **exact middle of what it clicks** (`centerHand`; what is clicked is the word the slide makes bold, else what it highlights, else what is under its cursor on the slide; checked: Menu button, Knowledge Base, Onboarding and the video are each 0 to 1 px from the fingertip; the words themselves when the
  clicked button / menu row / card has one text, else the thing's middle; the menu is the button labelled Menu, not the plus; a video's play button is the middle
  of `.iq2-kb-film`), hovers, clicks (it presses, a ring spreads, THEN the screen changes), and goes **straight on to the next thing to click**: menu →
  Knowledge Base → Onboarding → the video's play button. A *click* is where the slide has the cursor when the tablet changes in the next slide
  (`sources` in `directorOf`); the slides in between (the same tablet, only the cursor and a highlight moving) are not played (`dropped`), the frame after a
  click already has the cursor at the next thing. The cursor is never crossfaded with another one (that was the flicker). **It is always on top of the tablet that changes** (a frame is drawn over the one before: the cursor that glides is the NEW frame's, the old one waits out of sight, and the ring spreads over the new tablet; the old way, the cursor under the fading-in tablet, looked like the cursor fading out before the click). Checked by pixels (yellow in the cursor's box at every 0.06 s). Every move takes 0.6 s (`STD_MOVE`; a click move `CLICK_TOTAL` 1.15 s: the cursor stays on what it clicks, going along with the tablet as it glides, presses, the screen changes, and only THEN does the cursor go on; 
  a click move 1.4 times that, the cursor waits for the press), a hover 0.3 s; holds are short (`HOLD`: 0.4 to 1.4 s).
- **Hover:** the text under the cursor is as it is in the software until the cursor arrives; then it turns bold with a text stroke, smoothly, and **does not
  move** (what the slide did to it - bolder, bigger, nudged - is stripped from the slide, `emph`, and only tells which text it is). **Click:** the cursor
  presses, a ring spreads, then the cursor fades out and the screen changes.
- **After a title** the next scene is the one with its text (a tablet by itself in the middle is not shown). **Words that a slide shows while the cursor is still
  at work** (Knowledge Is Readily Accessible, on the slide where the drawer opens) are written after the click that opens what they are about (Onboarding), and
  the dark panel stays away until then. **A title and the conclusion** fade in small and grow; the first title fades out growing when the tablet comes, the conclusion stays on (it does not fade out at the end) (`TITLE`); the slides that show the
  title smaller or fainter (steps of that) are not played, the video does it.
- **Highlights** wait for the cursor to have arrived and be resting on them, then fade in (`LATE`); one that the slide draws earlier is moved to that frame.
- **The video's timeline** (the layer named Video timeline) is put exactly on the bottom of the film (its width, its bottom edge) and runs by the clock (0:00 up).
  The film is a real `<video>`: loaded as soon as the frame before it is built, it **plays by itself** while the video plays (set right only when it drifts
  by 0.4 s; set by the clock when the video is scrubbed), and is paused when its frame is left. The words that came after the Onboarding click stay (with the
  panel and the tablet beside them) while the video plays; the frame stays on for 4.5 s of it. The cursor stays on the film and leaves with the tablet at the
  scene change (it does not fade on its own).
- **Centering:** when the right side is gone (the Panel parked off the slide, `away`) the tablet and what is on it go to the middle of the video
  (`centerArt`) and glide to the left again when words come.
- **Speed (the user, 2026-10-09):** the pen's Speed slider is in **letters** a second (4 to 60; `anims` hold `cps`; older `wps` is read as 6 letters a word; an older `seconds` was only the old default, the video does not follow it), because words are not all as long;
  `node scripts/storyboard.mjs animate <frame> <chat box> handwrite [letters/s]`.
- The move animations are made when a move starts (a paused one holds its first keyframe and would override the fades the frames do by their clock).

**A good video of the software (the user, 2026-10-09/10: "scroll animation in the software, expanding to show mission details in a smooth way ... check every frame").**
- **The software moves, it is not crossfaded.** Two slides of the same screen (same tree, 3 levels deep: `morphable`) are one screen in two states: every
  element goes from its old inline values to its new ones (`morph`, `MORPH_PROPS`: transform, scale, opacity, visibility, height, width, top, left, maxHeight,
  gridTemplateRows, marginTop, clipPath, and the screen's own place) — the checklist scrolls, the chip opens into Mission Details, the quiz folds into its
  Done tile, the knob slides. A card the demo names (`data-card`, `data-mission-id`, `data-iq-target`: `MOVE_KEYS`) that is somewhere else in the next slide
  **glides there** (`glideFrom`): a claimed mission goes from Open to Claimed, the priority card rises to the top and the others slide down. Cards of one board
  laid out another way glide too (`magicMove`). Something put in the place of another (a picture by the video it took) is just swapped. The software shown by
  another piece altogether (the phones, then the tablet; overlap under 0.6 or another class) goes, then the other comes (`other`, `artSwap`, `artLeaves`).
- **Clicks, from the demo's own names.** The slides are named after the hand's stops (`lib/storyboardFrames.mjs`: "Hand on X (k of n)", "Click X (k of n)",
  "After the click (k of n)"). `clickTarget` (`lib/demoTargets.mjs`) gives the stop of the click (HAND_STOPS of the story) and `targetSelector` its element
  (the demos' TARGETS, now shared from `lib/demoTargets.mjs`; the steps' one spot is the n-th Yes): the cursor goes exactly there. A ripple's slide that still
  shows the screen before the click (a photo, a video come a moment later) is not played: the click gives the slide after it at once. "After the click" with
  no slide of its own click (the No before Add video) was made from the slide before. A "Click" of another stop than the slide before's: its change came from
  that one (the knob drag), this click's own comes after. A list still scrolling under a still cursor is not a click (`clicked`).
- **The cursor:** a click that ends its work fades in the next slide, drawn over the new screen (`handGone`); when the screen goes on by itself before the next
  click (the lesson plays, the quiz comes up) the cursor rests where it clicked (`rest`); a held stop **drags** (`drag`, `DRAG`): the rating knob goes with the
  cursor, Business Proofs and the report are swiped up as they scroll. A click's frame always rests `HOLD.rest` (0.45 s) after its move (a click was cut off).
- **Filmed from further back:** a slide that has the software going under the words' side (the ratings zoomed in) is zoomed out to fit the left side, whole,
  with the cursor and outlines (`fitArt`, `FIT`; measured on every layout by what paints, a device frame drawn by `::before` included; the layer may sit in
  the demo's scaled camera: moved by what the screen says, `zoomLayer`).
- **The words use the whole right side** (the user, 2026-10-10: "this whole space should be able to be used when writing", "font size 50"): in the video a
  chat box on the right has its words 45 px and ONE width everywhere, 380 px (what the room at the right of a tablet gives them: from where the tablet's frame is SEEN to end, 1173, to the edge, less 24 each side), in the middle of the room: with no tablet (a mission, the web report) the room is the dark side and the words are the same width in the middle of it (`spreadWords`, `WORDS`, `DEVICE`); the slides keep their 300 boxes.
- **Outlines:** one that changes place or size goes quickly and the new one comes once the screen is still; one only nudged (12 px) glides; the same one under
  another name stays; on an opening slide they come after the software (`late`, `keep`).
- **More rules from making the Animations reel (2026-10-12):** a **zoom** (the art's own scale changes by a third or more) is slower than the other moves,
  0.9 s (`ZOOM_MOVE`); a Panel stacked **over** the art in one of two slides stays over it all through the move (what runs under the words is cut all the way: the
  screen's black bezel never shows over the panel); a **title replaced by another title** leaves growing like any title; a card **lifted out of its board** (`Lifted
  mission`, `Isolated missions`) pops: it grows from 80% to its size, a little over, 0.6 s, around its own middle; **words only, then the software** (or the software,
  then words only) is a change of scene like a title is (the old goes, then the new comes: never both half there), and the software coming in alone after words only
  is a beat of its own (it is not dropped as an empty slide); art with nothing in common with the art before it (phones, then a tablet) goes out and the next comes after.
  A duplicate of a slide plays by the rules of its source's scene (`sceneOf`).
- **Smooth and the same in every video (the user, 2026-10-12: "fix this timeline … smooth out the pop animation and don't highlight … claim and close should have no highlights … run a pass over all videos to check for any issue like stutter, highlight behind UI, not smooth transitions … consistent across all videos").** Found by playing every video of both boards frame by frame (30 a second) and looking at what changes from one frame to the next (a frame that changes far more than its neighbours is a cut or a jump), then fixed in the engine, so every video has it:
  - **Words always have the gray side behind them** (the user, 2026-10-21: "some videos has this issue, where text background is not visible yet text is still visible"). In the Knowledge Base
    video, the words of `knowledge/words` are kept over `knowledge/result`, the menu the click opened (`wordsFirst`), and that slide has its gray side parked off the picture (x 1600). The
    words floated over the light plate. Two fixes in the director:
    - the kept words bring their gray side with them when it is in place on their slide (`bringPanel`);
    - a last pass gives any slide that shows words while its gray side is parked the gray side of the nearest slide that has it in place, of its own scene first (after `leaveOnNewScreen`,
      every video, every line).

    The detailed twins already did this; they now share `bringPanel`.
  - **The video's timeline** (the layer named Video timeline) is as wide as the film, flush with its bottom edge, its two bottom corners as round as the film's (`placeTimelines`, `components/storyboardDom.js`; the card the film is in clips the film to that radius). It is drawn at its own box and scaled to the film's width, so what is in it keeps its proportions; the art filmed from further back (`fitArt`) had scaled it a second time, so it came out 25% narrower, floating under the film. The board's frames place it the same way (it is one thing).
  - **The pop** (a card lifted out of its board: the layers `Lifted mission`, `Isolated missions`): the lifted card is drawn exactly over the board's own card, which the slide has hidden, and **rises from there** (`liftSpan`, `liftKeys`): it grows to its lifted size with a settle of about 2% and goes in a straight line, 0.85 s (`LIFT_MOVE`), the board dimming by the same clock; then the board goes and the card glides to the middle, 0.85 s. Before, the board swelled 5% and shrank back at the start (the demo's camera is zoomed more in the slide that has the card lifted, and the screen's own scale was what was moved), the card faded in somewhere else and the isolate was a cut. The demo's step of the lift (`emphasis/fading`) is not played: the video lifts in one move.
  - **No yellow outline in the pop and in claim and close** (`NO_MARKS`: `emphasis` of Improved Quality, `claim` and `close` of Increased Efficiency: the demo's outline is not played there; the Animations line's pop and claim slides have none either, `plain`). An outline that is the same one a little further on glides as the NEW frame's copy, so it is over the new art all the way and never goes behind it.
  - **A screen is moved by where it IS on the screen, not by its styles** (`glideSpan`): the root of a slide's art goes from where (and how big) the old one is to where the new one is, whatever camera the demo had in each slide. A lifted layer is put on another by its card, a copied layer by its piece (under its stage and camera wrappers: `anchorOf`, `coreOf`), tops side by side (a card that grows keeps its top). **The same software on the move glides and scales** (the Panel wipe's tablet to the left) instead of one going out and another coming in through nothing: `differentArt` tells different software apart by what it is (class, shape, words), never by where it is.
  - **Nothing starts or stops at speed.** The move runs on an eased clock, so something that starts part way through (the screen changes once the cursor has pressed, a swipe once the finger is down) used to start at the fastest the clock goes: now it eases in over its own part of the move (`windowEasing`, `windowKeys`, the animation's `easing`), and every fade is a smoothstep over its part in the time of the move (`fadeFrames`).
  - **No hard cuts at a click.** The new screen comes in over the old one in a short dissolve (`takeOver`, `TAKE_OVER`: 0.22 of the move) and the old goes once it is covered (the picture never dips): what is the same does not change, what is different is crossed. The Knowledge Base drawer slides in from the right where the menu was (`arrives`).
  - **Layout changes are animated** (`flip`): what is in both screens and is somewhere else now goes from where it was (a card that grows, the things under it that go lower, the rest higher when a row is gone), a box that is taller grows down, over the same part of the move; the two screens' children are paired by what they are (tag, class, words) in order (`pairKids`), so a thing put in among others does not make each one after it the one before.
  - **The jumps that were left after all of that** (found by the same frame-by-frame look, 8 of them, each a different cause): (1) a section that folds from `none` to `0.93fr` cannot be moved through (a track list and `none` do not interpolate: it switches half way, and everything under it jumped 11 px): its own height goes from the one to the other (`sameTracks`, `heightOf`); (2) a thing the demo grows (a gallery row, a reveal) already moves what is after it, because the page lays it out lower by itself, and the glide of the layout (`flip`) moved it again: the sections under it started a second time too far (the report table, 5 px): `flip` takes off what the things above it in the flow have grown (`carried`, `sum`; not what is absolutely positioned: a line drawn down a side makes no room); (3) a thing that is only in the old screen (a phone that goes) fades out where it was, over the first part of the move, instead of going at once (`found.gone` of `flip`, `showGone`: the old screen is not shown but for the way down to each of them, shown with its own paint hidden, and everything else that hangs on that way hidden by its opacity: `visibility: hidden` on the whole old screen is NOT enough, what the demo reveals by itself paints on, a ghost of the old rating card stayed to the end of the move); it shows where the new screen has nothing over it, where it has an opaque thing over it (a card's white) it is cut as before; words, numbers, times, colors and faces that change in a thing that stays change AT ONCE, as in the software (a crossfade of them was tried: the old one is under the new screen, so what came out was a blink, the old gone and the new fading in); (4) a card lifted out of a board only rises from the card of the board when that card is seen on the board of the same scene (`liftSpan`: not hidden, not scrolled out of its window, not the board of another scene under a page): else it grows where it is and fades in (the reel's Scroll to Drag popped a card in at half size from a card hidden under the page).
  - **No stutter while it plays** (a Chrome trace of a real-time play, the CPU profile attributed to each picture: `getBoundingClientRect` asked of a page that has just changed is what costs: it lays the whole screen out, and what the picture asks for after it lays it out again). (1) The page was asked where things are **every picture** (`layoutKeyOf` for "has the layout changed", the cursor's place in `clickAt` during a click: 10 ms a picture): now the size of the player is watched (`ResizeObserver`) and a click only asks for the cursor's element. (2) Getting the **next slide ready** (drawn, laid out, the pen made ready, every animation made: 60 to 140 ms) was all done in the first picture of each hold, which stopped the video there: it is a **step in each picture of the hold** now (`prepareAhead`: 1 drawn, 2 laid out — `build` no longer lays out, `ensureLayout` does —, 3 the pens, 4 the animations; a step that is done is kept, so a move that comes first does what is left at once; a jump on the bar still does it all at once). (3) The **pen** (`components/storyboardHandwriting.js`; its cost was the letters' pixels): when the canvas is big (200,000 px or more) the letters are thinned and walked from the same words drawn on a canvas twice as small (`GRID`: a quarter of the pixels to read and walk; the pen is as thick as the strokes, the path differs by under a pixel and was checked frame by frame against full size: at most 40 of a million pixels differ), their pixels are read as one number each (not four), a font's ascent is asked once for each font (not for every word), the path of words already made ready is kept (`PATHS`: the same words set the same way), and `thin` is faster (the same skeleton, tested). (4) A slide's chat boxes were **drawn again** (and the video's picture drawn twice) after every slide was drawn, because the fonts were "loading" (`whenFontsLoaded`): it does nothing when they are in already (`document.fonts.check`). (5) The pen of the first slide is made ready when the pointer **rests** on a video for a quarter of a second (`warm`, `onPointerEnter` of the player: not while the board is moving under the pointer), not as the video starts (60 to 120 ms there). What is left is the first time of anything: a new slide's style and layout (20 to 30 ms for a big screen, the phone's; it cannot be split: the page styles the whole new tree at once), and the first pen with a font (the glyphs drawn for the first time, 25 to 55 ms). In "The tablet and its Knowledge Base" (dev server, a real-time play): pictures over 16.7 ms 109 → 57, over 33 ms 10 → 5, the worst 133 → 58 ms (the first picture of a play, which has all of it to make); the light videos (Increased Efficiency) are at 3 to 5 ms a picture, a few pictures over 16.7 ms in all. **Looking for all of this again: `node scripts/storyboard-qa.mjs cuts|marks|play`** (the site running; `--video`, `--line "<part of a title>"`, `--fps`, `--keep <dir>`): `cuts` renders every video by seeking at 30 a second and prints the second of every jump (a picture that changes far more than its neighbours is a cut), `marks` finds a yellow outline drawn behind the software, or a popped card that hides what it is round, `play` is the real-time trace above (slow pictures in order, what each did).
- **A video of its own pace and look: PROFILES (`lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`, the user, 2026-10-13: "only the video of Documented evidence" = What managers see, the run that ends with that line):** 10 s in all (`tempo`, `natural`, `rest`, the pen at 42 letters a second); the tablet as the slides have it, over the edge of the gray plate (`asSlides`: not filmed from further back, the words 50 px in the room at its right); the menu opening **dims everything else in the tablet** (`dimMenu`: the software's own shade, `.bn-shade`, that the demo hides, `.sb-dim-menu` in Storyboard.css); what the cursor rests on **thickens over 0.55 s** (`hover`: the stroke grows with the video's time in `pensAt`; a stage has `transition: none !important`, so the usual bold, a CSS transition, came all at once in every video — the other videos still have that); and the list of Business Proofs is **scrolled once, continuously, by the time** (`scroll`: `oneScroll` in `directorOf` plays only the first slide of each scene after the click that opens the list, with the art of the last, and `driveScroll` moves the list from where it opens to where it ends, easing in and out, so the words change over it without it stopping; no hand on it). A video with no profile is exactly as before.
- **The lesson screen (the user, 2026-10-13: "when I hit the video of the shelving standards show what I sent as a new UI, like a video under it a quiz, while fading out the mission details, then after hitting Submit & Close go back to mission details"):** in the mission video, the click on the training video (Micro-training) opens a screen of its own, `lib/storyboardLesson.mjs` (the blurred video with its play button, the Quiz bar with its arrows, "1. Please select ALL of the correct statements.", the statements with their boxes, Submit & Close; CSS `.sb-lq-*` in Storyboard.css), drawn in the place of the mission details; the mission fades out, the lesson comes, the cursor ticks the first two statements and hits Submit & Close, the lesson fades out and the mission details come back with the training done. `lessonBeats` in `directorOf` puts the three beats in (the slides between, the video growing in the mission and its inline quiz, are not played; `PROFILES.mission.lesson`); the asset is made in the page (`sb-lesson:<state>`), not captured.
  **The video plays, and it is about stock shelving (the user, 2026-10-13: "make the video on top playing before answering the quiz, and make the quiz and video about stock shelfing"):** the lesson's video is the real clip of the training tile (`public/demo-quality/shelving-training.mp4`, 6 s; a stock-room shelf), not a blurred still. The lesson opens **paused** (`sb-lesson:play`: the first picture blurred, the big play button, no bar; the mission fades out as it comes in) and the cursor goes to the play button (`LESSON_PLAY`); it presses, and over `LESSON_FILM` (`lib/storyboardLesson.mjs`) the picture clears, the button goes, the bar runs from `0:06` to `0:00` and the clip plays by the video's clock (`film` in `directorOf`, the `film` block and `runFilm` in the player's `pensAt`; the slide is `rest` with `holdSel`, so the cursor waits on the button for the 6 s); the picture is on its LAST frame after (`public/demo-quality/shelving-training-end.jpg`, so there is no jump back to the first), and only then the cursor goes to the statements, ticks the first two and presses Submit & Close. The quiz is three stock-shelving statements (heavy items on the lower shelves; containers labeled, label out, older stock first; the wrong one: boxes on the floor when the shelves are full). The clip's length is in `LESSON_FILM.clip` (change both if the file is replaced).
  **The cursor in the mission video (the user, 2026-10-13: "make sure the cursor go and click doesn't stop at something for a while before clicking", "don't show the cursor coming from text when going to video ... just show the cursor fade in from where it was left", "don't fade out the cursor after the quiz just keep it visible from quiz to add photo and video going to the right place and clicking"):** `PROFILES.mission` has `keepCursor` and `dwell`. `dwell: 0.12`: a cursor that has come to what it clicks presses 0.12 s after it arrives (it was over half a second: the hover's lead + fade + pause; `tune.natural` / `tune.rest` per slide in `timelineOf`). `keepCursor`: the cursor is one thing from its first click to the last (the one fade is after the last click, Add video): between two scenes it does not fade out and come back from the caption; it rests where it pressed (the card scrolls under it) while the next scene's words are written, then goes to the next click from there (`rest` slides, `cursorOnly` so an otherwise empty beat is still dropped; the words slide `~words` keeps it). The first cursor of the video (the translate button) still comes in from its words: there is no place it was left.
  **The rating (the user, 2026-10-13: "the submit button should become gray after submit, and blue before", "the cursor is slower than the slide, it should be the one dragging the slide so stay put on top of the knob"; the video "The tablet, the mission, the ratings"):** (1) the cursor that drags the knob (or swipes a list) is on it at every moment: it goes by the SAME curve as what it holds, `ease` over the window `[0.12 (0.3 for a swipe), DRAG.end]` of the move (`windowEasing`), sampled on the move's eased clock (the drag branch of `prepare`); it was four linear keyframes on that already-eased clock, so it started late, ended early and trailed the knob. (2) Every Submit Rate is blue until it is submitted and gray after: the self rating's at the cursor's press (`submit`: ready / done); the peers' (`peerSubmit`, `sendPeers`, `PEER_SEND`) blue from the moment their cards are there (even before a knob has moved), and when the last peer slide has their ratings in, each is pressed in turn (no cursor: they rate on their own devices; it dips and the colour snaps) and turns gray; the slide holds long enough to see it (`move + PEER_SEND.total`).
  **The board to the mission's rating (the user, 2026-10-13: "the mission shouldn't change its position, why it came here, just fade out the tablet and keep the mission in it's right position and then after tablet completly fade out zoom in on it"):** the slides where the card is lifted out over the dimmed board (the card travelled to the middle) are not played; the board goes to the rating in ONE move (`fadeThenZoom`, `TABLET_FADE`: 2.4 s). First half: the tablet fades out (and the first words with it) while the card stays exactly on its place in the Closed column (the lifted copy is drawn over its own card, `liftSpan`); second half: the tablet gone, the card zooms to its place in the rating, easing in and out (`liftKeys(..., settle = false)` under `windowEasing(TABLET_FADE.share, 1)`: no lift, no overshoot), and the rating's words are written. The Pop up clip in the Animations line still lifts the card as it did (it does not go on to a rating). **Test it by PLAYING from 0:00, not by jumping to a time:** the first slide of a video comes in (a little small and faint, `intro`: `setFade` / `setSize`), and the move into the rating is made ready while it is still coming in; the card on the board was measured faint, `liftSpan` found no card, and the fallback (a copy growing at the left, the board's own card fading with the tablet) played, but only in a real play (a jump to a later time had the board settled and looked right). `settled(stage, measure)` now measures the board as it will be once its entrance is over.
  **Anything the clock moves, resizes or fades belongs in `fadesAt`, not in `pensAt` (the user, 2026-10-13: "the green guy review goes down for a split second if I play the video, but if I drag the timeline I don't see this glitch"):** a move is got ready AHEAD (`prepare`, in the first pictures of the previous slide's hold) and reads the outgoing slide as it is then, except for what `fadesAt` sets: `prepare` puts that in its end-of-hold state first (`fadesAt(A, 0, null, 'done')`) and back to the clock after. The peer cards' pop (`--sb-pop`: 14 px low, 94% size, faint, until the connector reaches them) was set in `pensAt`, so the move into the next slide began from the card as it was before it had popped and slid the next slide's card up from 22 px too low: a dip only a real play showed (a drag of the bar prepared the move with the slide at the end of its hold). It is in `fadesAt` now (the `peerFlow` block). To see a glitch like this, record the thing's position every animation frame while PLAYING from a few seconds before it, not from just before it, and compare with a scrub.
- **The tablet in every video, the rating, Submit, the report (the user, 2026-10-13; the slides are not touched, the videos play them differently):**
  - **Whenever there is a tablet it is the one of the 6th slide of the Scratch line** (`tabletFit`, `TABLET`: its frame 1220 x 851 at the left, 25 from the top, over the edge of the gray plate; the words have what is left at its right, 20 px margin). Not a tablet that is zoomed into (it goes past the slide) nor one that has no words' side (that is centered). What is not a tablet (the web report) is still filmed from further back (`fitArt`). The photo's file name under a picture (`.iq2-m-file`, barely visible blue text) is not shown in videos.
  - **A closed mission lifted out of the board does not jump** (Emphasis → Self-rating): the tablet fades while the card goes to its place in the rating layout, in one move (`profileMove` 1.3 s of the item, the "alone" slide between them is dropped); the card still glides once because it is at another place in the rating layout than on the board.
  - **The rating slider goes over every word** (Poor, OK, Good, Great, Excellent: `RATE_LABELS`, `followRate` in `showMove`): the knob is dragged slowly (`RATE_DRAG`) and the word under it changes as it passes, it does not stop on each.
  - **Submit is blue before the click and gray after it** (`x.submit` = `ready` | `done` in the director, a class on the stage in `build`), in Self-rating and Peer ratings.
  - **No blink when the cursor comes** (Business Proofs, the web report): the `~words` slide the director splits in two had `intro` twice, so the art faded out and in when the cursor arrived (`x.intro = false` in the split).
  - **Business Media in the menu: the yellow rectangle, a little bolder, a little bigger, all growing with the hover (the user, 2026-10-13: "highlight business media using the yellow rectangle, and bold it a tiny bit, like if it's 400 make it 600 weight with a smooth thickening transition, and make the font size slightly bigger like by 2 px but only on hover with a smooth transition, not a static change"; this replaces the blue tint of the same day, "remove the bolding on business media, just highlight it").** A row of `.bn-menu-item` that the cursor rests on (`setStroke`, the `.bn-menu-item` branch) gets an `<i class="sb-row-mark">` inside it (the demos' yellow rounded outline, `#ffc800`, 5 px, at the row's size and 10 px wider each side: Storyboard.css), its opacity, the label's `font-size` (+2 px of its own 14.2 px) and its text stroke all following the same smoothstep over the hover's `fade` (0.55 s), none of it before the cursor is there; seeking back puts it all back. The weight is a **text stroke, not `font-weight`**: Montserrat is loaded at 500, 600 and 700 only (the menu's 400 is drawn as 500), and a font has whole weights, so `font-weight` would jump half way. `0.021em` of stroke has the ink of a real 600 (measured on a canvas, 477 against 477 px of ink for "Business Media" at 15 px, the 500 being 381): `ROW_HOVER` in `storyboardPlayerDom.js`, said again in `PROFILES.managers.hover.row` (`size`, `weight`). Only this video (the one with `hover` in its profile) has it.
  - **The web report's tour is one scroll** (`PROFILES.report.scroll`, 3.4 s, easing in and out: slow, fast, slow), the words over it.
  - **The report does not scroll back up between "Touring the report" and "Who performed" (the user, 2026-10-13: "sometimes the scrolling goes up and down when playing the video, it doesn't show up when I hold the timeline and drag it").** A move is got ready AHEAD, in the pictures of the hold before it (`prepareAhead`), and `morph` takes the old slide's inline styles as where each thing goes FROM. The list that is scrolled by the time (`driveScroll`) was, in those first pictures of the hold, still at the top of its scroll, so the move into the next scene began with the list there and scrolled it down again (a jump of 691 px back to the top at about 8.1 s, then down over half a second) — only when playing, because dragging the bar gets the move ready at the moment it is shown. `prepare` now puts the driven lists of both slides where they are WHEN THE MOVE STARTS (`timeline.items[b].start`) before it measures and makes the animations, and puts them back to the clock's now when it is done (else the one picture shown right after would flash the end of the scroll). Checked by playing the video in real time and reading the list's `translateY` every picture: 3 of 3 plays had the jump before, 0 of 4 after.
  - **The dashboard is as big as the tablet, over the edge of the plate (the user, 2026-10-13: "make the dashboard bigger like what we did with the tablet in previous videos, where it sticks out of the dark gray right side area and big enough").** The web report (`.iq2-report`, the root of its art layer) goes through `tabletFit` like a tablet (`bigDevice`: the report if the slide has it, else the tablet's bezel; it was filmed from further back, 83%, by `fitArt`): it is as wide as the tablet's frame is SEEN (`REPORT`: x 40 to 1172 of the 1600, the iPad's photo has an empty margin round its frame, the report has none), on the tablet's middle line, never taller than the slide less 40 px each side, with its cursor and outlines scaled with it; the words have the room at its right (`spreadWords`, the report's right edge).
  - **The mission video opens on the card and opens it at once; instructions, alert and link have one beat (the user, 2026-10-13, on "The mission: instructions to video proof": "expand the mission as soon as the video starts and make the first sentence about instructions just two sentences so brief ... instruction alert and link should have same timing, and make them stay longer, highlight then write on the right, keep it for like .7 seconds and then move on, so 3 seconds for writing and highlighting and 0.7 seconds for holding it").**
    - **The words:** the instruction slides (`instructions:2` to `:4`, through `scripts/storyboard.mjs text`, then `move` to keep the box's middle on y = 450: a retyped box changes height) say **"Add Instructions. / Reinforce Standards."** — two short sentences, two lines like the alert and the link (it was "Within Each Mission Instructions Can Be Included To Remind About Standards", four lines). The second line is kept short on purpose: the video's words box is about 380 px wide and "Remind About Standards." wrapped to a third line.
    - **The opening** (`PROFILES.mission.open`, `openCard`): the first slide is the mission as a card with NO words (they were written beside it for 3.6 s before the mission opened): it comes in over `pop` (0.4 s) and the move into the next slide, the mission opened (Mission Details), takes `expand` (0.8 s), starting at once. The words and the highlight of the instructions are written after it.
    - **One beat** (`PROFILES.mission.beats`, `beatsOf`: the scenes `instructions`, `alerts`, `links`; `seconds: 3, hold: 0.7, fade: 0.35`): once the move into the scene is over the highlight comes in (0.35 s), THEN the pen writes, and highlight and words together take 3 s (the pen's speed is made to fit each text, 2.6 s of writing: `cps = letters / 2.6`), then the scene stays 0.7 s. So each of the three is its move + 3.7 s (4.5 s with the opening's 0.8 s move, 4.2 s with the usual 0.5 s). It needed the timeline to take a pen start of its own (`penAt`, seconds after the move; `penStart` in `lib/storyboardPlayer.mjs`) and the highlight's own lead and fade (`lateLead`, `lateFade`): until now the pen touched during the first half of the move (`WRITE.early`, "don't keep the right side empty") and the highlight came after it; here it is the other way round, so the right side is empty from the old words going to the highlight being in (about 0.6 s).
    - **A bug found on the way, in every video with a card that comes in at the start:** the move into the next slide is got ready AHEAD (`prepareAhead`) from what the slide it goes from looks like, and the fades that go by the clock (the opening card coming in, an outline fading in, the cursor's fade at the end of a click: `fadesAt`, taken out of `pensAt`) were read as they were at that moment, in the first pictures of the hold — the card half faded, so the move began with it faint (a flash), an outline not yet in read as not there. `prepare` now reads the slide it goes from as it is at the END of its hold (`fadesAt(A, 0, null, 'done')`) and puts it back to the clock when it was on show (the same trap as the scroll and the peers' connector above; the other two parts of `pensAt`, the pens and the clips that play, are not touched). Checked in a real-time play of the opening (the card's opacity: 0 → 1 in 0.3 s, never down; each scene's old highlight goes, the new one fades in, the words come after it) and with `node scripts/storyboard-qa.mjs cuts` (below).
  - **The peers' ratings: the purple connector is drawn out of the mission like a trim path, and the peers' faces wait for their Submit (the user, 2026-10-13, on "The tablet, the mission, the ratings": "don't show the avatar in mission until it hits submit, fix the purple line to be connected here, it connects afterwards but it didn't connect before, also make it has an animation like trim path in After Effects, where it smoothly goes from mission to rating 1 then rating 2").** The slides keep the connector as the demo had it when each was taken (the bracket as far down as the demo had got: the first slide's did not reach the second card's tick, the second slide's did, so it "connected afterwards"; the cards half in; both peers' faces already on the mission). The video draws the scene by its own clock instead (`peerDraw`, `PEER_DRAW`; the first slide of the scene is `peerFlow: 'draw'`, the rest `'drawn'`): once the move into the slide is over, one head runs along the path (the line out of the mission, down the bracket to the first card's level, its tick, on down to the second card's, its tick: 365 px of the layout's own, 1.5 s, smoothstep, so it eases in and out), the bracket is always its full height (294 px), and each card pops (0.35 s) as the head gets to its tick. The faces on the mission (`.chip-rating[data-sb-rater]`, tied to their card by who they are) are not there until that peer's Submit Rate is pressed (`PEER_SEND`), then pop over 0.3 s (the self rating's face still comes with its own Submit, as before). It is all **custom properties set by the clock** (`--sb-out`, `--sb-trunk`, `--sb-tick`, `--sb-pop`, `--sb-av`) that the rules at the end of `components/Storyboard.css` turn into `clip-path` / `opacity` / `transform`, never the elements' own inline styles: a move is got ready AHEAD (`prepareAhead`) from the inline styles of the slide it goes from, and a half-drawn line in them would have been taken as where the next slide goes from (the same trap as the report's scroll above). The first slide of the scene is held `PEER_DRAW.total` (2.3 s) after its move for the drawing (it was 1.7 s: the video is 0.6 s longer). Checked frame by frame (the properties at 14.8 … 19 s) and in a real-time play: every series only goes up, nothing flashes.
  - **Who performed and Date & time: 2 seconds longer, and a slow, smooth pan between them (the user, 2026-10-13: "add 2 seconds to who performed and 2 seconds to date & time, make transition slower and smoother, a stop a bit then move").** `PROFILES.report.scenes` (`performer: {extra: 2}`, `timestamp: {move: 1.8, wait: 0.4, extra: 2}`): `extra` is seconds added to the last slide of the scene once all of it is there (`tune.extra` in `timelineOf`: Who performed 1.5 → 3.5 s, Date & time 1.66 → 4.8 s with its longer move); `move` is the move into the scene's first slide (the report going sideways to the Date & Time column, it was 0.5 s), of which the screen **stands still for `wait`** (the old outlines and words go) and then goes, easing in and out over the rest (`info.still`, the `wait` of the morph in `prepare`: the new screen is there from the first moment at the place of the old one and its parts move after `wait`; **no dissolve** — the click's dissolve was tried and showed two reports at once for 0.2 s). The scenes before are not moved: only what comes after Who performed is later.
  - **A yellow outline around one line of text has the text in the middle of it**, side to side and up and down (`PROFILES.report.centerMarks`, `centerMarks`: measured on the words, the outline is moved, grown only if the words do not fit with 6 / 3 px around).
- **The words (the user, 2026-10-13, second batch: "make text 45 size and center it between tablet and right side", "show text on the right as soon as you highlight, maybe highlight after showing the first word", "don't keep right side empty, always fade out and show first word right away", "in video proof don't scroll up too much", then "fix this spacing to always be like this on all of them"):**
  - **45 px, 380 wide, in the middle of the room at the right of the art** (`WORDS`): the room starts where the tablet's frame is SEEN to end (the iPad photo has an empty margin at its right: `DEVICE.right`, 1173 of the 1220 box), not at its box, so the words are centered between the tablet and the right side; the box's own padding is outside the 380 (`bubblePad`). With no tablet the same width is centered in the dark side.
  - **The pen touches as soon as the old words are gone** (`penStart` in `lib/storyboardPlayer.mjs`: half the move + 0.05 s, the first frame 0.15 s), the new words are there at full strength from then (`'pen-in'` fade: no faint first letters), so the first word shows right away.
  - **The right side is never empty** (the pass "the right side is never empty" in `directorOf`): a slide with the software and no words (the outline that comes before its words, the cursor clicking before the words come) is played with the words of the next slide that has them (the last ones stay when none come); a slide that is only the outline in front of the next one's words (same screen) is not played. The outline comes after the first word (`late`, 0.25 s after the move). This replaces "Knowledge Is Readily Accessible is written only after Onboarding is opened" (2026-10-09).
  - **Video proof** (`PROFILES.mission.showStep`, `keepStepInView`): the list of the mission is not scrolled so far that the first words of its last step ("6. Is the equipment working properly?") are under the header that stays at the top: the list is put back down (the header the other way) so they have 18 px of room.
- An error in the video never takes the board down (`render` catches it). Looking frame by frame in development: `window.__sbScripts[title]` (the script)
  and `window.__sbSeek[title](seconds)`.
- **The proof checkpoints are not in the video (the user, 2026-10-12: "remove this part from the video", the slide "Photos or videos can be taken within missions, they are not stored on the device and cannot be used in future instances")**: the line "Photos and videos: the proof checkpoints" (`capture`) stays on the board, the mission video does not play it (`VIDEO_RUNS` in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`: put `'capture'` back before `'photo'` to play it again).

## The video editor (the user, 2026-10-22)

First: "make it like figma in terms of slides, the videos I should be able to open like a video editor when I double click a video". Then: "the video
editor should be like after effects in terms of scroll in video, press doesn't play it selects, all elements should be editable, and I can apply to all
... the background the text everything should selectable, I should be able to add images ... timeline should be like after effects timeline", and
"most importantly, is a shape tool, shapes, keyframes, and optimization where I can ask you to edit on the fly". Asked: an element's **look goes to
every slide of the video, its place is the slide's**; the timeline is **layers and bars, the video's own timing fixed**; a picture added is **a new
layer of the video**. The slides on the board stay as they are (Figma's way).

- **Opening.** Since 2026-10-30 (the user: "double clicking should let me edit it without changing how it was, and without opening the video editor ... the video editor should open if I right click
  the video"): a **right-click** on any video (the player at the end of a run, a cell of a line of videos) stops it and opens the board's menu, whose first item, **Open in video editor**, opens it
  at the second it was on (`onMenu` of `StoryboardPlayer`, `openVideo` / `vEdit` in `Storyboard.jsx`; the menu is "A video's right-click menu" below). A double-click on the board retypes a text in the video
  again. Esc (with nothing picked), **Board** or the cross closes the editor.
- **The layout** (`components/StoryboardVideoEditor.jsx`, `.sb-ve-*` in `Storyboard.css`), over the board at the left of the sidebar: the top bar
  (Board, the **tools**, the title, Undo / Redo, close), the **viewer**, the transport, the **timeline** (300 px). The board's **sidebar is the
  inspector** of what is picked.
- **The viewer (After Effects' composition panel).** The video's 1600 x 900 picture (the same `StoryboardPlayer`, `editor`: no handlers of its own,
  driven by `api`) zoomed and panned: the **wheel zooms around the pointer**, Shift + wheel pans sideways, the **middle button, Space held or the Hand
  (H) pans**, the menu at the bottom left (Fit, 25 to 400 %), Ctrl + / − / 0. **A press picks, it never plays** (Space plays): in this order
  - a **layer** of the video (`layerAt`, its turn counted): picked, dragged to move (Shift: on one axis), its 8 handles resize it in its own axes
    (Shift: keeps its proportions), the round one above turns it (Shift: by 15°); one step of Undo a drag;
  - a **color part** (an outline, a connector, the plate, the gray side) or the **words**: the video's inspector with that section (`api.pick`,
    `lookTargetAt`); the **background** where nothing else is;
  - an **element of the slide on** (the whole asset; **Ctrl: the deepest thing there**): picked as the board's selection, so the sidebar is the
    asset's own inspector; dragged it moves **on that slide** (`setMove`). Its look (fill, stroke, opacity, effects, text) goes to **the same element
    on every slide of the video** (`setLook` in `Storyboard.jsx`, `sameInVideo`: the same key, else the same name and path); its turn stays the slide's.
    **Apply to all** (under its inspector) gives that look to **every element of its name on every slide of every board**: `looks.layers[name]`
    (`setLayerLook`, lib/storyboardEdits.mjs), copied to every board's file like the other shared looks, put on by `applyEdits` before a slide's own
    styles (each slide's root carries `data-sb-name`, `frameHtml`); the video's own copies are taken off. A part of an element is not shared (its
    path differs from board to board).
  The video is held where its slide is all there before something is picked (the picture can only be picked on a still).
  A **double-click** retypes a text of the slide (the text editor, as before), a text layer in place, or lights a color's section.
- **Resizing anything, After Effects' way (the user, 2026-10-22: "look for keyframes keep them to code, but ... if I want to resize object it should apply, so
  add resize to all object same way after effects has it"; asked: "every slide of this video").** An element of a slide picked in the picture has handles: a UI
  element by its corners (it keeps its proportions), a picture or a box by all eight (a picture keeps its proportions unless Shift, a box only with Shift); it grows
  from its middle (the anchor point). The size is the VIDEO's: `frames[id].vscale[key] = [fx, fy]` on every slide of the video the element is on (`assetOps.resize`,
  `sameInVideo`), put on over the video's own layout (`applyVideoScale` in components/storyboardPlayerDom.js; the tablet's fit would undo a size given to the slide)
  and taken off at the next layout; the slide on the board is not touched. The inspector shows "Size in this video" with − to give the element back the video's
  size. The video's own movement from slide to slide (its keyframes, in a way) stays the code's.
- **The motion path of what is picked (the user, 2026-10-22: "make the keyframes actually show the selected layer position line, and I should able to change it. ctrl+pagedown or up
  should skip to keyframes of the selected layer").** Over the picture, After Effects' way: a line through where the picked thing is at each of its keyframes, a square at each (the
  one now filled). A layer of the video: its position keyframes (the line follows its eased path). An element of the slides: where it is on each slide of the video it is on, its middle,
  measured by the video on a slot of its own that is never shown (`measure` in components/storyboardPlayerDom.js; a few slides a picture, kept until the slide or its edits change).
  Dragging a square moves it there (Shift: on one axis): a layer's position at that keyframe, an element's place in the video on that slide (`vmove`, stage px over where the video lays
  it out, over the tablet's fit too; `setVideoMove`; `vmove <frame|line> <asset> <dx> <dy>` in the CLI; the slide on the board is not touched; a square moved on one slide alone makes the element go there and back as the video plays: to hold it still give all its slides the same `vmove`); a click on it does not move the time; the square of now is drawn over the others, and a square of another time that is under the picked thing itself leaves the press to the thing (dragging it then makes a keyframe now, as in After Effects). A picked element of the slides stays picked when the video is held on another slide it is on (the same element there). An element dragged on the picture or nudged is moved the same way (its
  place in the video on that slide). The inspector shows "Place in this video" with − to give it back the video's place on every slide. **J / K** (the user, 2026-10-22: "use j and
  k instead"; Ctrl+Page Up / Down are the browser's) and the two ◂◆ ◆▸ buttons of the transport go to the keyframe before / after of what is picked: a layer's keyframes, or the
  slides an element is on. The line shows as soon as a layer is picked (the user, 2026-10-22: "make sure the line of the position should show up in the canvas of the video on
  selecting layer"): a layer with no position keyframe has its square where it is now (dragged: it moves, with no keyframe); an element of the slides is measured while the video
  plays too.
- **The line is where it really goes, picking never moves the time, and what is picked stays picked (the user, 2026-10-22: "when I try to select layer to see the line of its
  movement, I don't see the line ... it jumps the timeline head ... I don't want it to move the playhead ... even though I move the playhead, it should keep the selection").**
  - The line of an element of the slides is its real path as the video plays: the glides, scrolls, zooms and the cursor's trips between two slides too. It is measured on a copy
    of the video that is never seen, off the page (`track` in components/StoryboardPlayer.jsx: closely while it moves in, every quarter second while it is held; a few times a
    picture; between two slides the one more seen counts). It grows dashed while it is measured, then it is solid, with a dark edge so it is seen over the white software too. It is
    kept until the video or its edits change. Its squares are still where it rests on each slide, to drag.
  - Something that never moves (a panel, a tablet: its points all in one place) says so by its point: "It does not move in this video". A layer with no position keyframe says
    "No position keyframes".
  - A press never moves the time:
    - in the picture, what is on it now is picked, held or between two slides (`onScreen` in the engine);
    - a row or a bar of the timeline: its element on the slide on now, or the first one it is on;
    - a keyframe diamond, or a square of the motion path.
    J / K go to the keyframes.
  - The box of an element of the slides is drawn on whichever slide shows it now (`elementIn`, by its row): it stays on while the video plays, is scrubbed or is between two slides.
    It goes only where the element is not on the picture. The pick itself stays until another press.
- **Undo (the user, 2026-10-22: "undo sometimes doesn't work").** The color picker kept every key to itself while it was open, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y too: they go through to the board now
  (`ownKey` in components/StoryboardPanel.jsx). And the video's inspector reads the look it shows from the picture, which is drawn again after the board's edits change: each video says
  when it is drawn again (`onDrawn`) and the inspector of the video picked is drawn again after it, so after Undo it shows what the video shows. Then ("still doesn't work, the jk nor
  ctrl+z when I move something accidentally"): a field of the sidebar used before kept the keyboard (the picture's presses do not take it), so J / K went into the field: a press in
  the editor that is not in a field takes the keyboard out of it (`takeKeys`); and Undo / Redo are the editor's own first of all (a capture on the window, `undoKeys`: Ctrl+Z,
  Ctrl+Shift+Z, Ctrl+Y), but a text being typed (in the picture, a layer's words, a name or a value of the timeline) keeps its own.
- **A reload keeps the editor (the user, 2026-10-22: "when I refresh the video editor, it should keep timeline head where I leave it, and I should be still in editor").** The video
  open in it and the second it is held at are kept in this browser (`storyboard:editor:<board>`, written when it opens and each time it is held, taken off when it closes); once the
  board is loaded again the editor opens again there.
- **Guide lines (the button by Undo / Redo on the board and in the editor's top bar, Shift+G; kept in this browser).** Thin blue lines across the slide, like
  Figma's: through where the tablet's frame ends (the web report's edge), the four edges of the words, and the gray side's edge on a slide with no tablet
  (`guidesOf` in components/storyboardDom.js). On the board over every slide drawn, in the editor over the picture while the video is held.
- **The gray side of a slide with no tablet (the user, 2026-10-22: "for tablet view the right side gray area is good, but for other views, the right side area
  should be as smaller as from tablet right side of the frame till the end of the slide ... gray right side get smaller to the point where the text is centered
  horizontally"; asked: videos and board slides).** Its left edge goes to `PANEL_EDGE` (x 1172: where the tablet's frame is seen to end in the tablet's slides)
  and the words go to the middle of it, x 1386, where they are in the tablet's slides; the art stays where it is (`narrowPanel` in components/storyboardDom.js:
  after the slide's edits on the board (`FrameArt`; not the lines Scratch and Fonts), after the tablet's fit in a video (`layoutSlot`, the words centered by
  `spreadWords`)). A slide counts as the tablet's when it shows a whole tablet or the web report (`deviceOf`); a zoom on a part of one does not. The gray side is
  moved, not changed: its right part goes past the slide's edge.
- **Black behind bare software (the user, 2026-10-22: "make all video that has no tablet, no frames like mobile just UI or mobile UI without a frame, make their
  background black behind the UI, not behind text").** In every video, a slide whose software has no device's frame round it (no `.tbl-tablet`, `.ph-phone` or
  `.sim-device` shown: a mission's details, a card, a web page) has its plate black (`blackPlate` in components/storyboardPlayerDom.js, undone at the next layout);
  the gray side of the words stays, and a slide with no software (a title) is left alone. Then (the user, 2026-10-22: "on all videos/slides in all boards") the slides
  on every board too (`blackPlate` in components/storyboardDom.js, shared; on the board in `FrameArt`, not the lines Scratch and Fonts, which no rule changes). Software counts while it is faded out (a card that comes in starts at opacity 0: its first slide went gray, "sometimes it shows up gray at the beginning"), and on the board while its slide is still off the screen (`checkVisibility`, not its boxes). Checked on Improved Quality, Standardizing Enterprise, Live Oversight and Reactive Training: every slide with bare software black, every one with a tablet or a phone as it was.
- **Tools** (the top bar): Selection V, Hand H, **Rectangle R, Ellipse E** (drag to draw, Shift: a square / a circle; a click: one of a usual size),
  **Text T** (a click: a text layer, typed in place), **Image I** (or drop / paste a picture: uploaded like a picture on a frame, as big as fits in
  640 x 400, in the middle of the picture). A new layer is on screen **from now to the end of the line it is in**, and is picked.
- **The layers of a video** (`lib/storyboardLayers.mjs`, pure; drawn by `components/storyboardLayers.js` in EVERY player of the video: on the board,
  in a cell, in the editor): `edits.layers[<video title>] = {items, at}`, items from the back to the front, `{id, kind: rect | ellipse | text | image,
  name, from, to, x, y, w, h, rotation, opacity, fill, stroke, strokeWidth, radius | text, font, size, weight, color, align | src, hidden, locked,
  keys}`. **Keyframes are After Effects'**: a keyframe holds the properties it sets (x, y, w, h, rotation, opacity); a property is animated once a
  keyframe has it (the stopwatch), eased in and out between two (or `linear`, `hold`); a change at a time on an animated property makes or changes the
  keyframe there (`setLayerAt`), on another changes it for the whole layer. Merged by the newer `at` per video.
- **The timeline (After Effects').** One scroller, the names stuck at the left, the ruler at the top (press or drag it, or an empty part of a track,
  to scrub; Ctrl / Alt + wheel zooms the time around the pointer; Shift + wheel scrolls it; the slider and Fit). Rows, the front first:
