  - each **layer**: eye, lock, the twirl, its label color, its name (double-click: rename); its **bar** (drag: move it and its keyframes in time;
    its ends: trim; snaps to the playhead and the slides' starts) and its **keyframes ◆** (click: go there and pick it, Delete takes it off; drag: move
    it). Twirled open: **Position, Size, Rotation, Opacity**, each with the **stopwatch**, its values in blue (drag sideways or click and type), ◂ ◆ ▸
    (the keyframe before, one now / off, after) and its own keyframes;
  - **From the slides**: one row per element of the slides (by name), a bar while it is on screen and a dot where a slide of it starts; the eye hides
    it **in this video** (`hidden` on its slides); a press picks it on the slide on (or the first slide it is on);
  - **Slides**: their pictures and board numbers, the one on screen outlined yellow; a click goes there, a double-click to that frame on the board.
- **The inspector of a layer** (`layerPanel` in `Storyboard.jsx`): its values at the editor's time (Position X Y, Rotation, Layout W H, Opacity, the
  corner radius), each group with a ◆ (blue: animated; yellow: a keyframe now), Fill (the paint picker) and Stroke for a shape, the words / font /
  size / weight / align / color of a text, Replace the picture, In / Out (seconds), its keyframes with their easing (Easy ease, Linear, Hold) and −,
  Duplicate, Bring forward, Send backward, Delete.
- **Keys** while the editor is open (the board's own wait; Undo / Redo / Delete of a connector line go through): Space play, V H R E T I, ← → ↑ ↓
  nudge what is picked (Shift 10) or ← → the slide before / after, **Page Up / Down a frame (Shift: ten; the user, 2026-10-28)**, Shift + ← → a second, `,` / `.` or Ctrl + ← → a frame (1/30 s), U / P S R T E show properties (see "The timeline like After Effects'"),
  Home / End, J / K the keyframe before / after, [ / ] the picked layers start / end now (Alt: trimmed there), B / N the work area starts / ends now,
  Ctrl+A all the layers (Ctrl+Shift+A none), Ctrl+C / X / V, Ctrl+D duplicate, Ctrl+Shift+D split at the playhead, Ctrl+] / [ forward / backward
  (Shift: front / back), Delete (the keyframes picked first, else the layers), F9 easy ease, Ctrl+Alt+H hold, Ctrl + / − / 0, ? the shortcuts, Esc (the
  menu, the shortcuts, the keyframes picked, the tool, then the pick, then close). The **?** button in the top bar lists them all over the picture.
- **Like a video editing software (the user, 2026-10-26: "make the video editor, much more smoother with more features like a video editing software,
  like multiselection etc.").**
  - **Several picked at once.** The selection is the one pressed last, with all of them in `group` (`{type, id | frame, key, name, group: [...]}`; the
    inspector and the motion path are the one pressed last's, a move, a nudge, Delete, Copy… are all of them's). On the picture: **Shift + click** adds or
    takes out (a layer or an element of the slide); **a rectangle** drawn from the plate, the background, the gray side, an outline or outside the picture
    picks the layers it touches and the elements of the slide on it touches (not a backdrop nor the words: `assetsIn` of the player's api; Shift: adds);
    **Ctrl+A** all the layers. A press on one of several without dragging leaves that one alone (Figma). On the timeline: **Ctrl + click** on a name adds or
    takes out, **Shift + click** a run from the one picked last; the rows of the slides the same.
  - **What is done to them.** Dragged on the picture they all move (layers and elements of the slides, ONE step of Undo: `layerOps.changeMany` and
    `assetOps.placeMany` with the same `merge`); the arrows nudge them all (`assetOps.shiftMany`); several layers have the box round them with handles that
    **scale them all** from the side across (Shift: proportions; elements of the slides in the group: no handles); each one picked is outlined dashed.
    The sidebar shows **"N picked"** (`groupPanel` in Storyboard.jsx): **Align** (left, centers, right, top, middle, bottom: to the box round them all) and
    **Distribute** (3 or more: the same room between each), their opacity, Duplicate, Split at the playhead, Bring to front, Send to back, Hide / Show,
    Delete (`veActions`, filled by the editor). A single layer's inspector aligns it to the picture.
  - **Copy, cut, paste** (Ctrl+C / X / V, the right-click menu): the layers picked, kept for the page and in this browser (`storyboard:ve-clip`), so they
    paste into **any video of any board, at the playhead** (the first of them starts now, where they were on the picture). A picture in the clipboard is still
    added as an image layer.
  - **Split at the playhead** (Ctrl+Shift+D, After Effects' Split Layer): the layer ends there and its copy starts there just above it, with the same keyframes
    (`splitVideoLayer`). **[ / ]** move the picked layers so they start / end now; Alt+[ / Alt+] trim them there.
  - **The stacking**: drag a layer's name up or down the timeline (all the picked ones go together, a blue line shows where; `orderVideoLayers`), or
    Ctrl+] / Ctrl+[ (Shift: to the front / back) for all of them.
  - **Keyframes picked together**: Shift / Ctrl + click on the diamonds, or a rectangle drawn on an empty part of the layers' tracks (a click there still moves
    the playhead; the ruler and the rows of the slides still scrub). Dragged, they all move by the same time (`shiftKeys`; a row of the twirl moves only its
    properties); Delete takes them off (`removeKeys`); **F9** easy ease, the right-click menu Linear / Hold (`easeKeys`). Bars of several layers picked are
    dragged together too.
  - **The magnet** (the button by the guide lines, on by default, kept in this browser: `storyboard:ve-snap`): what is dragged on the picture is taken by the
    picture's sides and middle, the slide's guide lines and the other layers' sides and middles, a red line showing where (`snapBox`); on the timeline bars,
    trims and keyframes are taken by the playhead, the slides' starts, the work area and the other layers' ends and keyframes.
  - **A right-click menu** on the picture, a layer's row or bar, a keyframe: Cut, Copy, Paste, Duplicate, Split, Delete, the stacking, start / end at the
    playhead, Hide, Lock, Select all; on keyframes: Easy ease, Linear, Hold, Delete.
  - **The transport**: the **loop** (plays the work area round and round, or all of the video; `setLoop` of the player's api), **B / N** set the work area (a
    blue band on the ruler, its × takes it off), the **speed** (0.25× to 2×: `setRate`), a frame at a time, and **a time typed in** (click the time: 1:08.4 or
    68.4).
  - **Smoother.** The picture pans and zooms without drawing the editor again (`viewRef` moves it at once, the React `view` follows a moment after; while it
    moves it is on a layer of its own, `.sb-ve-comp.is-moving`); a mouse's wheel **glides** to its zoom, a trackpad follows the fingers; Fit, the zoom menu and
    Ctrl + / − / 0 ease there in 0.2 s. The video itself is not drawn again for a change of the editor's (`playerEl` is memoized), nor the rows of the slides
    (`slidesPart`); scrubbing hands the time to the inspector a few times a second, not every picture. The layer under the pointer is outlined before it is
    pressed (`hoverAt`).
- **The timeline like After Effects' (the user, 2026-10-28, with a picture of After Effects' timeline: "make all layers this way ... where I can move it, trim it, move frame by frame using page up and down. u to reveal all keyframes, have properties like opacity, effects and everything like after effects").** Not tested when it was written (the user's rule: nothing is tested until they say so).
  - **Bars.** Every row is a bar like After Effects': the layers in the color of their **label** (16 After Effects' colors, `LABELS`; a new layer has its kind's; click the little square left of the name, or right-click > Label color…, or the inspector's Blend and label; `layer.label`), a light top edge and a fine grain; the two ends show a notch to trim (drag them; the bar itself moves the layer and its keyframes in time, the playhead, the slides' starts and the other layers' ends taking it). **The elements of the slides are bars too** (Sea Foam, `SLIDE_LABEL`): they live on their slides, so they **cannot move in time**, but their two ends **trim** them: the element is not seen before / after (`edits.layers[title].spans[<its name>] = {from?, to?}`, `setSpan` / `spansOf` in `lib/storyboardLayers.mjs`, drawn by `drawSpans` in `components/storyboardLayers.js` each picture: `visibility: hidden !important` on the elements of that name outside the window; the faint outline stays where it was seen, "trim ×" on its row gives it the whole video back; a window that holds the whole row is no window). A window is kept with the video's layers (a change of a layer leaves it, `setLayers`).
  - **A frame at a time: Page Up / Page Down** (the user: "using page up and down"; Shift: ten), as `,` `.` and Ctrl + ← →; two buttons by the play button do the same (the slide before / after are ← → when nothing is picked, and their buttons). The time shows After Effects' timecode, **0;00;01;07** (hours; minutes; seconds; frames at 30 a second), and a time is typed in that way too (or 1:08.4, or 68.4).
  - **U reveals the keyframes.** **U** shows, under each layer picked (none picked: every layer), the properties that have a keyframe; pressed again it folds them. **U U** (twice, within 0.45 s) shows what is changed too (a turn, an opacity, every effect). With the **pointer over the timeline**, **P S R T E** show Position, Size, Rotation, Opacity and the Effects (Shift: adds to what is shown or takes it out; pressed again folds them); elsewhere R E T are still the tools, so a shape is drawn with R / E and a layer's rotation is shown with the pointer on the timeline. The twirl still shows everything. (`revealWith`, `pressU`, `KEY_GROUPS` in `components/StoryboardVideoEditor.jsx`; `animatedGroups`, `modifiedGroups`, `groupsOf` in the lib.)
  - **Properties and effects.** A layer's twirl shows Position, Size, Rotation, Opacity, then its **effects**: Gaussian Blur, Drop Shadow, Glow, Brightness, Contrast, Saturation, Hue Shift, Black & White, Sepia, Invert (`EFFECTS`; each is a CSS filter, applied in the order of the list: ↑ ↓ in the inspector). Add one with the **fx** button of the layer (or right-click > Add effect…, or the inspector's Effects +); an effect has its on / off eye, its name, ✕, and a row for each number with **the stopwatch, ◂ ◆ ▸ and keyframes like the transform's** (a keyframe holds `fx:<effect id>:<number>`, `fx:blur-1:radius`; `propsOf`, `FX_PROP`, `withBase`). A color (the shadow's, the glow's) is not animated. **Blend mode** (After Effects' modes: `mix-blend-mode`) is in the inspector (Blend and label). The inspector (Storyboard.jsx, `layerPanel`) has the same Effects section with the numbers and their ◆.
  - **From the command line** (an open page shows it within seconds): `vlayer-fx <video> <id> <blur|shadow|glow|brightness|contrast|saturation|hue|grayscale|sepia|invert> [number=v … color=#hex]`, `vlayer-fx-rm <video> <id> <effect id>`, `vlayer-key <video> <id> <s> fx:blur-1:radius=40` (a keyframe of an effect's number), `vlayer-set … blend=multiply label=lavender`, `vlayer-span <video> <element name> <from s|-> <to s|->` (`clear` gives the whole video back); `vlayers` lists effects, blend, label and keyframes.
- **A change in the editor does not freeze it (the user, 2026-10-27: "changing an option in video editor is super slow and tedious, like it freezes").** Each change used
  to draw again, behind the editor where nothing is seen, every slide of the video on the board (`FrameArt` builds the whole slide again: a look of an element goes to
  every slide of the video) and to make again every copy of the video on the lines of videos (the run's player and its shelves), then to measure the motion path of the
  element picked over the whole video again. Now:
  - **The board behind the editor is frozen** (`frozen`, `boardEdits`, `boardFocus` in Storyboard.jsx): its frames, its players and its cells keep the edits and the
    frame picked they had when the editor opened, and are drawn again once, when it closes. Only the frame picked in the editor follows the changes (the inspector
    reads its look from it). Do not hand the live `edits` to the board's players or frames while `vEdit` is open.
  - **The editor's video takes the changes as they settle** (`veEdits = useDeferredValue(...)`, `vePlayer`): the sidebar answers at once; a field scrubbed or a
    color dragged makes the video again with the latest value, not at every step of it.
  - **The motion path is measured again only once the changes stop** (0.7 s; the path it had stays meanwhile and is replaced when the new one is all there), and
    the timeline's rows of the slides are not drawn again when a change leaves them as they were (`rowsKept`).
- **Editing on the fly from the command line** (the page shows a change within seconds): `vlayers [video]`, `vlayer-add <video> <kind> k=v...`,
  `vlayer-set <video> <id> k=v... [--at s]`, `vlayer-key <video> <id> <s> [k=v...]`, `vlayer-unkey`, `vlayer-still`, `vlayer-order`, `vlayer-rm`
  (`node scripts/storyboard.mjs help`). For example, a yellow box that comes in from the left between 2 and 3 s on the Knowledge Base video:
  `vlayer-add "The tablet and its Knowledge Base" rect x=-400 y=600 w=360 h=120 from=2 to=8 fill=#ffec00`, then
  `vlayer-key "The tablet and its Knowledge Base" rect-1 2 x=-400` and `vlayer-key "The tablet and its Knowledge Base" rect-1 3 x=120`.
- `scripts/storyboard-text-qa.mjs` opens a video in the editor before it double-clicks a text (its picture put at 1:1, the window 1300 high).
- **Paths: the Pen, the points of a line, and the purple lines of the software as paths (the user, 2026-10-28: "make everything in video editor selectable even these purple
  lines, make them as a path that I can change the ends of it, like to a circle, arrows, change colors etc.. where each point is pointing, add a path tool to create a new one,
  change position of its points, delete them etc.. just like after effects").** Written and **not tested yet** (the user's rule: nothing is run until they say so): look at it first.
  - **A new kind of layer, `path`** (`lib/storyboardLayers.mjs`; geometry in `lib/storyboardPaths.mjs`, tests in `lib/storyboardPaths.test.mjs`): `points` kept in the layer's own
    frame (its box, turned about its middle; a box made bigger by its handles scales its points: `pw` `ph` are the size they were made at), each `{x, y, ix, iy, ox, oy, smooth}`
    (`ix iy` / `ox oy`: the handles in / out as offsets; none = a corner; `smooth` keeps the two in one line). `closed`, `stroke`, `strokeWidth`, `lineCap`, `lineJoin`,
    `dash` (solid, dashed, dotted), `fill` (a closed path's, a color), **`startCap` / `endCap`** (none, arrow, triangle, dot, square, diamond, bar; `capScale`; a solid head stops the
    line at its base) and **Trim Paths**: `trimStart` / `trimEnd` (0 to 1) are animated like Position (stopwatch, ◆, the group "Trim Paths" on the timeline, U shows it when changed).
  - **The Pen (P; over the timeline P is Position)**: a click is a corner, a click and a drag a smooth point (its handles pulled out), a click on the first point closes the path,
    Enter / Esc / a double-click / another tool ends it (Backspace: the last point; Shift: 45°). It is a new layer, purple 4 px, on screen to the end of its line.
  - **Points** (the path picked, Selection tool; `components/StoryboardPathEditor.jsx`, drawn over the picture, nothing of it is in the layer's file): a press on a point picks it
    (Shift: more) and a drag moves all that are picked; the handles of a picked point bend the line (Alt: one handle alone); **Alt + press** makes a point smooth or a corner;
    **Delete** takes the points picked off (a path left with fewer than two goes); **a double-click on the line** adds a point there (the curve kept: de Casteljau). The frame is fitted
    to the points again after each change (`refitPath`, the path staying exactly where it is on the picture, turned or not), except when its place, size or turn has keyframes.
    The inspector has Line (color, width, dash, cap, join), Ends (start / end shape, size, swap), Trim Paths, Points (closed, fill, all smooth, all corners, reverse).
  - **The software's purple lines** (the rating's connector `.iq2-line`, the sections' `svg.sec-flow-lines` / `svg.sec-rk-arrows`): **a click on one** (5 px near; Ctrl+click is the
    deepest element as before) turns it into path layers (`api.pick` returns `{type: 'connector'}`, `convertConnector` in the editor, `connectorGeometry` in
    `components/storyboardLayers.js`): the pieces that meet are chained into trails (`chainSegments`: a trail stops where three meet), each a path layer with the line's own color,
    width and arrow head, from when the slide is all there to the end of its line (chapter); the video goes to where the slide is all there first if the playhead was held while it moved
    in. The rating's connector that the video draws out of the mission (`peerFlow` `'draw'`) keeps that drawing: its paths get Trim End keyframes (0.15 s after the slide is in, 1.5 s,
    the mission first). The software's lines are not seen while such a path is on (`replaces` = their names, `connectorName`; `hideReplaced` each picture): **Delete gives them back**,
    the eye hides the path and the lines both. One step of Undo.
  - **Command line**: `vlayer-add <video> path points="100,100;400,100;400,300" endCap=arrow strokeWidth=4`, `vlayer-set <video> path-1 points=… startCap=circle dash=dashed`,
    `vlayer-key <video> path-1 1.5 trimEnd=0` then `… 3 trimEnd=1` (the line drawn in). Points are on the picture; `help` says the rest.

## The video's text editor (the user, 2026-10-14)

"Work on a video editor so that I can double click any text on any video and I should be able to change the text size, font, content, position, boundaries on the fly." **Double-click any text in any video** (the runs' videos, the Animations line's cells, both boards): the video stops on that frame, all there, the text takes the keyboard (type to change the words, as before: Ctrl+Enter or **Done** keeps, Esc drops the words) and the editor comes with it:

- **The box**: a blue frame round the text's box with **8 handles** (drag to change the boundaries: the sides a handle is on move, the others stay; a corner pulls two; at least 40 x 20 px) and a **grip** above it (and the frame's edges) to **move** it (Shift: along one line). A chat box is as tall as its words until a top or bottom side is pulled (then it has a height of its own; `H` empty is auto again).
- **The fields** (since 2026-10-21 in the sidebar, the video's inspector: `TextFields`; the user: "as an inspector on the right instead of popping up"; before, a toolbar floated over the video): the font, the size, the weight, the color, the alignment, the box's X Y W H, Reset and Done. Pressing them does not end the editing (the sidebar's Text is `data-sb-te`, like the box). **Since 2026-10-21 the look is this video's words' and goes to every video only with the inspector's Apply to all** (the Done = all videos / Make unique below is how it was before).
- It works on **any text**: the words of the chat box, a title, and a text of the software itself (a menu item of the tablet: its color, size, place; nested texts are addressed by their path, `layer/1.2`). A press anywhere else ends the editing (and does not play the video).

**What is kept, and where.** The words are the board's own (`texts`, as before: retyping in the video retypes the slide). **The look and the box are the VIDEO's only**: `frames[id].vtext[boxKey]` = `{font, fontSize, weight, color, align, lineHeight, letterSpacing, rect: [x, y, w, h]}` (`lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`: `setVideoText`, `mergeVideoText`, `cleanVideoText`, and `normalizeEdits`, so it is in the saved file, the export / import and the backups; it is not one of the `CHANGES`: a slide whose video text only is changed is not an "edited" slide, and the slide on the board looks the same). `boxKey` is the key of the text's box (a chat box's layer, else the element that holds the text). `rect` is the box's top left and size in stage px (h 0: as tall as its words) and is **absolute**: where the user put it, whatever the layout under it does. A text that has a look but no `rect` stays where the video laid it out, around the middle of its box (a bigger size grows it evenly). `setVideoText` gives the same edits back when nothing changes (no step of Undo). A drag, a run of the stepper's clicks, a typed number are **one step of Undo** each (`restyleVideo` in `Storyboard.jsx`: `commit` with a `merge` key per text and field).

**Done is for ALL the videos, Make unique is for one text (the user, 2026-10-14: "if I double click the text and change the font size and everything, it should change in the whole video and in all the videos, unless I press Done and make it unique, next to Done another button").** For the **words** (a chat box) the toolbar has **Reset · Make unique · Done**. What was changed in the editing (font, size, weight, color, align, line height, letter spacing: the *look*, never the place or the box, which are a text's own) is, with **Done** (or Ctrl+Enter, Escape, a press elsewhere, Enter in the text), the look of ALL the words of ALL the videos: `edits.videoText.words` (`setVideoTextKind`, `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`; one record, with an `at` that says which copy is newer when two are merged), taken off the texts it was typed on, and put in the OTHER board's file too (`changeBoard` in `components/Storyboard.jsx`, as the Trash is). **Make unique** ends the editing with the change on this text alone (its `vtext`, on top of the shared look). The engine puts the shared look under every words box of a slide and the text's own `vtext` on top (`vtextOf`; a slide is drawn again when it changes, `slot.shared`). **Reset** puts this text back to the shared look (the video's own Montserrat 35 where there is none). Titles and the software's own texts have only Done and are always the text's own. The whole editing, its clicks and Done, is ONE step of Undo (`session`). **A number typed in a field is kept when Done is pressed**: pressing a button does not take the keyboard from the field, so the field never saw the end of the typing and the change was only shown (the video went back to what it was; `finishRef` in `StoryboardTextEditor.jsx` leaves the field first).

**How the video plays it.** `applyVideoText` (`components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`) puts the frame's `vtext` on the text **at the end of `layoutSlot`**, after the video's own layout (it has the last word over the video's Montserrat 35, `setWordsFont`, and the words spread over the right side, `spreadWords`): the look as the sidebar's own variables (`--sb-font`, `--sb-font-size`, ...: only what the text was given), the box with `sizeTo` (a chat box) or a width and height (any other text), then the box is moved so its top left is at `rect`. What it sets is saved first (`slot.vundo`: the style properties and data attributes it touches) and put back at the start of the next layout, so what is taken off comes back as it was. The fonts it uses are loaded on demand (`fontsOf` in `build`). **A caption that several slides show one after the other** (a scene's click after click) is changed **in all of them**, its look (`vtext`) and its words (`texts`), so it does not jump when the next click plays and the words typed are the words that play. `info.sameWords` (from `directorOf`) says which: the slides **joined** by the same chat box (the director shows one slide's chat box in the others, `carryLayer`, with the edits of the slide it came from: that **owner** (`x.owners`) is one of them, and it may be a slide the video does not even play: what is typed in a slide that only carries the words is overridden by the owner's, so the owner is where the words have to be kept), or one after the other in one scene with the same words (their chat boxes may have other keys: each gets its own). It is a union of those links (a group is every slide connected by any of them), so the first slide of a scene whose box has another key (the Knowledge Base's `words`, then `fading`) is in it too. The editor sends every one as a target (`onVideoText({targets, patch})`, and `onText({..., also})` for the words). The other texts are changed in the frame that is on.

**The engine's two calls** (`createPlayerEngine`): `textBox(key)` (the box as it is: `rect`, `look`, what the video gave it, `auto`) and `liveText(key, patch, after)` (a change put on the text at once, kept on the frame being shown, `slot.vlive`, until the board hands the frame down again with the saved edits). The editor (`components/StoryboardTextEditor.jsx`, geometry in `lib/storyboardTextEdit.mjs`, tested) asks `textBox` every picture, so the frame follows typing, a font that comes in, the board's pan and zoom. CSS: `.sb-te-*` in `Storyboard.css`; the editor is drawn inside the board's world (the handles keep their size on the screen: `--inv`, the zoom inverted) and the toolbar in a portal.

**Things that were found the hard way**: (1) the **keyboard leaves the text before a press on a handle is seen** (in headless Chrome the blur comes before the `pointerdown`), so a blur is judged a tick later (`ed.onBlur` in `StoryboardPlayer.jsx`: not while a handle is held, not while the keyboard is in the toolbar) and the text is given the keyboard back (`focusText`); (2) the non-cell player's screen has **its own stacking context** now (`.sb-pl-stage > .sb-pl-screen { z-index: 0 }`, as a cell's): the frames drawn in it have z-indexes in the tens and covered the editor's handles (compared with and without it, 10 pictures of 3 videos: the same pixels); (3) the + / − buttons read the text **as it is this moment** (`read()`), not what was last drawn, or quick clicks send the same number twice; (4) **retyping the words of a captured caption can move the director** (it drops the slides whose words are only the start of the next one's, `isTypingStep`, and shows a chat box from the slide it came from): the words are therefore typed in **every slide of the group and in the owner**, else the video went on saying the old words from the slide that was not changed (found by retyping in 7 videos: 6 held, the Knowledge Base's first caption did not until the groups were joined as above); (5) the board's sidebar covers the right of the screen when the board is zoomed so the video goes under it: a handle under it cannot be pressed (pan the video out from under it).

**Looking at it again** (the site running; the page's saves are blocked, nothing is written): a double-click, the toolbar, a drag, the saved payload, Undo, a cell video, a title, a text of the tablet, a `vtext` loaded from the file, the words of a caption several slides show: `node scripts/storyboard-text-qa.mjs [A,B,C,D,E,F,G,H]` (it exits 1 if a check fails).

## The video's inspector and the colors of every video (the user, 2026-10-21)

"Add also a way to edit everything like the colors of background, and it should apply to all of the videos in all the storyboard and all slides unless I press undo or I press make
unique and apply just like text." Then: "I would love to have it as an inspector on the right instead of popping up. Same thing for the text and everything. So basically, if I select a video,
it should show everything on the inspector when it comes to editing. But when it comes to the content itself, I can just double click and change the text on the video itself."

**Picking a video.** A press on a video (its picture, its bar, a cell of a line of videos) picks it (`onSelect`, `selectVideo` in `components/Storyboard.jsx`); a press on the board leaves
it. The sidebar is then the video's inspector (`videoPanel`).

**This video first, then Apply to all** (the user, 2026-10-21: "make sure it doesn't apply to all until I hit an apply to all button blue down in inspector, but first it should apply to only
the video I have"). Everything the inspector changes stays on THIS video: on its slides, `frames[id].ownLooks['kind:<kind>']` (`changeVideoLook`, once a picture, one step of Undo per
field). The slides it shares with its twins (the detailed lines play the same slides) show it too.
- **Words**: the font, weight, size, alignment and color of this video's words (`ownLooks['kind:words']`, over the shared `videoText.words`: `vtextOf` in
  `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`).
- **While a text is retyped** (double-click it in the video): its fields instead (`TextFields`, `components/StoryboardTextEditor.jsx`).
  - Its look (font, weight, size, alignment, color) is this video's words'; it is taken off the text itself, so the text shows it.
  - Its box's X Y W H is the text's own (`vtext`). The box with its handles stays ON the video, and the words are typed in the video.
  - Reset: this text and this video's words as the videos have them. Done (or a press elsewhere) ends it.
- **Background, Plate, Gray side, Yellow outline** (`LOOK_PARTS`): the paint picker (the outline: its color and thickness). The minus in a section's header puts this video back to how
  all the videos have it (`resetVideoLook`).
- **Apply to all**: the blue button kept at the sidebar's bottom (`applyVideoToAll`).
  - This video's looks become the shared ones, on every video and slide of every board: the colors go to `looks`, the words to `videoText.words`.
  - The video then follows them, its own taken off. One step of Undo.
  - It is off while nothing was changed on the video.
- **A double-click on a color** in a video (not a text: `lookTargetAt`) picks the video with that color's section lit and brought into view.
- **Connector lines** (the user, 2026-10-21: "make the purple line editable with changing arrow head and colors"): on a video that has them (`svg.sec-flow-lines`, `svg.sec-rk-arrows`), their color, thickness and arrow head (Arrow, Triangle, Dot, None: `ARROW_HEADS`, `HEADS` in `components/storyboardDom.js`, which rewrites the svg's `<marker>`). A look kind like the others (`connector`), so This video first, then Apply to all. **Deleting a line** (the user, 2026-10-22: "a way to cancel any line I want by selecting and deleting"): double-click it in the video, then Delete (the key, or the section's button). It is gone from this video's slides (`hide` in its own connector look, the lines named by `lineName`: their `data-iq-target`, else their place `#n` in the drawing). It is never shared by Apply to all. Bring back the lines shows them again.

**The gradients, the way Figma does them** (the user, 2026-10-21: "where you see just the gradient itself with dots ... where you can just change from a color wheel"): `PaintField`
(`components/StoryboardPanel.jsx`), in the inspector and everywhere in the sidebar (a frame's Background, an asset's Fill, the device color).
- **Solid / Linear / Radial** at the top.
- The gradient itself as a **bar with a dot for each color** (`GradientBar`):
  - drag a dot along the bar to move it;
  - click the bar to add a color there, in the color the gradient has at that point;
  - drag a dot away from the bar, or press −, to take it off (two stay; eight at most);
  - the **color wheel** under it changes the picked dot (or the plain color).
- A **dial** for a linear gradient's angle (Shift: steps of 15°), then the texture and the ready ones in a compact row.
- Pure parts: `moveStop`, `insertStop`, `colorAtStop`, and `paintFromCss` (a captured plate's CSS gradient made a paint, for the inspector to start from) in `lib/storyboardPaint.mjs`.

**What is kept, and where** (`lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`):
- **The shared look** is `edits.looks[kind]`: `{fill, at}` for background, plate and panel; `{stroke, strokeWidth, at}` for outline (`LOOK_KINDS`, `setSharedLook`).
  - It is in every board's file.
  - The page copies it to the other boards 0.8 s after it last changed here, with the look of the video words (`setSharedLooks`, `setVideoTextAll`; `looksSent` in
    `components/Storyboard.jsx`). That covers the inspector, Undo and Redo, but not a drag at every move.
  - What comes from the board's own file is not sent on. Two copies merge by `at`, the newer one winning.
- **A part's own look** is in `frames[id].ownLooks`:
  - `'kind:<kind>'`: This video;
  - a layer's key: the sidebar. A part restyled in the sidebar over a shared look keeps what it was given there; so does a slide's background (`BACKGROUND`, `'@background'`).

**How it is drawn.** `applyLooks` is called at the end of `applyEdits`, so it goes over the slide's own styles; the split design's metal plates follow it too. On each part it
puts the part's own look, else its kind's own on that slide, else the shared one. The kinds are found the same way everywhere: a layer root keyed `plate` or `panel` (`-2`…), and
`.iq2-mark` anywhere. The board's slides (`FrameArt`, the ghost of a duplicate) and every video (`build` in `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`; a slot is drawn again when
`edits.looks` changes) call it. The engine's `kindLook(kind)` says what a kind shows on the frame a video is on: the color a field starts from when no look is set.

**Note:** it is on every slide, the Scratch and Fonts lines too (the user asked for "all slides"). Zoomed far out, the board shows the captured pictures until a slide is
drawn live.

## The Animations line (the user, 2026-10-12)

"Add a line on top called animations where it's just different animation like cursor click on something, highlight, handwritten, zoom in, fade in with scale in, fadeout, etc. All animation we already did, make sure it looks amazing." The first line of Improved Quality is **Animations** (above Fonts and Scratch): one **video** for each animation the videos play, in the board's own look (the user, 2026-10-12: "make them videos not slides"), and **the reel of all of them** at the right of the last one (the first run of `VIDEO_RUNS`: about 1 min 15 s). A video on the board is a player in place of its slides: a purple **VIDEO** header with the animation's name, a poster (the picture it settles on), a big play button and a control bar; click it to play it. Its note under it says how it moves, with the numbers the player uses. Every slide of it still has a **name** ("Cursor click · first click: translated") and is a frame of the line.

Scenes, in order: fade in with scale (a title, small and faint to full size) · fade out (a title growing as it goes) · handwritten (the pen) · panel wipe (the software comes in, the dark panel wipes across, the tablet glides left) · cursor click (it arrives, hovers, presses, the screen changes; twice) · highlight (the outline after the art) · zoom in (the board pushed in on the closed missions, then out) · pop up (a mission lifted out of its board) · expand (a card opens into its details) · scroll (a list moves under a swipe) · drag (the rating knob) · glide (a card goes to the top, the others slide) · color change (a timer turns orange, then red) · claim and close (a mission goes Open, Claimed, Closed) · menu, drawer, video (the burger menu, the Knowledge Base, Onboarding, a video playing with its timeline).

**Videos, not slides** (`lib/storyboardCells.mjs`, pure and tested; the page draws a video with `<StoryboardPlayer cell>`): a line can be a **line of videos**: `lines.added[id].videos` is `[{id, name, ids}]`, each video made of the slides `ids` (frames of the line, in order). While the line is shown as videos, the board puts each video where its first slide is (its **anchor**): the header says its name and VIDEO, the note under it is the video's, a player takes the place of the picture; the other slides are not on the board (`cellsOf(...).hidden`) but are still frames of the line: the video plays them, the command line shows them, the line shown as slides has them. With the line picked by its label, the sidebar's **Show: Videos | Slides** switches (the slides come back together, each video's one after the other, and going back to videos keeps that order); the command line does the same: `node scripts/storyboard.mjs line-view animations slides`. **A video is one thing**: dragging it by its number bar reorders the videos; dragged to another line it takes its slides with it (`followersOf`, the last argument of `moveFrame`) and plays in that line's video; Delete and Ctrl+X take all its slides to the Trash, Ctrl+C copies them all (pasted as frames), Ctrl+D duplicates the video (every slide, with its changes, note and name, as a video right after it: `duplicateVideo`); a line duplicated is a line of videos too. The command line works on slides (a video's first slide is the video: `to-line` moves it with its slides).

How it is made: **the frames are duplicates of the board's own frames** (a scene of the video, so it plays exactly as it does there) with the words retyped (and the pen on them), or frames of the other board pasted as frames of assets (glide, color change, claim and close); `lib/storyboardAnimations.mjs` (pure, tested: `SCENES` is the list, `NOTE` the notes) and `scripts/storyboard-animations.mjs` (reads both boards, records the videos with the line, saves like the page does, a backup first):

```
node scripts/storyboard-animations.mjs --dry   # what it would make, nothing saved
node scripts/storyboard-animations.mjs         # the line again from the scenes in the lib
```

**Running it again makes the line anew** (its frames are removed and made again, the rest of the board is not touched), so changes made by hand to the line are lost: change the line with `scripts/storyboard.mjs` (`text`, `move`, `name`, `note`…) and run the script again only when the scenes themselves change in `lib/storyboardAnimations.mjs`. Make the numbers of a note match the player's when those change (`components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`). The video's director plays a duplicate of a slide by the rules of the scene it was captured in (`sceneOf`), so a duplicate of a click is still a click.

**The videos of a board in ONE line (the user, 2026-10-13: "take all videos and put them in one line, the videos starting from The tablet and its Knowledge Base").** Each video of a board (a run of lines, `VIDEO_RUNS` in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`) stands at the right of the last frame of its last line, which scatters them over twenty rows. A line you added can hold them instead: its record has a **`shelf`**, the ids of the runs whose video stands in its row, side by side in that order (`shelvedRuns`, pure and tested; `setLineShelf` in `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`; `videoSpots` in `components/Storyboard.jsx` puts each player at `x = labelW + labelGap + place * (frame + gap)`, in the row of the line, and the line has no "Add a frame" while it holds videos). A shelved video is **no copy**: it plays the runs' own lines, so an edit to a slide shows in it, and it is no longer at the end of its lines (it is in one place only). Improved Quality has one, the line **Videos** (`line-3`, right under Animations; drag its label to put it elsewhere): The tablet and its Knowledge Base | The mission: instructions to video proof | The tablet, the mission, the ratings | What managers see | The web report (the Animations video, the reel, stays at the end of its own line, and the Increased Efficiency board has its own four, not shelved). Made with the command line:

```
node scripts/storyboard.mjs runs                       # the videos of the board: id, title, lines, the line that holds it
node scripts/storyboard.mjs line-add "Videos" --after animations
node scripts/storyboard.mjs line-shelf line-3 knowledge mission ratings managers report    # their ids or the start of their titles, in the order they stand in
node scripts/storyboard.mjs line-shelf line-3 none     # each video back at the right of its own last line
```

**The detailed videos (the user, 2026-10-14: "add another line of videos under videos where it will have the same videos but citing all details and always having that gray area to the right, the details are like 'user clicks on menu' when the cursor is going to click on the menu, then 'he will find different options, and the knowledge base is...'"; on the first try: "the wording is not what I want, it doesn't say what we had, sometimes it gets bold, it's fast, and too long, should be short, and let the user read, and have same wording as we had just additional steps").** Under the line **Videos**, each board has the line **Videos in detail** (`line-4` on Improved Quality, `line-2` on Increased Efficiency): a **twin** of each video that has steps to add, playing the same lines (so slide edits show in it), with the same cursor, clicks, lesson, scroll and highlights, and **the gray area on every slide** (a slide that had it parked, the tablet alone in the middle while the cursor clicks through the Menu, has it back: the tablet is at its left as everywhere). **The words the video had stay as they were, where they were** (the scene's own words, and each time the scene changes them: "As Teams Work Information Is Readily Available" ... "Knowledge Is Readily Accessible"; `x.ownText` is what a slide says before the gaps are filled), and the twin **adds steps**, short captions of what the user does and finds, at the beats of a scene: each time the cursor goes to something ("The user taps Menu."), each result of a click ("Different options appear, including the Knowledge Base.", "It holds many media missions."), and, in the scenes of `each`, each slide where the software changes (a number pad opens, a mission moves: `screenOf` differs from the slide before). The steps are in **`lib/storyboardDetails.mjs`** (`DETAILS[board][video] = {scenes: {scene: [steps in order, the last stays]}, each: [scenes]}`: a few words, the user in the third person; a video with no step has no twin: What it shows). **Slow enough to read**: the twin's pen is 16 letters a second (`DETAIL_PEN`) and a caption stays 1.3 s once it is written (`DETAIL_READ`, `profile.detail.read`, the `tail` of the pen in `timelineOf`); the videos run longer than their originals (the mission 89 s). The twins are made from the list: `DETAIL_RUNS` / `allRunsOf` in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs` (id `<video>-detail`, title `<title> · in detail`, `like`), their profile is the video's own **without its pace** (no `tempo`, `natural`, `rest`, `beats`, `scenes`) and `detail: {run, scenes, each, read}`; `detailBeats` in `directorOf` puts the words on the slides (as `texts` of the chat box, and `info.caption = {run, beat, text}`). A twin stands only where it is put (a shelf: `runsOf(board, lines, true)` has them with `twin: true`, `videoSpots` skips an unshelved one). **A caption is retyped in the video itself** (double-click it, as any text): the words are kept as `edits.captions[<video>-detail][beat]` (`setCaption`, `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`; the beat is `scene:n` for step n, `scene:o<n>` for the scene's n-th own caption), over the list; the slides and the video it follows are not touched. The look of the words is the shared look of all the words as for every video. Made with the command line: `node scripts/storyboard.mjs line-add "Videos in detail" --after line-3`, `line-shelf line-4 knowledge-detail mission-detail ratings-detail managers-detail report-detail` (`runs` lists the ids). Looking again: `scripts/storyboard-text-qa.mjs L`; `window.__sbScripts['<title>']` has each item's `text` and `id`.

**Two more lines of the detailed videos: the cursor that points (the user, 2026-10-15: "a new line of videos like the videos with details but no yellow highlight, only the cursor visible all the time pointing on things we are talking about, on the bottom right of where the highlight rectangle was"; "another line where we have the yellow highlight with the cursor at the same time when we highlight"). WRITTEN, NOT TESTED YET.** Under **Videos in detail** each board has **Videos in detail · cursor only** and **Videos in detail · highlight and cursor** (`line-5` / `line-6` on Improved Quality, `line-3` / `line-4` on Increased Efficiency): the same videos as the detailed ones (the video's own words plus the short steps, slow, the gray area on every slide), as two more variants of each (`DETAIL_VARIANTS` in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`: ids `<video>-detail-cursor` and `<video>-detail-both`, titles `<title> · in detail, cursor only` / `· in detail, highlight and cursor`; the profile has `pointer: {marks: false}` / `{marks: true}`; the captions are the detailed video's, `detail.run` is shared, so the words typed over one are the words of the three). `pointBeats` in `directorOf` gives every slide with software a cursor: where the demo's own cursor is (a click, a drag) it is as it was, but it does not fade after its click (`clickOut`, `handGone`, `handOut`, `endClick` are off), and a slide that only shows something has the **pointer** (`lib/storyboardPointer.mjs`: the demo's hand picture as a layer of its own, `POINTER_LAYER`, an asset named `sb-pointer:hand` that the director and the engine know like the lesson's) with its fingertip at the **bottom right corner of the slide's highlight rectangle** (`cornerOf`, `POINT`: 10 px in, 6 up; two highlights: the corner of both), put by slide coordinates (`pointerMove`) so the fitting of the art to the video moves it as it moves the demo's; a slide with no highlight keeps the cursor where the one before left it, the first slides before any highlight have it low in the picture (70 % across, 78 % down the art); words alone (a title) have none. **Cursor only:** the highlight layers of every slide are taken away. **Highlight and cursor:** they stay, and come in as the cursor gets there (`lateLead` 0: the highlight does not wait 0.25 s after the move). Made with `line-add` / `line-shelf` (ids in `runs`). The Increased Efficiency board has no cursor of its own: its pointer is this layer. To look at them: play them from their lines; `scripts/storyboard-qa.mjs cuts|marks --line "cursor"`; unit tests `lib/storyboardVideos.test.mjs`.

**A fourth variant: the cursor and a pop-up (the user, 2026-10-19: "add a different line for the videos where we will be highlighting using cursor but with a small popup of the area we are highlighting and slight dimming of other things").** `DETAIL_VARIANTS` has `{suffix: '-popup', title: ', cursor and pop-up', pointer: {marks: true, popup: true}}` (ids `<video>-detail-popup`, the detailed video's captions). The cursor points as in **highlight and cursor**; the yellow outline is the border of a **card** (since the CEO's review of 2026-10-16: "do cursor, do highlight and pop") that holds a copy of the software under it (each art piece cloned at the very place and size it is seen, cut to the outline's box, its names taken off: `unnamed`), so the card is the same picture, lifted. Being inside the outline, the card fades and glides exactly when the outline does; by the clock (`spotsAt`, each picture) it grows to `POP.grow` (1.05, less where it would leave the picture or run into the words) and rises `POP.lift` (4 px) as it comes in. A **shade** (`.sb-spot-shade`, z-index 9: over the software, the plate and the gray side, under the outlines, the words and the cursor) dims the rest by `POP.dim` (30 %) times the most a card is in. A card that leaves at a move is copied over the new slide and fades there (`ghostOf`, quickly, as the screen starts to move), so it never drops behind the new software (the outlines of the other lines still do: the jump at 29.5 s of Precision Planning's "How a mission is scheduled"). Built at the end of a layout (`popMarks`); styles at "the video cursor and pop-up" in `components/Storyboard.css`. **The cursor in it** (the user, 2026-10-19): always over the cards (`.sb-popping .dh-hand`, z-index 13); it glides from one thing to the next and never fades between two (the fade out / in it first had was taken off the same day: "don't fade out the cursor between two things, it should just move to the next place, pop, then so on"); a cursor that comes in fades in where it is (no glide from the words). **On the boards** (the user, 2026-10-19: "it should be on all storyboards videos"): every board has the line "Videos in detail · cursor and pop-up" right under "· highlight and cursor", holding the same videos (`<chunk>-detail-popup`): Improved Quality `line-7`, Live Oversight `line-6`, the others `line-5`. A new board gets it with `line-add "Videos in detail · cursor and pop-up" --after <its highlight and cursor line>` and `line-shelf <line> <chunk>-detail-popup …`.

**Words written by the pen are as heavy as the page's own (the user, 2026-10-14: "sometimes for some reason it gets bold").** The pen shows its letters from a canvas, the page sets the same font and weight with about a quarter more ink (measured on Montserrat semibold, white on dark: 116,619 against 146,054), so the words jumped to bold the moment the pen lifted. The letters the pen shows are outlined by `FAT` (3 % of their size) in `components/storyboardHandwriting.js`: 148,691 against 146,054 now. Applies to every video's words.

**The pen writes during the move (the user, 2026-10-14, found with the detailed ratings video: the caption of the rating's 3 s drag appeared whole at the end of the move).** The pen touches as soon as the old words are gone (`penStart`: half the move + 0.05 s), which is part way through a move; the engine only wrote in held frames (a move showed its words as "before"), so a pen that was due to touch at 0.3 s of a 0.5 s move came in with 0.2 s of letters already written, and a 3 s move brought in the whole caption at once. `writeDuringMove` (`components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`) writes during the move too.

**Increased Efficiency follows the Improved Quality videos (the user, 2026-10-13: "update the increase efficiency, slides + videos, put videos on a line and fix the slides to follow improve quality videos that we made"; asked which parts: "same beat per scene", then "same look too pls").** (1) **Its four videos are in one line**, **Videos**, at the top of the board (`line-1`; `line-shelf line-1 board tablet results assign --video increased-efficiency-v2`, see above). (2) **The same beat in every scene that has a highlight** (`PROFILES['increased-efficiency-v2']`, the same `beats` as the Improved Quality mission's, `BEAT` in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`): once the move into the scene is over the highlight comes in (0.35 s), THEN the words are written, together 3 s (the pen's speed fits each caption), then 0.7 s held; schedule, points, urgency, priority | shared tablet, personal code | execution, performers, reminder | individuals, teams, initiative. Each scene is its move (0.5 s) + 3.7 s; the first slide of a video, whose art is still coming in (0.8 s), counts that as its move (4.5 s). The beat is put on the slide that WRITES the scene's words and has its highlight (`beatsOf`: the first slide of the scene whose words are new and that has an outline), not on the scene's last slide: the other slides of a scene (the second outline of Reward points, the end of a chapter, the board's own activity in "Teams take initiative") keep the time they had. Claim and close are actions, with no highlight: as they were. (3) **A yellow outline around one line of text has the text in its middle** (`centerMarks: true` in each of its four profiles, as the web report's). (4) **The look checks of Improved Quality were run on the four videos** (`node scripts/storyboard-qa.mjs cuts|marks --video increased-efficiency-v2`): no jumps; one real defect, **the outline round a whole tablet was cut by the picture's left edge** (the tablet sits against it: "On the shared tablet", "Who does what"; 36 and 134 samples with an outline covered or off the picture), so a profile can say **`marksInside: 14`** (`keepMarksIn` in `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`, put on the four Efficiency profiles; Improved Quality's approved videos are not touched): each side of an outline that comes closer than 14 stage px to the picture's edge is brought in (the outline is smaller on that side, nothing else moves; a layout again starts from the outline as it was, `data-sb-trim`). After it: 0 and 1 samples. What is left in `marks` (a few single samples exactly at the start of a move: an old outline under the new screen for one picture) is the same in Improved Quality (8 samples), the way the check measures a move's first picture. (5) The slides themselves are the same design as Improved Quality's (the split design, the chat boxes 300 wide, centered vertically, no tails), which the board already had; nothing on them was changed.

**The words in every video are Montserrat, 35 px (the user, 2026-10-14: "on the videos fix the font to be monsterrat 35 size").** They were the slides' handwriting face (Caveat, 35 on the slides) made 45 px by the video. Now `WORDS` in `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js` is `{size: 35, font: 'montserrat'}` and `setWordsFont` (called first in `layoutSlot`) puts the font and the size on **every chat box of words of a slide in a video**, whether the words are spread over the words' side or not (`--sb-font` / `--sb-font-size`, the same variables the sidebar sets): the slides on the board keep their own face, the pen writes along the Montserrat letters, and the lines wrap to the same one width (380) as before (a slide's own line breaks are re-flowed there: "Alerts Can Be / Included To Warn / About Risks"). Titles (the opening and closing words, `role title`) are not captions and are as they were. Applies to both boards and to the Animations line's videos too (its "Handwritten" demo now writes in Montserrat as well). **Cost:** captions of three Montserrat lines make a taller canvas for the pen than two lines of handwriting, and the pen redraws it every picture; in a real-time play of "The tablet and its Knowledge Base" the picture cost went from 11.7 ms (the old face) to 13.1 ms (p95 17.0 to 19.0), so the pen in a video draws at 2 canvas px per stage px (`PEN_K`; the board's own play button keeps 3: `prepareWriting(stage, bubble, maxK)`), which is crisp at the video's size and brought it to 12.2 ms (p95 17.2; pictures over 33 ms 6, it was 12).

The user designed one frame (the line **Scratch**, frame 3, id `knowledge/bubble#3`) and asked for the whole
storyboard to follow it ("same background, same text instead of a chat box, two half backgrounds with the
tablet on its half ... redo everything except the scratch line on top, don't change anything there"). What the
model is, and so what every frame has now:

- **Two halves**: the frame's own background is a dark metal gradient (the right half); over its left part
  (to x ≈ 1031) a **plate** (a rectangle named *Plate*, -238.7, -29.5, 1269.9 × 958.9, gradient #656a73 → #9ea4b0,
  stacked at the back) holds the frame's art.
- **The art** (the tablet, the mission, the report, the cards: what the frame's main layers paint) fits the model's tablet
  slot (the tablet's body, x 44.6, y 45.5, 1128 × 809, middle at y 450); art narrower than the slot is centered on the
  plate (x 515.6), wider art sits as the tablet does. One scale for a whole line (the biggest that lets every frame of the
  line fit, 0.3 to 1.35), each frame centered; a **partial** frame (art under 55% of the line's biggest: a card still
  alone) keeps the place of the next full frame, so what stays on screen does not jump. Hands, ripples, outlines and
  other layers over the art move and scale with it.
- **The words** are no chat box: the model's look (Caveat, 34.99 px, white, fill `transparent`, outline width 0, no tail,
  width as it was, 300), the middle of the words at (1370.3, 450). A frame of only words (a title) has the same
  background, **no plate**, its words white and in the font they always had (Poppins bold 700, the demos' own title font, at the demo's size: 62 px; the user, 2026-10-08: "make the conclusion and title font the font we had before instead of handwriting"; the title and closing-words frames of both boards, and the four `c1-title` frames of the first line: knowledge 1, 2, 16, 17).
- **The iPad** is the model's: a flat black frame, its screen scaled 1.03 (a thinner frame), the column names
  (*Open - 3 ...*) 10% bigger, a drop shadow on every mission chip.
- The line named **Scratch** is never touched (`--skip`, default *Scratch, Fonts, Animations*).

`node scripts/storyboard-split.mjs` does it (the site must be running: it measures every frame in a headless browser;
`lib/storyboardSplit.mjs` is the pure part, with tests; `scripts/storyboard-split.page.js` runs in the page). It reads the
model frame for the background, plate, words, bezel and screen scale, measures where the art and the words are, and
saves like the page does (the board's revision, a backup first: `backups/`, reason *manual*). Running it again leaves
frames already made as they are, so it is also how a **new or recaptured frame** gets the design:

```
node scripts/storyboard-split.mjs                              # both boards, every line but Scratch
node scripts/storyboard-split.mjs --video improved-quality-v2 --lines alerts,links
node scripts/storyboard-split.mjs --dry                        # what it would do, nothing saved
```

Done on 2026-10-07: 127 frames on Improved Quality and 68 on Increased Efficiency (the backups are the
`--manual` ones of that day). It needed one fix in `applyEdits` (`components/storyboardDom.js`, `stack`): a layer that
sits inside a demo's camera wrapper, or is a plain block (not positioned) with a transform on it, ignored its
place in the stacking, so a plate sent behind it still covered it; both now follow the stacking order.

## The other sections: a board each (the user, 2026-10-14: "work on all the other sections ... exactly like the Improved Quality videos")

The narration is `website Narration (October 14, 2026).docx` (project root; ~25 sections in four groups: Operations, Training, Risk, Knowledge). **One group at a time, Operations first** (the user's answer);
each section is finished and looked at before the next. A section is **data** and gets **a board of its own** (`/storyboard?video=<id>-v2`) with the same four lines of videos the Improved Quality board has:
**Videos** (one video per chunk of the narration), **Videos in detail** (the same videos, steps added), **· cursor only**, **· highlight and cursor**.

- **The spec**, `lib/sections/<id>.mjs` (the model is `live-oversight.mjs`; the format is described at its top): `chapters` (the words of the narration as written, `/` ends a line; `art` the state the stage is in; `end`
  a state the art goes to while the words are said; `marks` the yellow outlines, `{col}` a column of the table or `{target}` a `data-iq-target`; `acts` what the cursor presses and the state it gives; `title: true` a
  words-only chapter), `chunks` (the videos: the chapters each plays), `details` (the steps of the detailed twins, short, "The manager clicks Reports."). Register it in `lib/sections/index.mjs`.
  The registry feeds the storyboard's board menu and CLI (`SECTION_BOARDS`), the cursor's stops (`SECTION_STOPS`, `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`), the click targets (`SECTION_TARGETS`, `lib/demoTargets.mjs`),
  the runs (`SECTION_RUNS`, `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`: `VIDEO_RUNS`, the detailed twins `DETAIL_RUNS`, a profile per chunk with `centerMarks` and `marksInside`) and the steps (`SECTION_DETAILS`, `lib/storyboardDetails.mjs`).
- **The art** is a stage component, `components/sections/<Name>Stage.jsx`, drawn from a state object, registered in `STAGES` of `components/sections/SectionStage.jsx` and shown by the dev-only route
  `/section-stage` (`window.__stage.show(stage, state)`). Live Oversight's reuses the product's own pieces (`OverviewScene`, `DataReport`, `UsageReport`) with data in `lib/liveOversightData.mjs`.
  **The Team Board's column header is always seen** (the user, 2026-10-19: "that header that should always be visible ... it can be filtered but always having a header for each column"): in `OverviewScene` it is
  its own table (`.iq-ow-head`) above the rows (`.iq-ow-rows`), because the capture films a scroll as a move of the scrolled element's first child, and a sticky header inside the rows' table left with
  them. Its look is the product's (the user's screenshot): `#1271b7`, the tree's lines, pills as wide as their time, the boosted row with its flag, Canceled, Triggered at ↓, Position past the edge. Anything the cursor
  presses or an outline goes around carries `data-iq-target="<name>"` (a table column: `th[data-iq-col]`). The report's window is the class `iq2-report` (with the page `iq2-report-page` inside it, panned by
  `translateX`): the video treats it as a **web device** (`REPORT`: as wide as the tablet's frame is seen), the same as the Improved Quality report.
- **The capture**: `node scripts/capture-sections.mjs [--site] [--section <id>] [--only ch,ch]` (the site running) opens the stage in a headless Chrome and makes every frame of every chapter (start, outline, empty box,
  words, hand, click, result), writes `public/storyboard/<board>.json` and, for frames the board has no edits for, the **split design's** bubble edits (`styles.bubble`, no tail, 300 wide). It warns "target is not in view"
  when an outline's or a click's target is outside the window (pan the chapter's `art` to it). **Every box is measured in stage px** (divided by the page's own scale: the page used to film the stage at 1700 wide, and
  the outlines and the cursor came out 6% off the software, even past the window's edge); a recapture keeps the edits. **The loop is `lib/sections/captureCore.mjs`** (`captureSection`, `extract`,
  `defaultLooks`), shared with the AI demo call, which draws the same frames in the visitor's page with the visitor's data (docs/mission-animation.md, "Storyboard videos"); `--out <file>` writes one section's
  board elsewhere and leaves the edits alone (to compare a capture with the one on disk). **Precision Planning and Requesting Maintenance draw from data** (`lib/sections/liveData.mjs`, a `data` prop whose
  default is the board's own: a capture without data is byte-identical to the board on disk; check it with `--out` after changing a stage).
- **The board's lines**: after the first capture, `node scripts/storyboard.mjs line-add "Videos" --first --video <board>` and `line-shelf <line> <video ids…>`, the same for the three more (ids `<chunk>`, `<chunk>-detail`,
  **Since 2026-10-20 one command does it:** `node scripts/storyboard.mjs section-lines --video <board>` makes the five lines (Videos, Videos in detail and its three variants) at the
  top, each holding its videos side by side; run again, it finds them by their titles and puts their videos back.
  `<chunk>-detail-cursor`, `<chunk>-detail-both`; `DETAIL_VARIANTS`). Check each video on its board before the next section (`qa-pen.mjs`-style frames, `scripts/storyboard-qa.mjs cuts|marks|play --video <board> --line "<part>"`).
- **The cursor that points** (cursor only / highlight and cursor): at the bottom right of the highlight, **never lower than `POINT.room` (92 px) above the picture's bottom** (a column of a table runs to the bottom of the
  window; the hand would be cut by the edge), and it **comes in with the software** (`intro`: faded in with the first slide, not before it). `lib/storyboardPointer.mjs`.
- **The spec's words for what happens in a chapter** (read by `scripts/capture-sections.mjs`, in the order the frames come): `art` the state it starts in; `marks` first (a chapter with no acts), or `marksFirst` (the
  outlines, then the cursor works), or `marksAfter` (on the state it ends in), or `marksLate` (the words first, then the outlines one after the other: the way to have two or more outlines in a scene, each
  is on screen for its own 1.5 s); `point: [targets]` (what the cursor of the pointing videos goes to when the slide has no outline: the first one in view; an invisible `Pointer hint` layer, `x.hint` in
  `pointBeats`); `before` states the art goes to before the cursor works (a quiz that pops); `acts` `{target, name, state, then: [{name, state}]}`
  (the cursor presses `target`, the art goes to `state`, and on to each `then` by itself: a mission that opens and then expands); `end` a state, or `[{name, state, marks: false}]` one after the other (`marks: false`:
  the outlines go when it comes); `view` what the frame's scene is called (`data-view`); `title: true` words only. A chunk (a video) may have `showStep` (the mission's list keeps the step under the cursor in
  view: `{scene, gap}`) and `pace` (`{scene: {extra: seconds}}`: the scene stays longer). Scenes that only show something (no outline, no cursor, no states) get the same beat as an outline's (3 s + 0.7 s held:
  `beats.plain`, `beatsOf`). A title's lines are short (a title wider than the picture is cut: break it with `/`).
- **The pieces** (`components/sections/parts.jsx`; each a `data-sec-part`, named as the video knows it): `TabletPart` (the board; `compact` for five cards in a column; names `card-<id>`, `card-<id>-exec|who|pill|title`,
  `menu`, `add`, `menu-<item>`), `DemandPanel` (the On-Demand list: `demand-<title>`, or `demand-<id>` for an item `[kind, title, id]`), `MissionPanel` (the mission by itself: checkpoints `yes-<id>`, `no-<id>`, `cp-<id>`, `field-<id>`, `add-photo|video-<id>`,
  a training with its text, player or quiz; `expand` 0 to 1 pops it as a card then opens it; `scrollTo` a name), `FlowPart` (cards on their own with the purple connectors: `<id>`, `<id>-exec|who|pill|ratings`),
  `Qr` (the look of a QR code, not a real one). The web report is the `iq2-report` window. **Keep the DOM the same in every state** and fold what is closed (`.iq2-reveal`, `gridTemplateRows: 0fr`): a state that
  changes the structure is crossfaded through a blank, one that only changes styles is morphed (rows fold open, lists scroll).
- **What is seen is centered, and only what is INSIDE a window moves** (the user, 2026-10-15: "always center what we are seeing, it's a phone then center to that view, if it's a dashboard then scroll right or
  left to center always what we highlight ... never show empty space or move tablet, tablet stay where it is, also use the tablet we used in improve quality"):
  - a **phone view** (`MissionPanel`, the three phones, the phone that scans, the QR cards) is centered on the plate, x 521 (the plate is 0 to 1042; `left` 341 for the mission at 1.5). A card that pops for it
    (the ticket of Exception Management) pops over its empty lower part; the phone does not move aside.
  - a **dashboard** (the `iq2-report` window) never moves: its page slides sideways and its list scrolls inside it, so that what is outlined (or pressed) is in the middle, **never past an edge** (the pan
    is held between 0 and the page's width less the window's, the scroll between 0 and its end; a thing wider or taller than the window starts at its edge). The spec says nothing: the capture puts
    `state.focus` (the data-iq-target names, or `col:<name>` for a table column) on the first outline, on what the cursor presses, and on each outline in turn (`focusOf`); a stage with a window calls
    `useCenter(ref, {focus, pan, scroller, scrollTo})` (`components/sections/parts.jsx`). A state with a `pan` of its own (a drawer that has to be seen) takes the centering off. The video moves from one
    centering to the next as a scroll.
  - **cards on their own** (`FlowPart`: a mission, its arrow, the ticket, the next mission) are one group, centered on the plate (x 521, y 450) by measuring (`sec-flow-inner`, `center`), again in every state: a
    card that pops in makes the group wider and the first card glides over to keep it centered (the user, 2026-10-16: "the missions etc. it should be centered"). A card that pops over a phone view
    (Exception Management's ticket) is not centered (`center={false}`): the phone is.
  - check: `centerqa.mjs`-style probing: at each slide's hold, the union of the cards / phones / dashboard boxes against x 521 (the tablet is 89 px right of it by design; the dashboard is at its fixed fit).
  - a **tablet** stays where it is (`tabletFit`), and is the Improved Quality one: a flat black frame, thinner (its screen 1.03), the column titles 15.67 px (`TABLET` in the capture, put on every frame).
  - a layer with nothing in it is not drawn (`FlowPart` returns null), and a flow layer is not named `Isolated missions` (the video lifts that one out of a board): an empty layer was glided into the card
    that came, from the size of the whole layer, as a huge card shrinking. A card's id is never the same as a target of the mission beside it (the video glides things with the same `data-iq-target`).
- **The alert ticket** (the user, 2026-10-16: "create this UI for ticket ... ticket board is just like team board but with a red title saying what department it is instead of the black title ... make sure all
  tickets are like this, all alert tickets"): `components/TicketCard.jsx` (+ `TicketCard.css`, imported by `Storyboard.jsx` too: the captured sections are plain HTML there): the title and the timer pill, a
  red-orange "A" badge with "N Response(s) Triggered This Ticket", a dashed rule, the response that raised it, a building icon with the location, a dashed rule, the date right-aligned (who answered it at the
  left, once claimed, with the execution timer before the pill). **The red-pink border with the soft glow is only for a ticket on its own, popping out of what raised it (`alert`: the cards of `FlowPart`); on a
  ticket board the card is plain, like the team board's (the user, 2026-10-16: "the red outline only shows up when the ticket is not in the ticket board, like on the normal background").** Used by the interactive board (any mission of `type: 'Ticket'`), the cards on
  their own (`FlowPart`, `kind: 'Ticket'`, with `response`, `location`) and the simulator's boards (`SimDevice` `TicketChip`). A **ticket board** is the team board with the department in red
  (`demoState.ticketBoard`, `TabletPart ticketBoard`, `.mi-department--ticket`: "Maintenance", "Operations"); its cards are tall (232 px at scale 1: two to a column), so `card()` centers a ticket on the same
  line as a mission card. In the sections: Maintenance's ticket, claimed and closed boards are the Maintenance ticket board (the other tickets on it are samples); the escalation alerts, Item Expired and
  the exception alerts are tickets with their own response lines.
- **Precision Planning is the product's Overview, Personal Board, Timeline** (the user, 2026-10-19: "change the planning board in storyboard to look exactly like the UI I sent"): `PlanningStage.jsx`
  draws the web app's own screen in the 1110 px window, nothing panned: the top bar (Overview outlined), the boards (Personal Board) and views (Timeline outlined), the toolbar (Bulk Action, Filter,
  Group by, Expand/Collapse, Export, Idle; 12 AM - 12 AM, 1D, the date), and the table Name / Positions / the 24 hours: a level-1 row (a location) on `#f0f9ff` with blank hours, a level-2 row (a
  team: its count and the green clipboard, the missions of each hour in orange, a small 0 where none) on `#f0f9ff` when open and white when closed, a mission row (number, name, ⋮, a position tag,
  a dark count or a gray small 0); the blue toggles (filled with a down chevron when open, outlined with a right one when closed) and the tree's gray lines. The rows are Units, then Teams, then
  Missions (the narration's), so the toolbar says Group by: Unit. The cursor's "number 1" is the count in the first hour the mission occurs (`badge-<id>`). Styles: `.sec-pl-*` in `sections.css`.
- **The quiz is the Improved Quality one** (the user, 2026-10-16: "make the quiz always the same as the one in improve quality"): a section's training with a quiz plays the same lesson screen the mission video
  has (`lib/storyboardLesson.mjs`: the notice (or the video) on top, the arrows bar with "Quiz", "1. Please select ALL of the correct statements.", the statements with their square boxes, the blue
  Submit & Close), the cursor ticking the first two statements and pressing Submit & Close, then back to the mission with the training Done. A chunk says `lesson: {scene, topic}`; a `topic`
  (`LESSON_TOPICS`: its title, a written notice, its three statements, where the mission sits on that board's stage) makes the screen a written lesson (no clip to play); no topic is the shelving video.
  The stage's own quiz (`.iq2-m-quiz` under the training tile) is no longer played in a video. Used by Dynamic Auditing ("A Quiz Can Be Required…", topic `exits`).
- **The cursor presses the right thing** (the user, 2026-10-16: "make sure that the cursor clicks on the right thing, sometimes it clicks in a weird position"): the cursor's fingertip goes to the middle of
  the element its stop names (`clickSel`, `placeHand`, `centerHand`), so (1) the name is on the thing itself: a card on its own (`FlowPart`) has its `data-iq-target` on its `.chip`, not on the 1080 px wide
  board it is drawn on (the click landed 400 px to the right of the card, on the dark side); (2) the old rule "the plus is never what the videos click, the burger menu is" is for the Improved Quality videos only:
  a stop that names `add` goes to the plus (the On-Demand list of Maintenance and Support went to the menu). Check with `clickqa.mjs`-style probing: seek each stop's end of move and compare the fingertip
  with the box of the target (`scripts/` scratch; the click moment itself is a slide later, with the screen already changed).
- **Titles are white on the dark** (the user, 2026-10-15: "the title and conclusion are black, they should always be white"): the capture puts the dark panel over the whole picture on a title frame and gives its
  `title` layer the Improved Quality look (Poppins, white, 700; 36 since the CEO's review of 2026-10-16, was 62: every board's title slides were set to 36 on 2026-10-19) when the frame has no edits yet; a title line is at most 37 letters (`sections.test.mjs`).
- **Outlines** (the user, 2026-10-15: "the highlight sometimes doesn't look really good"): a thing is outlined with room round it that grows as the thing gets small (`PAD` 8, 11, 15 px: the thick border never
  touches a pill or a name); `pad` / `padX` / `padY` on a mark set it; rows that sit one under the other get **`marksSequence`** (one outline at a time, going from one to the next, not stacked over each other)
  and a small `pad`; `node scripts/capture-sections.mjs --section <id> --shots <dir>` saves a picture of each outline over its art, to look at them (`scripts/` scratch `shotsheets.mjs` makes sheets).
- **Increased Efficiency** (the user, 2026-10-15: "go over increased efficiency, it's not good"): remade as a section like the others (`increased-efficiency-v3`, `lib/sections/increased-efficiency.mjs`,
  `components/sections/EfficiencyStage.jsx`: the three personal boards, the team board with schedules, points and timer colors, the faces, the personal code panel by the mission, claim and close, the
  execution times and names, the initiative), with the Oct 14 narration, its four lines of videos and the pointing cursor. The old board stays in the menu as "Increased Efficiency (previous)".
- **Done** (2026-10-15): Live Oversight, Dynamic Auditing, Requesting Maintenance, Requesting Support, Tracking Expirations, Replenishing Inventory, Exception Management, Reporting & AI, Precision Planning (with
- **Training, Risk, Knowledge (the user, 2026-10-20: "do not forget about the other videos in training/risk/knowledge"; not Streamlining Complexity):** 15 more boards, each
  registered in `lib/sections/index.mjs` with its own spec, stage (`components/sections/<Name>Stage.jsx`) and CSS (`components/sections/<id>.css`, loaded everywhere the art is drawn
  through `components/sections/sectionStyles.js`). A spec with `draft: true` is being built: the board menu leaves it out. A section's own quiz lessons are in its spec's `lessonTopics`
  (`SECTION_LESSONS`, read by `lib/storyboardLesson.mjs`). `scripts/capture-sections.mjs` takes a Chrome port of its own (`--port`, else random): several sections can be captured at once.
- **The board menu is grouped** (the user, 2026-10-20: "add the name of each section in the dropdown like Risk then indent and show subtitles"): `BOARD_GROUPS` in
  `lib/sections/index.mjs` (Operations, Training, Risk, Knowledge, in the narration's order), drawn as `<optgroup>`s by `menuOf` in `components/Storyboard.jsx`; the Trash after them.
- **The real product is the reference** (the user, 2026-10-20, admin.taskmaverick.com, the organization TM Training only, looking only): **"search by mission / person / ..." is a
  column's ⋮ menu, on every board and report**: View Options ▸ (Pin, Highlight, Autosize, Expand / Collapse All, Reset), then the column's filter (Name: Sort by + Search By Keyword;
  a person's column: People and Positions to pick from, the People list a search, the names with their boxes, Close), Hide Column, Apply (blue once there is something), Close.
  `OverviewScene` draws it (`ColumnMenu`, `frame.menu`, `frame.filter`; Live Oversight's state `colMenu`, `filter`). **History is the real one**: no Bulk Action, one date-range box
  at the right, its own columns (Name, Reference, Closed By, Opened At ↓, Open, Claimed, Duration, Closed At, Type, Position - Closed by), every run a row of its own, newest first,
  group rows with "-" in every column. Live Oversight's last video: Performance History (Overview, then History), By Mission (the Name column's menu: the keyword, Apply), By Person
  (the toolbar's Group by: Person since 2026-10-21, see "The CEO's review of 2026-10-20"; it was the Closed By column's menu), then By Checkpoint, Gallery View and the area's evolution in the report as before (the user: "Report's Gallery View";
  History has no checkpoint column).
  Improved Quality and Increased Efficiency, which were done before: the whole Operations group). Each has its four lines of videos. **Next, after the user has looked: Training, then Risk, then Knowledge.**
- **Photographs instead of drawings** (the user, 2026-10-27: "instead of vectors, generate images like the ones I sent, use openai api key. where you give our UI and improve the image"): what a
  stage DRAWS that is not the product (a room, a crowd, a map, a document) becomes a photograph made by `node scripts/generate-images.mjs [--only <job>] [--n 3] [--dry]` (`npm run gen:images`): an
  OpenAI image EDIT (`gpt-image-2.5-sunburst`, high) whose inputs are a layout guide (the drawing as it was, rebuilt in the script), our own UI (a captured piece of `public/storyboard/ui/`) and the
  user's style reference (`scripts/image-refs/`: the kitchen with the Team Board on the wall, the Operational Manual); the prompt and the inputs of each picture are a job in `JOBS`. The picture is
  made at the art's own proportions (what the model frames is what the board shows) with a mask that holds the device where the guide has it. A job with `place` keeps the live device on top (the
  real UI, sharp and animated): the device the model drew is measured (its black frame) and the stage's constant is written so that the live one covers it, so **the board is captured again after
  it** (`node scripts/capture-sections.mjs --section <id>`, the site running). `--n 3` makes variants (each prints the place it would give), `--use <picture.png>` takes one of them without a new
