  API call, `--dry` only writes the inputs; the key is `OPENAI_API_KEY` (environment, else `~/.claude/api-keys.env`), never printed. Done: **Meal & Rest Breaks's room** (`break-room`,
  `public/storyboard-art/break-room.jpg`, the `.sec-br-scene::before` in `meal-rest-breaks.css`, so the boards captured with the drawing show it too, their drawn pieces hidden; the wall tablet is
  smaller, WALL 0.33 → 0.22: "the tablet looks too big, should be smaller", the user, 2026-10-27).
  **Then every board (the user, 2026-10-27: "update things like operational manual to the image I sent without the background + any other UI that is not the software like the A Consultant May
  Generate Revenue From Mass Adoption to be a generated image, please do not generate multiple images for one thing, just a good one, on all the storyboard"): ONE call per picture, no
  variants.** Run with no job, the script only lists them (`--all` makes every picture again). What carries exact words or data stays live over its picture (the break room's tablet, the BI
  report on the monitor's screen, the certificate's name and dates); a transparent picture (`transparent`: the model leaves the background out) is trimmed to what is in it.
  - Standardizing Enterprise: the Operations Manual is the user's picture (the book, its light, six plaques), `operational-manual`: no API call, its backdrop taken out by `cutout` (the solid
    things kept whole, the light kept as a warm glow for the dark plate); `KnowledgeFlow` places it 590 × 394 at the left, the line starts at the plaques, the cards are at x 712.
  - Innovating Consulting: the crowd (`crowd`, one picture that fades in as the drawn people did) and the coin (`coin`, every coin); the BI report is on a monitor (`bi-monitor`: its screen is
    chroma green, found and blacked out by the script, which prints where it is; the report is laid out at the screen's proportions at its own height and scaled into it).
  - Content Adaptation: the two states keep their exact outlines, filled with land (`land`) under their color (white until the mission lands).
  - Automated Certification and Learning Efficiency: the certificate's paper (`certificate`: its border and its seal) under its live words (CSS only: boards captured before it show it too).
  - Risk & Liability: the HR training's cover (`hr-training`) instead of the drawn title card.
  - Requesting Support: the sign and the package are photographs with their QR codes and words printed on them (`qr-sign`, `package`; CSS only, boards captured before them show them too).
  A picture whose HTML changed (the manual, the crowd, the monitor, the states, the HR cover, the break room's tablet) is on a board only once that board is captured again.

## The CEO's review of 2026-10-16: point and leave, big boards, the heartbeat

Notes, quotes and transcript: `docs/meetings/2026-10-16-ceo-review.md`. All of it is in the video player (`components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`); the slides on the board are as they were, except
Precision Planning's page (1600 wide now) and the sections' outlines (they carry what they are round). **It overrides the 2026-10-01 rule "the hand is for buttons only".** "The CEO's videos" below
are every video without a cursor of its own (`profile.pointer`: the "cursor only", "highlight and cursor" and "cursor and pop-up" lines keep their own cursor).

- **The cursor goes from thing to thing, it never fades between two (the user, 2026-10-19, after this review: "don't fade out the cursor between two things, it should just move to the next
  place, pop, then so on, change all of them"; this wins over "point, then leave" and "click and go" below):** `keepHand` (after `pointMarks`): between two slides where the cursor works (it points,
  it presses) in one run of a video (no title between), it stays where it was while the next words are written (`rest`), then glides to the next thing, which pops; a slide that was to fade it
  (`handGone`, `clickOut`, the lesson's play) keeps it instead. It comes in at the first thing of a run (out of the words, or in place on a big board) and fades after the last one. The fade-out /
  fade-in between two places (the hop) is gone from every line, the pointer lines too.
- **But it leaves when the screen changes (the user, 2026-10-20, every video, every line: "the cursor should fade out and come from the left of the words if the scene UI changed,
  it should never stay put at something when screen changes even when something like a picture appears, only stay if it's same UI and continuous movement like the step by step yes yes
  no add video"):** `leaveOnNewScreen` (after `keepHand` / `pointBeats`, so in all the lines) compares the screen of every slide where the cursor would go on with the next one's
  (`uiOf`: `uiSkeleton` of each art layer, its tags and classes three levels down with states and texts left out, plus the overlays named wherever they are: the Knowledge Base, the
  photo / video viewer `iq-mv`, the side panel `iq-od`, a video player `iq2-m-player`, the lesson, the gallery's rows; a menu or dropdown is left out: what the cursor opens itself is
  the same screen). Another screen: the cursor stays where it was (where it pressed) and fades as the screen changes (`handGone`; a slide of its own, `~away`, when the next one had
  it going on), then comes in again from the LEFT of the words, at their middle (`enter`). Same screen (Yes, Yes, No, Add video, ticking a quiz, Menu then its item): it glides on.
  Checked: a card that opens its mission, the photo viewer, the side panel, a new page, the Knowledge Base, the video viewer leave; step by step, a menu, the next photo in the viewer
  glide. `storyboard-qa.mjs cuts` finds no jump it did not find before.
- **And it is only there to point or to click (the user, 2026-10-20, about Improved Quality, "prioritize this before anything": "the cursor not showing until I want to highlight or
  click on something, pop shouldn't be on top of cursor and cursor shouldn't be there if there is no continuous movement, clicks or highlight"):** `keepHand` keeps the cursor between two
  things only when they are `calm`: the same scene, the same screen (`uiOf`), no title and no film between (step by step, the outlines of one scene, a menu then its item). Between two
  scenes (the next words are written meanwhile) it fades after what it did and comes in again for the next thing. The lesson's play: it presses and goes while the clip plays, in every
  line (`lessonBeats(…, true)`). The pointer lines (`pointBeats`): a slide with nothing to point at (no outline, no hint, no click) has no cursor, and one left waiting is kept only when
  it is `calmAround` the things before and after it. A press that gives a slide with no cursor keeps it there to fade (`handGone`), and a slide that was only to fade it after a
  `~away` loses it (it does not come in again to fade). A cursor that fades out during a move is drawn over the NEW slide (a ghost copy in `B.stage`), never under its cards and pops;
  in every video the cursor is z 13 (`.sb-stage.sb-in-video .dh-hand`), over the shade, the outlines, the popped cards and the words. Check: the director's trace
  (`window.__sbScripts[title]`) has no `rest` cursor across scenes.
- **The words first, then the highlight and the click; a panel's cards one by one (the user, 2026-10-21, Improved Quality: "do not highlight or pop until the text finishes writing then
  highlight, clicks ... while missions showing up ... first mission then 2nd then 3rd ... one by one while everything is dimmed a bit and then text then highlight onboarding"):** a profile's
  `wordsFirst` (IQ `knowledge`): new words are never pushed on to the slide after a click; the slide that brings them is played twice, the words first (no cursor, no highlight), even when the
  cursor goes on from a click, and the detailed twins' own steps too (`~say`); the words then stay while what the click opened goes on (a video playing). `stagger` (IQ `knowledge`: the
  Knowledge Base): on the first slide where the panel is, its cards (`.iq-kb-card`) come in one by one over the dimmed board once the move is over (`staggerAt`, by the clock), and the pen
  waits for the last one (`penAt`). A cursor leaving (`~away`) never brings the next slide's highlight with it. The cursor's layer root is put over everything in a video (z 1000, important),
  not only `.dh-hand`: a demo's cursor comes in a wrapper the board stacks with the others.
- **Three cuts the section builders found, fixed in the player for every board (2026-10-21):** a click that swaps the whole screen for another (the states for the California
  mission): the cursor fades with the old screen (gone by 45 % of the move, not 60 %), so it is never left alone on an empty picture; a cursor that leaves on such a swap goes the same way
  (`swap-out`). A popped card of the pop-up line that leaves fades over half the move (`swap-out`, was `quick-out`), and the shade with it: in a quarter it went in a picture or two.
  A panel that opens all the way drops its clip (`clip-path: none`, `MissionPanel` in `components/sections/parts.jsx`): a morph cannot go to `none`, so it switched half way (the "pop
  then open" snapped); the morph now goes to the same clip opened all the way (`inset(0 … round r)`), whatever the board's captures say.
- **Point, then leave** (`pointMarks`, in `directorOf`; the leaving is now only after the last thing of a run, see above): a slide that brings in a yellow outline is played as the words (the slide without its new outlines), then for each new outline the hand on it
  (`POINTER_LAYER`, its fingertip at the outline's bottom right corner, a little inside, so the hand hangs out of it and covers none of its text: the user, 2026-10-19; kept above the words' band on a big board: `placePoint`) with the outline coming out of its fingertip as a growing circle (`revealMark`, a clip-path; the
  outline is whole, not faint, so the page never shows twice), then the hand fading where it is (`handGone`); the outlines stay. Two new outlines: the hand points at one, then the other (it fades
  out and in between: the hop). Timing `POINT` (0.6 s there, 0.45 s circle, 0.45 s on it, 0.5 s leaving, 1.2 s after). An outline is new when it is not the same one (same scene, same place within
  6 px) in the slide before: the captures call every outline `circle`. Not a slide where the cursor clicks (its outline is the click's). The beats of `profile.beats` (highlight first, then the words)
  are off in these videos: the words come first.
- **The yellow circle with a small pop** (`popMarks`, `spotsAt`): every outline is a card that holds a copy of the software under it, its yellow border and glow on the card, lifted 5 % (less near an
  edge or the words) and 4 px up as it comes in. Only the "cursor and pop-up" line dims the rest. A card that goes is copied over the next slide and fades there (`ghostOf`), so it never drops behind
  the new software. **A card never hides what it is round (2026-10-27):** a piece of the art is copied when its own box OR what paints in it (`paintedBox`) is under the outline: Increased
  Efficiency's "Mission cards" layer is a 1600 x 0 box its cards hang out of, so tested by its own box it was never copied and every card of Unique schedules, Reward points, Timers change color
  and Automatic priority was an empty white box over the very card, pill or points it was about. A list scrolled by the clock (`driveScroll`) is scrolled in the cards' copies too.
  `storyboard-qa.mjs marks` reports a card that shows no text or picture where the art under it does ("its card hides what it is round").
- **Click and go** (now only after the last press of a run, see above): `keepCursor` is off; a press after which the screen goes on by itself (a lesson plays, a quiz comes) makes the hand leave (`clickOut`) and come again for the next press; a
  section's act with `leave: true` too (Reporting & AI: the photo, then the × that closes it, then the video). A run of presses with nothing to look at in between (step by step, Yes, Yes, Yes)
  still glides. On a section board only the presses its spec names are presses (`known`): the page
  re-centered for the next target is not one.
- **Big boards: words at the bottom** (`boardLayout`): a slide whose software is the web window (`.iq2-report`: the overview, the reports, the planning, a dashboard) and that has the words' side shows
  the window's WHOLE page (the pan taken off, the window as wide as its page; what is drawn over it moves with it), across the picture (`BOARD`: edge to edge since 2026-10-21, see "The CEO's review of 2026-10-20" below; it was 24 px margins and 16 px from the top), the gray side as a band under it (132 px) with the words in its middle, centered, up to 1320 px wide. Outlines that know what they are round (`data-sb-target`,
  `data-sb-pad-x/-y`: `scripts/capture-sections.mjs` writes them) are put round it again (`remeasureMarks`), the others move with the page. On such a slide the hand appears where it goes (it fades
  in) instead of gliding out of the words (`slot.bottom`). Everything it changes is undone at the next layout (`undoBoard`).
- **The heartbeat** (`timerPlan`, `tickTimers`; the sections' boards only, `TICKING`): their slides are captured at states, so their timers are the same in every slide; in the video a running timer
  counts up one second a second from the first slide it runs in, across the slides (known by its mission and kind: the overview's row and column, a card's title and kind, the phone's mission by its
  title: the card and the phone are one clock), and stops at what it reached when the mission is closed. Which run is the board's rule (CLAUDE.md, the timer rules). A popped card ticks too.
- **Sizes:** the words in the videos are 26 (`WORDS.size`; the video text editor's shared look also says 26 since 2026-10-17), the titles 36 (`TITLE_SIZE`, `setTitleFont`).

## The CEO's review of 2026-10-20 and the user's answers (2026-10-21)

Notes and transcript: `docs/meetings/2026-10-20-ceo-review.md`.

- **The missions are outlined as rows, with their column's title.** On the overview, open, claimed and closed each have two outlines:
  - the rows of the missions in that state, from the mission's number to that state's column;
  - the column's title on top.

  A mark `{rows: '<state>', to: '<column>'}` is measured by `rowsRect` (`lib/sections/rows.mjs`, from each row's `data-ov-state`) in the page's
  centering and the video's big board, and the same way in the capture's `extract`. `{head: '<column>'}` is a column's title. Their names are
  `rows:<state>:<column>` and `head:<column>`.

  "Who performed each mission" and "The execution duration" stay columns. "Who Is Performing" was taken out (the same as who performed).

  A chapter's `focus` says what the page looks at when its outlines do not fit in the window together: Closed At's rows are 1400 px wide. The video
  shows the whole page anyway.
- **Big boards edge to edge.** `BOARD` is margin 0, top 0, gap 0. The window is as wide as the picture and as tall as everything over the 132 px
  band: cut if its page is taller, white under the page if it is shorter. It is square, with no shadow and no border, so the light gray plate is
  never seen. The full board of Live Oversight (`layout: 'under'`) is drawn the same way by its stage: 1600 × 768, the words at `UNDER` in the band.
- **A failed audit triggers missions (Reactive Training).** No Close. A Fail draws a purple line to a lesson's card. The audit then scrolls just far
  enough to show the next checkpoint (`scrollTo: '<name>@bottom'` in the shared `Panel`), and that Fail draws a line to another lesson.

  The audit is centered until the first lesson, then moves aside (scale 1.3). Each card is level with its checkpoint, and a checkpoint scrolled
  under the summary keeps its line from the window's top (`AuditView`, measured in whole px).
- **By Person is a Group by** (the CEO: "group by, I would say ... you can do it by person with group by"; the user, 2026-10-21). In Live Oversight's History video, the cursor presses the
  toolbar's "Group by: Unit" (`board-group-by`) and then Person (`board-group-Person`). The runs of the mission searched for are listed under the person who closed each one, with no second
  level (`historyByPerson`). The menu is the Usage report's own (`GroupMenu`, exported, its options named by `target`), with Unit, Mission, Team (the real Team Board's) and Person (the CEO's; the real product has it on the Personal Board). Its targets are not the
  report's `group-*`, so the video never glides one into the other. The stage's state: `boardGroup`, `boardGroupMenu`. By Mission stays the Name column's filter (the CEO's "uh-huh").

## Exporting the videos as MP4 files (the user, 2026-10-28: "add a way to export the videos in /storyboard")

**On the All videos page too, the Remotion films included (the user, 2026-11-11: "add a way to export videos from the storyboard videos"; written, NOT tested).** Every video of `/storyboard?video=all-videos` has an **Export MP4** button in its head, beside its status (`ExportAllButton` in components/StoryboardExport.jsx; development only, like every export). When the board plays a Remotion cut, the film itself is rendered: `POST /api/storyboard/export {video, cut: true}` reads the board's saved edits, takes the command of the cut its variation plays (`renderCommandOf`, lib/remotion/cuts.mjs: `cd "D:DocumentsSaasideos" && npx remotion render <composition> <file>`) and runs it there (`startCut`); the button shows Remotion's "Rendered n/total" as a percent, and the file of that project's `out/` is downloaded when it is done. The film's right-click menu has **Export as MP4** as well, over "Copy the render command". When the board plays its slides, the button exports every part of its video, one MP4 each, one after the other (`exportMany`; each is the export described below). One export at a time on the site; a click on the button while it works stops it. A Remotion render needs that project's `node_modules` (`npm install` there once).

**What it makes.** A silent MP4 (H.264, yuv420p, BT.709, `+faststart`) of a video exactly as the board plays it: 1920 x 1080 at 30 pictures a second by default (the video is drawn 1600 x 900, `SCREEN` in `lib/storyboardExport.mjs`, and
is taken at the file's size through the browser's device scale, never stretched). Every shelf video, every Animations cell and the main board's player are videos; a video of a minute takes a few minutes (about 15 pictures a second).

**How (deterministic, not a recording).** `scripts/storyboard-export.mjs` opens the board in a headless Chrome (the same way `scripts/storyboard-qa.mjs` does: a fresh profile, the software renderer, the page's writes to `/api/storyboard` blocked, so nothing
on the board is saved), makes the window 1600 x 900 (device scale = file width / 1600), moves the `.sb-world` so the video's `.sb-pl-screen` is at the window's corner and hides everything round it (the sidebar, the other videos, the controls: the siblings of
the screen's ancestors get `visibility:hidden`; the screen and its `.sb-pl-layers`, the editor's shapes, texts and pictures, stay). Then for each picture n it calls `window.__sbSeek[title](n / fps)` (dev only, the same as dragging the video's bar), waits for
two animation frames and for what is on the picture to load (an image, the lesson's clip being put on its second; at most 1.5 s each, only what is seen), takes `Page.captureScreenshot` (JPEG q95, or `--format png`) and writes it to ffmpeg's stdin
(`image2pipe`). The video's length is `window.__sbTotal[title]()` (the exact timeline total, set next to `__sbSeek` in `StoryboardPlayer`; the clock on the bar is rounded). Because every picture is a seek, the file does not stutter whatever the machine
does, and the same video gives the same file.

**Where the buttons are** (development only: the file needs the dev server, Chrome and ffmpeg): the video editor's top bar (**Export**, then the percent while it is made; a click while it is made stops it) and the sidebar with a video picked
(**Export** section UNDER **Apply to all**, the user, 2026-10-28; also under the Apply to all of an element picked in the video editor: size 1280 - 3840, pictures a second 24 / 30 / 60, **Export as MP4**). Both are `components/StoryboardExport.jsx`: they `POST /api/storyboard/export` `{video, title, width?, fps?}`, which spawns the script with `--json --exact` and
tracks it in memory (one export at a time; `GET ?id=` is its progress, `GET ?id=&download=1` the file as an attachment, `DELETE ?id=` stops it and kills Chrome and ffmpeg with it). What an export is doing is kept in the module, not in a button, so picking
something else does not lose it, and the browser downloads the file when it is done. The files are also kept in `exports/storyboard/<board>/<title as a file name>.mp4` (not in git).

**From the command line** (for several, or all, videos):

```
npm run export:storyboard -- --video improved-quality-v2 --list
npm run export:storyboard -- --video improved-quality-v2 --line "The tablet and its Knowledge Base"
npm run export:storyboard -- --video improved-quality-v2,increased-efficiency-v2 --width 1600 --fps 24
```

`--line` is a whole title or words in titles (`--exact`: a whole title only); without it, EVERY video of the board (an hour, for a board of forty). `--crf` (0 - 51, 18 is as good as the eye can tell), `--format png` (no loss before H.264, slower), `--out`,
`--site`. ffmpeg is looked for in `FFMPEG_PATH`, the PATH, Shotcut and the copy the Python package `imageio-ffmpeg` has (the first that has an H.264 encoder: libx264, libopenh264 or Windows' h264_mf); else `winget install Gyan.FFmpeg`. `CHROME_PATH` for a Chrome
or Edge in another place. The pure parts (options, size, which pictures, file names, the encoder, ffmpeg's arguments) are `lib/storyboardExport.mjs` with `lib/storyboardExport.test.mjs`.

**Not exported:** sound (the videos are silent), the lesson video's own track (the clip plays muted in them), the board's slides (frames; those are the board).

## The page (what the user has)

- **Sidebar** (right, like Figma's design panel, no hints): an asset → Position (align in its
  frame, or to the others picked with it; X, Y; rotation), Layout (W, H; a UI element and a
  picture keep their proportions), Appearance (opacity, corner radius), Fill, Stroke, Effects (drop
  shadow, layer blur, background blur: the + lists them; a chat box takes a layer blur, which blurs
  its words and its shape), Text (font, weight, size, line height, letter spacing, alignment,
  **color**), Asset (Put back, Apply to all in this row, Delete). W and H each have a **lock**. Position has
  **copy / paste position** (also Ctrl+Shift+C / Ctrl+Shift+V and the right-click menu): pasted on
  the picked assets, or, with frames picked, on the same asset in each of them. A frame → its line, Duplicate, Add frame after, Reset, Delete. A line →
  its name, Add frame, Add line below, Duplicate, Delete. Nothing → the board: Add line, Export,
  Import, Back up now, Backups. Number fields: Enter, arrows (Shift = 10), drag the label to scrub.
- **Scaling several at once** (the user, 2026-11-09: "multiselection should give me ability to scale up everything at once"; written, NOT tested): with several
  assets picked, one box is drawn round the ones in the frame pressed ("N picked · w × h"), each keeps its own outline, and the box's **corner handles scale
  them all** by one factor from the opposite corner, like Figma: their places spread out or close in, a UI element grows by its scale, a box (picture,
  drawing, rectangle, outline) by its size, a text box and a chat box WITH their words (font size; the line height, letter spacing, corners and outline they
  were given). Picked in several frames, each frame's ones scale from the same corner of their own box. The background (fixed) stays; a locked width or
  height keeps that one's size, it still moves. One step of Undo. `startGroupScale`, `groupRectOf` and the `group-scale` drag in components/Storyboard.jsx.
- **Picking** (the user, 2026-10-06): Shift+click adds an asset to the picked ones **in any frame**
  (they move, nudge, copy, cut, delete, align and take the sidebar's changes together); **a click again
  at the same place** (not a double-click) goes to the layer under, one by one, then round again;
  **a rectangle drawn on a frame's background** picks the assets it touches (Shift adds, in any frame;
  drawn from a full-frame backdrop that is not selected it also draws one, a click still selects it);
  **frames picked together are carried together** by their number bar (`moveFrame` takes several ids);
  **double-click on text** retypes it and the scale handles stay on, to change its size. Pure parts:
  `lib/storyboardPick.mjs`; the page's measuring: `layersAt`, `assetsIn` in `components/storyboardDom.js`.
- **Rectangle tool** (R, the toolbar; the user, 2026-10-07: "a rectangle tool where I can put it with any gradient"): drag on a frame (Shift: a square; a click: a 360 x 220 box there). The sidebar of the tool sets the Fill first (the same control as Background: Solid / Linear / Radial, colors, textures, all the metal presets) and the corner radius; afterwards it is an ordinary asset (handles stretch it freely, Fill / Stroke / Shadow / blur in the sidebar). `lib/storyboardShapes.mjs`; the box is `<div data-sb-box="rect">`, the fill is `styles[key].fill`.
- **Scale tool** (K, the toolbar; the user, 2026-10-07: "if I press K I should be able to scale it up with font not just boundaries"): like Figma's. K (or the toolbar button) turns it on, K / V / Esc turn it off. Picking, moving and retyping work as with Move; only a handle drag differs: a box or a chat box **scales with what is in it**, always keeping its proportions (Shift does nothing), the corner or side dragged under the pointer, the opposite side staying put. A chat box's **words scale with it** (`styles.size`, and `lineHeight` / `letterSpacing` / `strokeWidth` when the sidebar gave it one) while its box gets the matching size (`sizes`): the area inside its padding scales by one factor, so the words wrap exactly as they did; the padding, corners and outline its style gives it stay as they are. A box (a rectangle, picture, spotlight) is scaled by its handles keeping its proportions, and the corner radius and outline width it was given scale too. A UI element already scaled by its handles (CSS `scale`, fonts included) is the same as with Move. A locked width or height leaves it alone (no handles). One drag is one undo step. Without K a chat box's handles still resize only its box (its words keep their size). Pure maths in `lib/storyboardScale.mjs` (`contentFactor`, `scaledBox`, `scaledStyles`; tests beside it); the drag is `type: 'scale'` with `content` in `components/Storyboard.jsx`. From the command line the same result is `style <frame> <chat box> size=<px>` plus `size <frame> <chat box> <w> <h>`.
- **Stacking** (the user, 2026-10-07): **Ctrl+]** brings the picked assets forward one place, **Ctrl+[** sends them back one; with **Shift** to the front / the back (in every frame they are picked in). Stored per frame as `order` (the keys, the one at the back first; `layersOf` sorts by it, `applyEdits` gives each top-level asset its z-index by its place, a chat box's words just above its shape); things added later go on top. Reset clears it. `reorderKeys` / `moveLayers` in `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`.
- **Device color** (the user, 2026-10-07: "make all iPads' frame #535353 instead of black"): an asset with an iPad in it has a **Device color** section; style key `bezel` (a color). The iPad's frame is a photo (`public/tablet-frame.png`), so it is tinted by lightening it: `.tbl-tablet[data-sb-bezel]::after` in `Storyboard.css` (the color, masked by the photo's own shape, `mix-blend-mode: lighten`): black becomes the color, highlights stay. Device color is a **paint** (the sidebar has the same color / gradient / metal presets as Background): a plain color lifts the photo's black; a gradient (**Chrome mirror**, gold…) replaces it (`[data-sb-bezel="paint"]`, normal blend). A thinner frame = scale the iPad's screen up (`scale <frame> <asset>/<path to .tbl-screen> 1.07`: the cutout is 1099×766 in a 92 / 64 px frame, so 1.07 thins it ~40% evenly); done for `knowledge:5` on 2026-10-07 with Chrome mirror. The UI is always above the frame (`.tbl-screen` z-index 1 in `Storyboard.css`: a bigger screen covers the frame), and a gradient frame gets a **rim light** (a bright top-left edge, a dark bottom-right one, a soft glow on top: drop-shadows of the frame's own shape) so it reads as 3D. A gradient frame has its own outline (an SVG mask in `Storyboard.css`: the photo's body x 47-1235, y 22-874 of 1285 x 896 and the screen cutout 92, 64, 1099 x 766) with outer corners of **36 px** where the photo's are about 55 (the user, 2026-10-07: "a bit less round, to follow the norms"); change the radius there (the `A36 36` arcs). **iPad glass** (the first preset; the user sent a photo of a real iPad's top edge, 2026-10-07): near-black glass with soft diagonal streaks of light, and a paint option **Polished metal edge** (`edge: true`, `addRim` in `storyboardDom.js`): a thin bright silver line with a streak of light, and a dark hairline inside it, drawn round the frame's outline as an SVG added last inside the `.tbl-tablet`. CLI: `node scripts/storyboard.mjs device <frame|all> <color|preset id|none>` (every asset whose HTML has an iPad, one save; `--video` for the other board). Done on 2026-10-07 for every iPad of both boards (35 + 28) with `#535353`.
- **Professional faces** (the user, 2026-10-07: "different fonts, much better but professional"): **Playfair Display** and **Fraunces** (serifs), **DM Sans**, **Sora**, **Space Grotesk** and **Manrope** (sans) are in the Font menu too (ids `playfair fraunces dmsans sora spacegrotesk manrope`; `next/font` with `preload: false` in `app/layout.jsx`, so no other page fetches them). To add one: `layout.jsx`, `FONTS` (lib/storyboardEdits.mjs), `FONT_STACK` / `FONT_NAMES` / the match in `lookOf` (components/storyboardDom.js).
- **Fonts** (Text > Font): Inter, Montserrat, Poppins and two **handwritten** ones, **Caveat** and **Kalam** (the user, 2026-10-07: "a handwritten font for the chat"; registered in `app/layout.jsx` with `next/font`, listed in `FONTS` in `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`, stacks in `FONT_STACK`). A chat box takes it like any text; its shape is drawn again once the font has loaded (`whenFontsLoaded`).
- **Background and gradients** (the user, 2026-10-06: "change background color, with gradient … a metal plate like color as one of the presets"): with a frame picked the sidebar has **Background** (all picked frames at once): Solid, Linear or Radial gradient (colors with positions, angle, + Add color, a **Texture** menu: brushed metal, fine grain, cross-hatch, perforated, diamond plate) and the ready ones: **metals first** (Metal plate, brushed steel, aluminium, chrome, titanium, dark metal, gunmetal, diamond plate, perforated steel, carbon, gold, brass, copper, rose gold: each a gradient with a texture over it, `TEXTURES` in `lib/storyboardPaint.mjs`), then a few color gradients. **No plain-color presets** (the user, 2026-10-07; Solid is still a type to pick any color). Stored as `edits.frames[id].background` (a color string or `{kind, angle, stops:[{color, at}], brushed}`, `lib/storyboardPaint.mjs`); an asset's **Fill** takes the same (a chat box: plain colors only). `applyEdits` puts it on the stage, so the board, Alt+drag ghosts and the video all show it; in the video it is a layer of its own that cross-fades while the assets glide.
- **The video** (the user, 2026-10-06: "on the right side of the first line … a video where it changes
  position of its element if you change them in the storyboard, same for content … smooth, clean"): past the
  last frame of the first line the board has a player. **Since 2026-10-08 (the user: "animate the first part,
  The tablet and its Knowledge Base, and put it on its right") it is that part's video**: it sits at the right of the demo's
  first captured line (whichever lines of your own are above it: Fonts, Scratch) and plays only that line's
  frames; on Increased Efficiency that is its first line, The title. Before, it played **all the frames in board order** (lines
  one after the other), each drawn like the board draws it (the demo's HTML, then `applyEdits`), so any move,
  scale, retyped words, asset brought in or taken out, and Undo, show in it. Timing: a frame stays as long as the
  demo did before the next (`time`), between 0.6 and 2.6 s (1.2 s when it has no time); the move into it is
  at most 0.5 s, eased. Between two frames drawn alike, an asset in both **glides** from where it was to where
  it is (the old and the new look cross on the way), one only in the first fades out, one only in the second
  fades in; another scene just dissolves. Play / pause (the button, or a click on the picture), a bar to go
  anywhere (a mark where each line starts), the clock, the line and number of the frame it is on. While it
  plays that frame's number bar is green; picking a frame on the board (not while playing) puts the video on
  it. **Handwriting plays in it** (the user, 2026-10-08: "add the hand write animation to the video"): a frame whose chat box has a
  pen (`anims`) stays long enough for it (`timelineOf(frames, writeSecondsOf(edits))`: the move in, 0.15 s, the seconds, 0.8 s), its words are
  not there while the frame moves in, are written stroke by stroke by the video's clock (the bar can be dragged through the writing;
  `prepareWriting` in `components/storyboardHandwriting.js`, made ready while the frame before is held, so it does not stop the
  picture), and are plain words again after; a frame picked on the board shows them written. On the Knowledge Base line the pen is on
  `knowledge/words` (knowledge:5) and `knowledge/hand-3#2` (knowledge:11), where its two chat boxes first appear. **Text is editable inside the
  video**: a double-click on a text in the picture stops it on that frame (all there: moved in, written) and retypes it there, exactly like
  on the board (Ctrl+Enter or a click away keeps it, Esc drops it); the words are changed on the board too (that slide, and the other slides
  of the video that show the very same chat box: `retype` in `Storyboard.jsx`, one place for the board and the video; nothing else is linked), Undo undoes it. **The text's look, place and
  boundaries are editable in the video too (the video's text editor, below).** **Changes between two frames** (since 2026-10-08): an asset in both frames that looks the same only
  moves (the frame's chat-box mode, empty or words, is part of the look of its chat box only: it used to make the tablet and the plate
  cross each other and wash out); a picture that is in only one of the two is crossed with the other without a dip (the new one comes
  in over the first half, the old one leaves in the second); **words changed in the same place** (a title, a chat box) are swapped one
  after the other: the old ones leave in the first half of the move, the new ones come in the second, never both at once. Code: `components/StoryboardPlayer.jsx` (controls, clock), `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js` (two stages,
  Web Animations, paused and set by time), `lib/storyboardPlayer.mjs` (timeline, pairing, fades; tests in
  `lib/storyboardPlayer.test.mjs`). It is not on the Trash board.
- **Lines**: click a line's green label to pick it (Delete, Ctrl+D, Ctrl+C / V, Shift+2, Enter =
  rename), double-click to rename it there, right-click for its menu; `+ Add line` under the last
  line; an empty line has an "Add a frame" placeholder. **Drag a line's label up or down to move
  the whole line** (its frames go with it, the other lines slide to make room, Esc cancels;
  `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs` `moveLine`, kept as `lines.order` in the saved file).
- **What you see never depends on where you are** (the user, 2026-10-06, after a screenshot of a blank,
  half-drawn board when zooming out): a frame that was changed is drawn live as soon as it comes near
  the view, at any zoom, and then stays drawn (`drawnOnce` in `Storyboard.jsx`); only unchanged frames
  (their picture IS the capture) switch between picture and live. While the board moves it is one layer (`will-change: transform`) that keeps
  the picture it had when it started: zoomed far out from there it would have to cover the whole board
  at the old sharpness (gigabytes of graphics memory: the browser goes white), so after each factor 2 of
  zoom the layer is drawn again at the new zoom (`rasterZoom` in `Storyboard.jsx`). Frames coming
  into view are drawn a few per animation frame, nearest the middle first (`liveQueue`), so a fast
  zoom never freezes the board; the frame you are on is drawn at once. **While the board moves, nothing
  is drawn** (`liveQueue.hold`): the frames wait until it settles (130 ms), their pictures showing until then.
- **Speed (the user, 2026-10-22: "make the storyboard tool mooore clean and faster")**, measured in
  headless Chrome on Improved Quality (long tasks, frame times, CPU profiles): **an edit** (8 nudges of
  an asset) blocked the page 4.7 s (625 ms a nudge) → about 0.05 s; **the page at load** had 140 live
  frames and 29 700 elements → 16 and 4 900. What it was, so it is not brought back:
  - **Every edited frame was drawn at load**, on screen or not (with the split design: all of them),
    and worked on again at every edit. Now only those near the view (`drawnOnce`, above).
  - **Reading after writing.** `drawShape` measured a chat box after the one before had been drawn,
    and the stacking (`stackAll`) read a layer's style after the one before had been written: the page
    laid itself out again each time. Now all is read, then all is written (`drawShapes` for several
    stages at once).
  - **Each edit rebuilt every video's script** (`directorOf`). `StoryboardPlayer` now keeps it until one
    of ITS slides, their edits or the shared looks change (`made` ref); the slow string parsers
    (`capturedWords`, `uiSkeleton`, `offsetIn`) keep what they found (`keep` in `storyboardPlayerDom.js`).
  - **`document.fonts.ready` read after every frame drawn** (each read worked the styles out again), and
    each frame's chat boxes drawn again after it. Now one listener for the board (`fontRedraws`):
    when a font has come in, the drawn frames' boxes are drawn again once, in the next animation frame.
  - **What this browser keeps (the user, 2026-10-22: "cache everything so it can make it super fast for me, it can use my local
    storage")**: a board opens with the edits as this browser last had them, read from **IndexedDB** (`components/storyboardCache.js`,
    the store `boards`: `{rev, edits, pending}`; it was localStorage, 5 MB in all, too small for every board) while its frames are
    still being fetched (`openLocal`), and the file is only asked whether it changed since (`/api/storyboard?rev=`: a few bytes when
    it did not, else its edits, which are taken at once, merged with what was not saved yet: the file always wins over an older copy,
    and an older copy is never saved over it). `pending` is true from the moment a change is kept until the file has it: a copy
    with changes the file never got is saved again when the board opens. Without IndexedDB (a private window) the page reads the
    file as before. The board's frames (`/storyboard/<board>.json`) are fetched while the page's code loads (`preload` in
    `app/storyboard/page.jsx`). **A video's script is made when the video comes near the screen** (`armed`), not for all 41 videos
    when the page opens; until then its header and bar show the length and the line marks it had the last time
    (`storyboard:clock:<board>:<title>` in localStorage). **The frame a playing video is on** is marked on the frame
    (`data-sb-playing`), not in the board's state: the whole board was drawn again at every frame of a video. On Improved Quality
    (headless Chrome, dev server): a reload is settled in about 0.47 s (0.7 before), the first play stalls once 0.11 s (0.3).
    Not kept, because it would not help: the frames' finished HTML (what costs is the browser laying them out and painting them,
    which it does as much from kept HTML) and the pen's paths (made in a few ms since the pen was made faster).
  - What is left: zooming far out over a whole section board paints about a hundred full screens
    (200 to 300 ms of the browser's own painting, the same as before); the first play of a video makes
    its first slide (about 0.1 s). The measuring script is not in the repository (it ran from a scratch folder).
- **Zoom**: a wheel notch (Ctrl / ⌘) is 15% at most and glides, fractional notches too (Windows
  scaling sends 133.33…); notches add up; a pinch follows the fingers, never more than ~16% an
  event; Ctrl + / Ctrl − walk presets about a fifth apart (`lib/storyboardView.mjs`).
- **Snapping**: a dragged asset lines up with the frame's middle and edges and with the other
  assets' edges and middles, red guides showing where (Ctrl / ⌘ held: freely; `lib/storyboardSnap.mjs`).

## A video's speed (the user, 2026-10-29: "add a way to change speed from editor")

After "make the words show up faster" (Increased Efficiency: the pen at 42 letters a second, a scene's highlight + words in 1.8 s), the speed is a thing to change from the editor. A video picked
(on the board, or in the video editor) has a **Speed** section in the sidebar, under its Words:

- **Words** - the pen's letters a second (`cps`, 4 to 90). The titles and the scenes without a beat (claim, close...) are written at it.
- **Scene** - the seconds a highlighted scene takes, the highlight and the words together (`beats.seconds`, 0.6 to 8; the scene then stays its `hold` more). Only videos that have scenes with a beat show it.
  In those scenes the pen goes at the speed that has the words written by then, so this is their words' speed.
- The minus next to **Speed** goes back to the video's usual (its profile in `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`).

It is the VIDEO's own (`edits.speed[<video title>]` = `{cps, seconds, at}`, `setVideoSpeed` / `videoSpeedOf` in `lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`, put over the profile by `withSpeed` in `StoryboardPlayer`), it is kept
in the board's file, merged between copies (the newer wins) and is in Export / Import. The "Videos in detail" twins have their own slow pen (a caption has to be read); their Words slider changes it.
Not built / tested when written.

## All text at 30 letters a second (the user, 2026-10-29: "make all text speed 30 letters per second")

Written, **not tested**. `ANIM_CPS.default` (`lib/storyboardEdits.mjs`) is 30 (it was 22), and that is the pen of **every** video:
- **No profile has its own `cps` any more** (the 42 of Managers, the old Increased Efficiency, the sections' `FAST`, Live Oversight's full board are gone) and the detailed twins' `DETAIL_PEN` is the same 30 (it was 16; a caption still stays 1.3 s once it is written).
- **A video's pen never takes the speed a slide's chat box was given** (`anim` in `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`): the video's `cps` (the Speed section, `edits.speed`) else 30.
- **A scene with a beat** (highlight, then the words) **takes what the words need**: `beatsOf(kept, spec, cps)` makes the words' time `letters / cps` and the scene `fade + gap + that`, so the pen really goes at 30. The seconds a
  profile says (`beats.seconds`: 3 in Improved Quality's mission, 1.8 in the sections) are then only the usual of the **Scene** slider: once that slider is moved (`withSpeed` puts `beats.fixed`), those seconds are kept and the pen goes at the
  speed that fits, as it did before. A scene's hold after the words (`beats.hold`) is unchanged.
- The stored note under the Animations line's "Handwritten" video still says 22 letters a second (the line's text is made by `lib/storyboardAnimations.mjs`, now 30; the stored one changes when the line is made anew).
- Tests changed to match: `lib/storyboardEdits.test.mjs` (the default), `lib/storyboardVideos.test.mjs` (the twin's pen).

## All videos: one stitched video per board (the user, 2026-10-29)

Written; looked at in the browser (2026-10-29). `/storyboard?video=all-videos` ("All videos", the group "Videos" at the top of the board menu). **It is a board like the others** (the user: "the all video board should be like the
other boards, with figma style paning etc"): the same canvas (the wheel pans, Ctrl + wheel and a pinch zoom, Space + drag, the middle button or the Hand tool pan, the background pans too, Shift 1 / Shift 0, Ctrl + / −, the zoom % menu,
the view kept for a reload: `storyboard:view:all-videos`), a green label for each group (Operations, Training, Risk, Knowledge) at the left, **the size of a line's (1010 x 112), level with the bars**, and **all the group's videos in ONE line** at its right (no next line: the
user, "exactly like the other boards"), each placed as a video of a board is (`.sb-pl`: the purple bar with the pill, its name and length, 188 px under it the picture 1600 x 900, then the board's bar of controls). **No title inside the video's
picture**: far away it is an empty picture with the play button, and a panel at the right with the videos by group and their status (click one: the canvas goes to it). `lib/storyboardAllVideos.mjs` (the data and the
layout, `layoutAll`), `components/StoryboardAllVideos.jsx` + `.css` (`.sb-av-*`), `node scripts/storyboard.mjs all-videos` (read-only list).
- **References: anything put on the canvas stays where it was put** (the user, 2026-10-30: "if I put a video in /storyboard in any of the boards it should put it where I put it, even in a blank space, videos/images and anything, it's like a reference for me"; written, NOT tested yet). On every board and on this page: a file dropped or pasted (at the pointer) is an item on the canvas itself, in no frame and in no video, so no video plays it: `edits.refs` {at, items} (`lib/storyboardRefs.mjs`, `components/StoryboardRefs.jsx`; this page's are in `content/storyboard/all-videos.json`), the file in `public/storyboard/references/` (`/api/storyboard/reference`, streamed, up to 4 GB, not in git). A picture, a video or a sound with its own bar, anything else a card with its name (double-click: opens it). A drag moves it (Shift: one axis), a corner resizes it, a click plays it, Delete deletes it (the board's Undo brings it back; All videos has no Undo), right-click: Open in a new tab, Delete; Zoom to fit and a reload count them. A picture dropped ON the picture of a frame still goes in that frame, at that point (it is an asset of the slide); on a video, on a frame's notes or on a blank space it is a reference.
- **A reload keeps each video on its second** (the user, 2026-10-30; written, NOT tested yet): a video that was played or moved saves its part and that part's second when the page goes or it leaves the screen (`storyboard:all-videos:place:<board>`, `placeKeyOf`) and comes back held there, paused.
- **Which:** Improved Quality and every section board that is not `draft` (the previous Increased Efficiency is not in it), in the board menu's order (26 now).
- **Each video is the board's line "Videos"** (the videos on the left of that line, from `edits.lines`' shelf; NOT "Videos in detail" and its variants) played from the first video to the last as one video: one play button, one
  bar, one clock. **No numbers, no ticks, no "where am I"** (the user: "remove the numbering and those lines in the timeline, telling me where I am doesn't matter to me"). A board with no such line plays its runs in
  `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`' order (Improved Quality without the Animations).
- **It plays the board's own slides and edits** (each part is an unchanged `StoryboardPlayer` in `cell` mode for the real run), so a slide, a pen speed (`edits.speed`) or a layer fixed on the board shows in the stitched video, as in the detailed twins.
- **A video is made ready only when it is on screen and the zoom is 28% or more** (`LIVE_ZOOM`; 26 at once would be too much): far away it is its bar and an empty picture.
- **Status on top of each video:** a pill in its bar, "In progress" (amber) or "Final" (green). `FINAL_BOARDS` in `lib/storyboardAllVideos.mjs` lists the final ones (empty: all are in progress, Increased Efficiency too; add `'increased-efficiency-v3'` when its fixing is done).
- **A double-click on a video** retypes a text in the part that is on, the **right-click menu** opens that part in its own board's video editor, in a new tab (it writes `storyboard:editor:<board>` and the board reopens on it): "A video's right-click menu", below.
- **One video is selected at a time** (the user, 2026-10-30, the first step of working on the comments here: "first I should be able to select the video, when I play it, it should select it and show me selection in the title purple rectangle by a blue stroke, I can select video from title or from playing the video itself"; written, NOT tested yet): the selected video's purple title has a blue stroke (`.sb-av-cell.is-selected .sb-pl-head`, 12 world px of `--sb-blue`). A press on the video selects it: its title, its picture or its controls, so playing it selects it, and it does not stop another video that is playing. Picking a comment on it does too (`select` in `onPin`; a pin keeps its press to itself). Esc or a click on the empty canvas (not a drag of it) let it go, with any comment picked; selecting another video puts down a comment picked on the one before. The Hand tool, Space and the middle button pan over a video without selecting it. A reload keeps it (`SELECTED_KEY`).
- **The inspector of a selected video is all its comments** (the user, 2026-10-30: "selecting a video will show in inspector in the right all comments in the video"; written, NOT tested yet): the sidebar's list of all the videos gives way to the video's name, how many comments it has and how many are done, and one row per comment. A stitched video keeps its comments on its parts (`edits.comments[<the part's title>]`), the list joins them (`stitchedComments`): in the order the video plays them, numbered through the whole video. **The pins on this page say the same number** (`number` of `StoryboardPins`), so in the board's video editor, which numbers a part's pins by the order they were placed, a pin can say another number. A row: the pin's number (green when done), the words, the second of the whole video (`timing`, told by the video once it is ready; empty until then). A click on a row stops the video on that comment, brings the video on screen if it was not, and picks it: **its fields stay under the list** (`CommentPanel`; its At is the whole video's second: `offset`), so the next comment is one click away. A pin picked on the picture lights its row. A video with no comments says how to put one. **The selected video is always made ready**, wherever it is and however far the zoom (`live`), or its comments could not be read. Retyping a text puts its fields in the sidebar instead; letting the video go brings the list of all the videos back.
- **Pins on the picture can be picked, dragged and read** (the user, 2026-10-30: "comments should be easily selectable from video, I should be able to move their position by dragging, and on hover it should show the message"; written, NOT tested yet). **The cause:** on this page the pins were UNDER the video's click layer, so a hover or a press never reached them. `Storyboard.jsx` imports `StoryboardAllVideos.jsx` before `Storyboard.css`, so **`StoryboardAllVideos.css` loads FIRST and a rule of the same weight in `Storyboard.css` beats it**: `.sb-pl-pins { z-index:4 }` beat `.sb-av-pins { z-index:7 }`, and the click layer is 5. Now `.sb-av-cell .sb-av-pins`. A rule here that must win over `Storyboard.css` needs a heavier selector. Also: **a pin is the same size on screen at any zoom** (32 px, a wider invisible target round it; `--sb-inv` on the world, set once the view settles; it stops growing when far out), its tip on its point, so its message is readable (17 px) at the zoom the page is used at; **the message is no longer cut at the picture's edge** (`.sb-av-screen` has no `overflow`, the picture is clipped by its own `.sb-art`); **pressing a pin holds the video** where it is (`pickPin`), without moving it to the comment's second, so the picture does not change under the pointer. All of this is the All videos page only: on a board the pins were never under anything, and they still scale with the board.
- **A comment lets go when the video is pressed anywhere else** (the user, 2026-10-30: "when I select a comment and I click elsewhere in the video to play it, the comment should deselect itself"; written, NOT tested yet): `select` always puts the comment down, and every press on a video that is not on a pin comes through it: the picture (the click plays the video as well), the play button, the timeline, the title. So a press on a comment of another video needs no rule of its own. The video stays selected. The sidebar's rows are not on the video, so a click on one picks it as before; a right-click on a pin picks it (`onPinMenu`).
- **A pin opens into its message** (the user, 2026-10-30: "on hover over the comment show a beautiful animation like it expands smoothly to show the content of comment"; written, NOT tested yet; all pins, the boards' too: one component, `StoryboardPins`; the video editor draws its own). The message is no longer a box beside the pin: the pin IS the card. `.sb-pl-pin` is an anchor with no size on the point, `.sb-pl-pin-body` is what is seen, its sharp corner on the anchor, and it opens out of that tip: up and to the right, up and to the left when `.is-flip` (x past 60%: the pin is mirrored, its tip on its right). Hovered or picked, its width and height ease from 46 px to the size of the words in 0.36 s with a soft stop, the amber turns dark, the number stays as an amber (green when done) disc, the words fade and slide in just after; it closes in 0.22 s. The sizes are `max-content`, animated by `interpolate-size: allow-keywords` (Chrome 129+); a browser without it opens the card at once. The words are laid out at their final width from the start, clipped by the body, so they never re-wrap while it opens. A comment with no words does not open (`.has-text`). A picked comment stays open, with a blue border, and closes when it is put down.
- **A pin lasts for its scene** (the user, 2026-10-30: "the comment should last only for the scene where I commented, not the whole last half of video, just the scene"; written, NOT tested yet; on this page and on the boards, `showPins`). Before, a held video showed all the pins of its part, and a part can be half of the stitched video. Now a pin shows only while the video is on the scene of its second, from the start of that scene to its end, playing or held, and the picked one always. **A scene is a line of the board** (`sceneOf`, now exported by `lib/storyboardPlayer.mjs` and shared with the director: one caption and what it shows, e.g. "Assigned to individuals"; a duplicate's is the line it was copied from), not a slide: the slides of a line are its beats, about a second each. `sceneAt(frames, timeline, t)` -> `{from, to}`; a video's scenes come from its script (`scripts` in `Stitched`). A comment's At moved to another second moves it to that second's scene. **The video editor is as it was**: a pin there is on the picture from its second to `COMMENT_SHOW.after` (3 s) after, so its inspector keeps saying that (`byScene` of `CommentPanel` is off there).
- **To look at:** the join between two parts is a 0.45 s cross-fade, not the engine's glide (so "one continuous video" is not yet as smooth as one run; a part can open on the very picture the one before ends on: `joins`, see "From the missions to the tablet, no fade"); exporting the stitched video as one MP4 (each part exports with
  `npm run export:storyboard -- --video <board> --line "<part title>" --exact`; the files can be joined with ffmpeg concat); the editor cannot host the stitched video itself.

## Comments on a video (the user, 2026-10-29: "add a way to add comment to the video ... a point I can place ... deleted right clicking it ... or selecting and hitting delete on the right inspector")

Written, **not tested**. (Since 2026-10-30 the comments are on the board too, before going to the video editor: see "A video's right-click menu, and the comments on the board" below.) In the video editor (`components/StoryboardVideoEditor.jsx`): the **Comment** tool (**C**, or the bubble button after Text) and a click on the picture puts a **numbered pin** there; the right inspector then has the **Comment**
section (`components/StoryboardCommentPanel.jsx`): the words (saved as typed), Done, X, Y, the second (At), "Go to its second", and a red **Delete**. **Right-click** a pin (or its mark on the ruler) for Delete / Mark as done / Go to its second;
**Delete / Backspace** with a pin picked deletes it too. Dragging a pin moves it (one Undo step). A pin never moves the playhead and is never part of any video, export or clip (editor only).
- **Data:** `edits.comments[<video title>] = {items: [{id, x, y, at, text, done?, created}], updated}` (`lib/storyboardComments.mjs`; x, y in the 1600 x 900 stage px; `at` the second it was placed on; the newer list wins on a merge, as the layers do);
  in the board's own file, Undo / Redo, Export / Import. Not copied to the other boards.
- **Shown** from one picture before its second to 3 s after it (`COMMENT_SHOW`); the pin that is picked is always shown, faded.
- **A comment marked done is not shown** (the user, 2026-11-03: "when a comment is marked as done don't show it in the video unless it's not done"; written, NOT tested yet): its pin is on no video, on a board, on All videos or in the editor, picked or not, so it leaves as soon as it is marked (`showPins`, `isCommentShown`, `placePins`). The comment itself stays: All videos' list and the editor's ruler still have it, green, and a click there picks it, so **Done** can be unticked and the pin comes back. A board has no list of comments: go through one of those two. `vcomment-set … done=false` does the same.
- **For Claude:** `node scripts/storyboard.mjs vcomments [<video>] [--video <board>]` lists them; `vcomment-add <board> <video> <second> <x> <y> "<words>"`, `vcomment-set <board> <video> <n|id> [text="…"] [done=true|false] [at=…] [x=…] [y=…]`, `vcomment-rm <board> <video> <n|id>`.
- Tests written, not run: `node --test lib/storyboardComments.test.mjs`, `lib/storyboardAllVideos.test.mjs`.

## A video's right-click menu, and the comments on the board (the user, 2026-10-30)

"The comments should be in /storyboard before going to video editor. Double clicking should let me edit it without changing how it was, and without opening the video editor. The video editor should open
if I right click the video, and it should show multiple options like delete, duplicate, export, and take to video editor." **Written, not tested** (nothing was run: no build, no browser).
- **Double-click on a video on the board** = what it was before the editor (2026-10-14/21): a text is retyped in place (with the text editor's box and the sidebar's fields), a color part is picked in the
  sidebar's inspector. It no longer opens the video editor (`StoryboardPlayer`: `onDoubleClick={retype}`; there is no `onOpen` any more).
- **Right-click on a video** (`openMenu` in the player, `videoMenu` / `ctx.video` in `Storyboard.jsx`): the video stops and is picked (the sidebar is its inspector), then the menu:
  - **Open in video editor** (`at <second>`): the editor opens where the video is.
  - **Add a comment here**: a pin where you right-clicked (disabled off the picture), at the second the video is on; it is picked and the keyboard goes to its words in the sidebar.
  - **Duplicate**: a video of a line of videos (a *cell*) is copied whole, with its slides, as a video right after it (`duplicateVideo`); the video of some lines (a *run*, e.g. the one at the end of a
    row) is made of the lines it plays, so **its lines are copied** (each right after its own; the copies are lines of slides); a run standing on a line of videos (a shelf) is disabled: a video stands once there.
  - **Export as MP4** (development only, like the sidebar's Export): starts the usual export (1920 x 1080, 30 a second; the sidebar's Export section chooses others), "Stop the export" while it is made.
  - **Delete**: a cell: the video and all its slides go to the Trash (Undo takes them back); a run: it and its lines go to the Trash; a run on a shelf: it is **taken off that line** (`setLineShelf`),
    its lines are untouched (a detailed video, which stands only on a shelf, is then gone until Undo).
- **Comments on the board**: the player draws the comments of its video as numbered pins over its picture (`pins` prop: `{list, picked, pick, move, menu}`, `.sb-pl-pin`), in the picture's own 1600 x 900 px so they
  scale with the board. While the video is on a scene only that scene's pins show, playing or held (see "A pin lasts for its scene", in "All videos"), and the picked one. They are in a layer beside the screen, so no export or clip
  has them (the export keeps `.sb-pl-screen` and `.sb-pl-layers` only). A press **picks** a pin (the sidebar is the same `CommentPanel` as in the editor; its "Open in the video editor" opens the video at its
  second with the pin picked), a drag **moves** it (one Undo step), **Delete** deletes it, **Esc** puts it down, right-click: Open in video editor / Mark as done / Delete the comment. The data is the same
  `edits.comments[<video title>]` (`lib/storyboardComments.mjs`), so a comment placed on the board is in the editor and the other way round. `commentOps.addTo / changeIn / removeIn` take the video's title; the
  title-less `add / change / remove` are the editor's video, else the one whose pin is picked (`pinSel`).
- **The Comment tool (C)** (the user, 2026-10-30: "no comment tool in /storyboard"): a speech-bubble button in the bottom toolbar of every board and of the All videos page. With it on, a click on a video's
  picture (instead of playing it) puts a pin there, at the second the video is on, picks it (the keyboard goes to its words in the sidebar) and puts the tool down; Esc, C or V leave it sooner. `commentTool` and
  `pins.add` of `StoryboardPlayer`; the cursor is a crosshair (`.sb-root.is-commenting`).
- **The All videos page (`/storyboard?video=all-videos`)** has the same (it was the page the user was on when the first version only had it on the boards): `StoryboardAllVideos.jsx` keeps the edits of the boards it
  shows (`store`), shows a change at once and keeps it in the board's file 0.4 s later (`apply`, `changeBoard` from `Storyboard.jsx`; it returns `{rev, edits}`). **Double-click** = retype a text in the part that is on
  (`api.retype` of its player; its text editor's box is on the picture, its fields in the sidebar; the text, the look and the box are kept in that board). **Right-click** = Open in video editor (the board in a new tab, the
  editor reopened at the part and the second: `openInBoard`), Add a comment here, Export this part as MP4 (dev), Open its board. A video there has no Duplicate / Delete (a video is its board's own). **Pins** are over
  the click layer (`.sb-av-pins`), keep their comments in `edits.comments[<the part's title>]` of that board (so the board's video editor shows them), a press picks one (the sidebar's Comment panel), a drag moves it,
  Delete deletes it, right-click: Open in video editor / Mark as done / Delete the comment.
- Looked at in the browser on 2026-10-30 (the user lifted "no testing" for it): the right-click menus, a comment placed from the menu and with the tool, typed, moved, marked done, deleted, the double-click retype on
  a board and on the All videos page, the editor opening at a comment's second, the build. Not run: Export, Duplicate / Delete of a video at the end of a row, `scripts/storyboard-text-qa.mjs`.
- `scripts/storyboard-text-qa.mjs` opens the editor through this menu now (it used to double-click). Not run.

## Variations of a video (the user, 2026-11-03, on the All videos page: "make me an other variation of this video (variations should show up on the right side inspector with dropdown between comments/the variations)")

Written, **NOT tested** (nothing was played or built: the dev server was running, and a build would write into its `.next`). A board's videos can be played another way, picked in the inspector of the All videos page.
- **The data:** `edits.variations` = `{list: [{id, name, look}], active, at}` in the board's file (`lib/storyboardVariations.mjs`: `variationsOf`, `activeVariation`, `addVariation`, `setActiveVariation`, `renameVariation`, `removeVariation`, kept by `normalizeEdits` and `mergeEdits`, the newer copy winning). Variation 1 (`v1`) is the video as it is made and is not stored. `look` names one of `LOOKS`, what the variation changes in how the video plays: it is put in the video's profile (`withVariation` in `StoryboardPlayer`, over `profileOf` and `withSpeed`), so the one picked plays EVERYWHERE the board's videos play: the board, All videos, the video editor, an export. The variation is the board's, so it is the stitched video's and each of its parts'.
- **The inspector:** a selected video's sidebar on the All videos page has a dropdown, Comments | Variations, next to its name (`sideHead`, `variationsPanel` in `components/StoryboardAllVideos.jsx`, kept for this browser: `storyboard:all-videos:inspector`). Variations lists them (the one that plays is blue), a click plays one, × deletes one, "+ New variation: …" adds one with a look and plays it; kept in the board's file like a comment (`apply`). A comment picked on the video shows the Comments again.
- **The CLI:** `variations`, `variation-add <look> ["<name>"]`, `variation-use <id>`, `variation-name <id> "<name>"`, `variation-rm <id>`, with `--video <board>`.
- **Each variation has its own comments** (the user, the same day: "Yes, separate them"): Variation 1's are kept by the video's title, as they always were; another's under `"<title> [v2]"` (`commentKeyOf` in `lib/storyboardVariations.mjs`). `commentsOf`, `addVideoComment`, `updateVideoComment`, `removeVideoComment` (`lib/storyboardComments.mjs`) read and change the ones of the variation that plays, so the board's pins, the video editor, the All videos list (its header names the variation) and the CLI all follow it; switching the variation switches the list. Deleting a variation keeps its comments in the file, and its id is never given to a new one (`addVariation` skips an id that has comments). `vcomments` lists every list in the file, another variation's as `<video> [v2]`.
- **Increased Efficiency has Variation 2** (`v2`, look `middle`), and it is the one that plays.

**`middle`: the words in the middle first** (the user, the same day: "our second variation I want to show text in middle then go to the right, so middle with gray background, then go to the right and show art to the left with fade in"; asked, for **every new sentence**). Every slide that brings new words over the software is played in two: first the gray of the words' side covers the whole picture and the words are written in its middle; then the gray goes back to its side, the words glide with it to their place, and the software fades in at the left. Scenes that keep the same words go on as before (the claim and the close of Increased Efficiency say one sentence). A title, a slide with no words' side (the tablet alone in the middle), and a slide whose time is drawn by the clock (pops, a pulse, a code, a stagger, a landing, posted missions, a film) are left as they are.
- **The director** (`directorOf`, `profile.middle`): before each such slide a copy of it (`~middle`), which is the slide but for what is drawn over the software (outlines, a timeline) and the cursor: so the copy and the slide are the SAME in every other layer (same signature: the pen is not in it), and the move between them shows the copy's pieces (B's are hidden) with nothing animated by the engine. The copy has the pen, the slide none; the copy takes the slide's `intro` / `joined` (it opens a part now, and on the All videos page the cross brings the gray in). What the slide before went on to (a zoom, another screen, the old words going) happens in the move into the copy, under the gray coming in. The copy's move is `cover`, the slide's is `reveal`, both on Easy Ease at `influence` (`LOOKS.middle.profile`: 0.76 s, 0.76 s, 70%, Increased Efficiency's one speed); the slide keeps its own hold if it had one, at least `reveal + still`. A list scrolled by the time keeps its two slides (`scroll`).
- **The engine** (`middlePlan`, `middleAt`, `midSetUp`, `middleOwn`, `midCopies` in `components/storyboardPlayerDom.js`), by the clock: on the copy's slide a copy of the panel (`.sb-mid`, z 900, its look as the gray side has it) is stretched from where the panel is to the whole picture (its right edge staying), its opacity coming in over the move into the copy; the words are over it (z 901); the software is at 0 under it while the words are written; in the move on to the slide the box goes back to the panel's and the software's opacity to 1. All of it is given back after, before a move is got ready (`prepare` measures the slides as they are) and before a slide is laid out again.
- **On the same screen, a rectangle and no gray** (the user, the same day: "if the text shown like in same scene we don't have to go to gray background, just put it in art area with rectangle background on it and fade out and start action"): when the slide before shows the same screen (`uiOf`: the same structure of the software, so a zoom in or out on one tablet is the same screen; the first slide of a part when it is the picture the part before ends on, a `/before` slide), the copy is a `card`: the software stays as it is, the words are written in the middle of the software's side (from the picture's left edge to where the gray side starts), on a rounded rectangle in the gray side's paint (`.sb-mid--card`, `CARD`: 30 x 22 px round the words' box, corners of 22, a soft shadow; `paintOf`); the rectangle comes in with the move into the copy; **the gray side keeps the words before all that time** (the user, 2026-11-04: "it shouldn't remove the right side brother, when it fades out from center then fade in on the right"): a copy of them over the copy's slide (`keepBefore`, `midBefore`), made from the slide before's words in the move into the copy, or, after a jump on the timeline or for a part's first slide, from that slide laid out on the player's hidden slot (`wordsLaidOut`, the `meter` of `measure`; `before` of the plan: the slide before, or the board's slide before it, the part before's last: `slideBefore`). Always a copy of the real words as the video lays them out, so their box, place and look are the ones they have on their own slide, the text editor's box (`vtext`) too (the user, 2026-11-04, on "Each Person Uses A Unique Code…" wrapping wider at the start of What it shows: "this one changed it size why? it should always be same size, on edit and when transitioning"; the first version wrote the words before into the new slide's words box, which has the default width); in the move on to the slide the rectangle and the words on it fade out in its first half, and on the gray side the words before go and the new ones come in ('swap-out', 'swap-in'), then the slide goes on (its outlines, clicks, the queue). In Increased Efficiency the gray is for a new screen only: the phones, the team board, the timer's mission alone and the picture of the three people; every other sentence is a card (worked out from the screens, not played). In the move into a gray copy, the words before fade where they are, never gliding to the middle; into a card they stay (the words' case at the top of `prepare`'s loop).
- **The words are LAID OUT in the middle** (`centerWords`, the last step of `layoutSlot` on a copy; the user, on a picture of the first try with the words still at the right: "center the text bro"). The pen writes on a canvas of its own (components/storyboardHandwriting.js, z 9000) placed where the words are when it is made ready, and hides the words while it writes, so moving the words by the clock left the writing at the right. Laid out in the middle, the pen is made ready there, and the move on to the slide glides the words to their place as the engine glides any words that move (`slide`; on a zoomed tablet the copy of them over the new slide, `overPanel`, the same way).
- **Not done:** nothing was played. Things to look at first: the gray coming in over a zoom (Schedules, Reward Points, the closed missions), the words' copy on a zoomed tablet, the first slide of a part on the All videos page, and the avatars of the queue right after "Teams Are Constantly Guided" (their first step starts in the last 0.06 s of the reveal, on the slide that is hidden then).

**`focus`: one place for the words, Increased Efficiency's Variation 3, the one that plays now** (the user, 2026-11-04: "add another variation which make sense to you, keep same design overhaull, but better animations, better titles etc.. because it feels like I have to look left right center etc.. fix it please in the new variation"; written, NOT tested). The design as it is (the plate, the tablet, the gray side, Montserrat 26, the video's speed); what changes is where the eye has to go.
- **The words never leave the gray side**: no middle, no rectangle (no `~middle` copies: the look is `focus`, not `middle`).
- **Left-aligned** (`align`, `--sb-align` on the words of the gray side, `focusWords`): the lines start next to the software, not wherever a centered short line falls.
- **As high as what they are about** (`focusWords`, `focusYOf`, `info.focus`): the slides that say the same words one after the other in a scene are a group, and the first of them with yellow outlines is the one its words stand as high as (the outlines' middle, measured as the video lays that slide out: on the slide itself, or on the slot never shown, `meter`; kept per layout, `focusMemo`), `margin` 70 px inside the picture. So the words are already at the outlines' height when they are written, the outlines pop beside them, and the eye goes straight across. A scene with no outline keeps its words in the middle of the side; a big board's band is left as it is.
- **Word by word, not a pen** (`reveal` anims, `revealWords`, `wordSpans`): each word fades in and rises 0.35 em over 0.42 s, one after the other, the last one in when the pen would have been done (the scene's time is the same: `animSeconds` of the pen it replaces). The words are spans made once in the words (`data-sb-words`, `.sb-w`, inline-block, the spaces between them kept, so the lines wrap where they did).
- **Titles** (`profile.focus.title`, the title branch of `fadesAt`): the words rise one after the other, 0.11 s apart, 0.6 s each, 0.45 em, and the title pushes in by 2.5 % over its hold. A title that goes grows from the size it is (`scaleNow`; it went from 1, which is still the size every other video's title rests at).

- **Variation 5, look `aside`: the second words over the art (the user, 2026-11-04, Increased Efficiency: "add a fifth option where we will have the following: the secondary text wouldn't replace the text to the right, rather show up in middle of art (or in a place where users might be watching) fades out and keep right side same", "I said fifth because there is another session working on a 4th variation"; written, NOT played by Claude):** id `v5` (`variation-add aside "Variation 5" --id v5`: `addVariation` takes a wanted id when it is free, and keeps the list in the order of the numbers, so the 4th, added later, comes before it). Added, it plays (as a new variation always does): pick another in the All videos inspector to go back.
  - **First and second words:** the words a screen comes with are written on the gray side as always. A sentence that comes after them on the SAME screen (`uiOf`) while they are on is a second one: a `~middle` copy with `card` (the variation `middle`'s rectangle in the paint of the gray side, in the middle of the software's side: `centerWords`, `midSetUp`, `middleAt`) writes it on the rectangle, the rectangle and it fade out, and the gray side keeps the first words all the time: the slide itself and the ones after it that still say the second words are `held` (the first words carried onto them, no pen; in the move off the card the kept copy of the first words, `keepBefore`, gives way to them with no dip). First words, never a card: a `/before` slide (its words are the next screen's), words after the gray side was emptied (`clearFirst`), a section's closing words (`closingScenes`, made from `closing: true` in the spec by `lib/storyboardVideos.mjs`). The rectangle comes in over the copy's move: a slide with a longer move of its own keeps it (the schedules going, then the missions moving back, before "Reward Points…").
